| stub hiss (= the Graph LEAK descriptor's HISS witness class at the STUB anchor, AA-24; Audio adds no separate stub asset; Boarding's attack-ramp ask is the HISS start ramp), load breath layers (Suit), pickup and set-down, pod hush | Boarding *(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)* | `BS:333` | `wit`, `suit`, `foot`, `pod` | yes |
| the post-restore notification | Kernel, Save/Load | CR-8 | all | prime only |

**AA-9. Listener and positional model.**
*Rule.* The listener is the local player's avatar position, never the camera, its lead or its zoom. Positional attenuation and panning are functions of world-px distance and are identical at every step of the integer zoom ladder (k in [1, k_auto+2]) and at every camera lead. Sources on a moving body stay attached to their ship-local anchors, composed each frame through `section_offset` and the body transform. Cue requests are pure functions of published state (AA-3). The node choice (a 2D listener inside the 640x360 SubViewport versus a manual pan and gain mix) is an engine note gated by a spike; the manual mix is the named fallback (-> ADR candidate; OQ-AA-1). The non-positional tell bed does not depend on the spike, which is a further reason the bed is contractual. 
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* PIPE (the camera has no audio role, integer ladder); `AT:989`, `AT:1278`; HIGH [UNV] SubViewport listener; reflexion pattern 3.

**AA-10. Transmission through geometry: it attenuates, it never culls a witness, and an unknown door is open.**
*Rule.* Airborne sources are attenuated by distance and by the closed doors and hull boundaries between the listener's room (`room_at`) and the source's room (D.9). Where the Graph publishes a door's true opening state the attenuation follows it continuously (a half-open door softens less than a shut one); **until that read exists an unknown door state is treated as open**, never closed (`opening(edge_id)`, Layout's ask, CX-A4). Transmission attenuates; it never removes a `wit` source with `sev >= 1` that is inside its class audible radius (a per-class floor, `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB`). Structure-borne sources (the positional hull-stress layer, the seam-cut TOOL loop, Horror's ingress and egress creak) ignore door penalties and take a reduced hull penalty; the tell bed is non-positional and exempt from all of it (AA-16) (engine note: no `Area2D` bus-override zones; see Engine feasibility notes).
*Why.* A guessed muffle that hides a real hiss is a suppression by default: it breaks P1 and the `LSG:1110` lint. The seam-cut loop is the far-side victim's tell (ASK AU1, `MSA:1071`). The constants and the formula are Formulas (D.9) and Tuning Knobs.
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* P1; `LSG:1110`; AUD-4; BS-4 (listening is a stance). *(orchestrator ruling: this reverses the brief's interim default "closed unless known", per ruling R10.)*

**AA-11. Mix priority tiers and ducking.**
*Rule.* Five tiers, Attention's M3 verbatim: **(1)** Graph consequence cues (witness and running) and the player's own footsteps; **(2)** tell beats and the breach alarm; **(3)** the positional hull-stress layer; **(4)** ambient and pre-tell cues; **(5)** music. This GDD places the families M3 does not name *without reordering it*: Suit body cues are tier 1 (consequence channel, `SE:1222`); the tell bed stems and Horror's strike and REACH-resolve cues are tier 2 (the strike cue is the witness of strike one, `HE:1285`); Horror's creep and ingress/egress creak, the `hal` leak and the P1 cue are tier 3; foley, interaction, assembly, body events and ambience are tier 4; UI and Voice sit outside the world mix. **Ducking:** a tier-4 or lower-importance bed (ambience, the TOOL channel loops **except the seam-cut TOOL loop, which is never ducked**, the pod leg bed, the berth bed) ducks under a tier-2 impulse cue (a beat, a strike, a breach pulse) by `DUCK_DB` for a short envelope. **The tell family, the `hal` leak, Horror's cues, the seam-cut TOOL loop (the far-side victim's tell, AA-33) and every tier-1 cue are never ducked**, and **no compressor, limiter or sidechain may attenuate a tier-1 cue**. Beats never duck under music (music is empty at MVP). The alarm family's duty is capped at `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` of each sweep (D.2). Hallucination cues never duck, delay or mask a true cue (AA-37). *(orchestrator ruling: ducking reaches tiers >= 4 only, the systems-designer's scope, not the audio-director's wider "beds including the hull-stress layer"; a duck on the positional hull-stress layer, a Horror creep or a lie's leak would mask a truth or leak channel, and M3's "beds" is read as the tier-4 beds.)*
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* M3, HE-37, SE-29, SN L-A.

**AA-12. Voice budget, reserved impulse voices, stealing order.**
*Rule.* A fixed pool of `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` voices (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 32, `[UNV]`). `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` of them (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 2; floor 1, which is M5's literal "one reserved voice") are reserved for tell and alarm one-shots (onset carrying beat 0, beats, breach entry, alarm pulses, release, subside) and are never lent to another family. The reserve is 2 because a beat's tail can outlast the next beat's start and the breach-entry event overlaps the last beat's tail: `ceil(BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS)` (D.10); M5's "one" is therefore read as a floor. The **tell bed is a persistent looping stream outside the pool (pool-exempt)**, so a full pool can never drop the bed (CX-A12); the **INTEGRITY room tone** is likewise one pool-exempt stream per listener, so a consequence-channel sound cannot be stolen (D.7, D.10). When no voice is free, a request steals from the playing voice of the highest tier number that is strictly lower in importance than the request, then the quietest at the listener, then the oldest; a request that finds no victim is dropped; tier-1 and tier-2 voices are never stolen by tier-3-to-5 requests; the tell family and `hal` voices are PROTECTED, and **the seam-cut TOOL loop is not stealable by tier >= 4 requests**. **A presented lie's SOUND leak is never stolen while the number of lies is within `LIES_MAX`** (Sanity's registered constant, cited by name): a lie that is visually presented but has lost its leak would be unfalsifiable by sound. Pre-tell creaks and foley drop first. A witness is never culled for voice count while any tier >= 2 voice is stealable; a tier-1 steal raises a DIAGNOSTIC counter whose non-zero value in a CI soak is a bug (the audibility cull must trigger before the count cull). *(orchestrator ruling: `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` stays 32 `[UNV]` as ruling R19 fixed it; the systems-designer's derivation (plausible worst-case demand 37, tiers 1 to 3 alone about 31) is carried to Formulas D.10 as the reason the range is 32 to 64 and to OQ-AA-9 as a recommendation of 48. It is not written as the value. `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` 2 is an addition to run ruling R19's list, a refinement of M5 confirmed at the 2026-10-26 checkpoint (OQ-AA-8 closed).)*
*Pillar.* P2, P4. *Decided by.* M3, M5; `LIES_MAX`; routed thread T6.

**AA-13. Audio first: by construction, not by latency.**
*Rule.* Every cue is requested on the same P5 notification that publishes its fact, and the visual is drawn from the same notification, so "audio no later than visual" (HR-9, AUD-2, AC-AT-24) is an **ordering guarantee**. Beat 0 is published in the `attention.step` run that enters TELL, and the presenter draws nothing for that epoch before it. `HE:1285`'s "strike_T to the positional strike cue starting within 1 tick (17 ms)" is the same ordering guarantee (the request is created in the handler of the notification that publishes `strike_T` and `play()` is called in the same frame); a measured latency figure (device buffer, the Windows audio stack) is a spike (OQ-AA-4), not a rule.
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-2; `HE:1285`; KRN CR-10.

#### B. The tell layer (`attn`): Pillar 2's audio spine

*Attention owns the phases, the schedule, the tiers, the abort and breach machine, the beacon and TellAudit.* The rules below say only what Audio does on each *observed* delta of `attention.view`. Beat times, tier thresholds, the abort rule and every Attention value are cited by name (`beat_schedule`, `ambient_cue_step`, `tell_window_contract`) and never restated.

**AA-14. The onset carries beat 0.**
*Rule.* On `phase` CALM to TELL (a new `tell_epoch` with `phase == TELL`) the onset cue plays on a reserved impulse voice. Beat 0 is published in the same run and is **carried by the onset asset's own attack**: one voice, one event, no second beat-0 sound. The onset is the only `attn` event with a long strain tail and it is unpitched. It begins no later than the first filament (AUD-2). The pre-tell creak recurrence stops at TELL entry (AA-22): no new creak starts, and one already in flight finishes rather than being cut, because a cut at onset would be an audible anomaly ahead of beat 0.
*Why.* Onset and beat 0 occur at the same tick; two sounds cost two voices and read as a double. TellAudit counts beat 0, so the onset counts as the audible beat 0 (five beats in the floor window at jitter 0).
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-1, AUD-2, AUD-3, AUD-5; AT-9.

**AA-15. Beats: one unpitched hull knock per published beat.**
*Rule.* For each `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` strictly greater than last-seen there is exactly one beat cue (never more than one per poll). The beat is a hull knock or creak, **unpitched**, drawn from a variant set by a presentation hash of `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` so no two consecutive beats are identical; its authored loudness does not change with tell progress or beat index (the shrinking interval of `beat_schedule` *is* the escalation: there is no rising pitch and no louder-as-closer). Every legal un-aborted `(tell_len, seed)` publishes at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats in the first `TELL_MIN_TICKS`, and Audio plays each of them: **no beat is suppressed before `breach_due_T`**, and Audio never drops a published beat for a pool or ducking reason because the impulse voices are reserved (AA-12). The audible count equals the published count only for frame times under about 500 ms (a hitch can coalesce beats, AA-4); the audible half of AC-AT-47 is therefore captured at nominal frame rate.
*Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* AUD-3, AUD-5, AUD-8; `beat_schedule` (F-12).

**AA-16. The structure-borne bed: non-positional, every living player, with a measured margin.**
*Rule.* While `phase == TELL` a low structure-borne bed plays for **every living player** on a pool-exempt stream. It is never attenuated by distance to the locus, by transmission (AA-10), by zoom, by camera, by the listener's room, by pod transit or by reduce-flashing or reduce-motion. It is at least `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` above the loudest concurrent non-tell bed in the worst allowed concurrent state (the running loops at their per-source ceiling and count cap, a TOOL-class channel loop, the ambience and Suit fan, the INTEGRITY room tone, and up to `WIT_VOICE_CAP` sustained witness classes counted through their in-band energy only, D.6), compared **per band**, for the low structure-borne band and for an upper-partial edge layer that keeps the bed audible on small speakers (D.6, AA-45). The margin is verified at tier 1 (the quietest bed state) by a listening test with a retained capture; it is not headless-testable (M2). Mono downmix keeps the bed. The bed contains no tone that moves with tell progress: it is textural and its only changes are the discrete tier layers (AA-18). The bed exists only while `phase == TELL`: it stops at TELL to BREACH (AA-20) and decays on abort (AA-19).
*Why.* AT-15 and AUD-4(b): "perceivable" is defined relative to every living player; the bed is what makes a tell at an unoccupied lit derelict fair. "Non-critical bed" in M2 is read conservatively as every concurrent bed that is not the tell family, including tier-1 running loops, which are bounded by a per-source ceiling and a count cap rather than a limiter because AA-11 forbids attenuating tier 1 (conflict CX-A11).
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-4(b), M2, AT-15, run ruling R24.

**AA-17. The positional hull-stress layer at the locus.**
*Rule.* A positional creak layer plays anchored at the hull anchor of `locus_section_id` (tier 3, structure-borne transmission, AA-10). The hull anchor is derived by Audio (D.16): the centroid of the lowest-id HULL_SEGMENT plate set of that section composed with `section_offset` `(provisional — Layout has no hull_anchor(section_id) read; ask routed, CX-A4)`. Attention publishes no coordinates and none are invented. The layer is additional evidence only: the tell contract is met by the bed and the beats without it. It is gated by `tier`: absent at tier 1, present at tiers 2 and 3 (AA-18). *(This is the one place where the tell is positional; it is the locus section the Attention contract already makes public, and its loudness steps only with `tier`, so it localises the section without becoming a continuous meter. Pre-tell creaks, in contrast, are never placed at the locus, AA-22.)*
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-4(a), `LY:262-266`.

**AA-18. Tier layering is discrete.**
*Rule.* `tier = 0` outside TELL, otherwise 1 to 3 (Attention's definition; `TIER2_TICKS` and `TIER3_TICKS` cited). Bed layers change only at tier steps, crossfaded over `TIER_XFADE_TICKS`: tier 1 is the base bed; tier 2 adds the mid layer and the positional hull-stress layer; tier 3 adds the upper layer. The increase in bed loudness per tier step is `TIER_STEP_DB`. **No audible parameter varies continuously with `tell_elapsed`.** The bed margin (AA-16) is verified at tier 1.
*Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* V5, AUD-8, AT-26.

**AA-19. The abort: the web loses interest (release without reward).**
*Rule.* On TELL to CALM with `tell_outcome == ABORTED` the beats stop and the bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS` (D.3), with a release cue whose spectral identity is distinct from subside's: an exhale or settling relaxation, with no sting, no reward chime and no "success" sound (relief without reward). The constraint is registered as one formula, `release_cue_relation`: `RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` **and** `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS` (Attention owns `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS`; equality is the requirement); a false relation at data load is a boot error. `TELL_REARM_TICKS` guarantees the release finishes long before any new onset. No beacon and no STRESS on an abort, so no breach cue. A restore into CALM with `tell_outcome == ABORTED` plays nothing (AA-5).
*Why.* The player must hear the web lose interest or restraint is never learned (AUD-6, P3).
*Pillar.* P3, P2. *Decided by.* AUD-6; `AT:1150`, `AT:1258`; reflexion pattern 2.

**AA-20. Breach entry and the alarm cadence (the alarm-versus-`alarm_eligible` ruling, written into the live clause).**
*Rule.* On TELL to BREACH (or CALM to BREACH after a hitch that skipped TELL): (a) the **breach-entry event** plays on a reserved impulse voice at the entry notification, at `breach_enter_T`, and **is pulse 0 of the alarm cadence**; its loudness step relative to the last tell beat is bounded by `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (D.5): the tell precedes it, so it must not become a jump scare; (b) later pulses follow **one per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`** (M1), each sounding for at most `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` of the sweep (D.2); (c) the pulse clock has two branches. **Aligned:** while `beacon_live` (`exists e in emitters() with e.kind == BEACON and e.body_id == the home-ship body and e.output_pm > 0`, EM-27) and the listener is on the home-ship's body, pulses start at the beam's sweep boundary, `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` (`beacon_aim` is a pure function of absolute `T`, so the beam returns to its authored phase exactly there, independent of `breach_enter_T`). **Fallback:** otherwise (listener on a derelict, home-ship `NOT_LIVE`, beacon absent or not yet commanded), pulses start at `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`. Reduce-flashing never changes either branch; (d) the branch is re-evaluated **only at a pulse boundary**, and a pulse is emitted only if at least `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS div 2`) have passed since the last alarm-family pulse, so a branch switch (the listener changes body mid-breach) or an aligned boundary landing soon after the entry event cannot double-sting inside a second, and the gap after a switch stays within `[ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS, 3 * ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1]` ticks (64 to 191 at the registered sweep); (e) this event and cadence belong to the `attn` family, a tell-contract event that is **never "the first warning"** because a tell of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS` precedes it by construction, so it is **exempt from the `alarm_eligible` gate** that governs the Graph's alarm-class annunciator (AA-27); the exemption is the conflict CX-A1 and its wording refresh is routed to the Graph owner; (f) a lie never uses either identity (L-A, AUD-7); (g) audio carries the breach **alone** on a derelict (AUD-10), since the derelicts have no beacon. The tell bed ends at BREACH entry; the entry event's own tail carries the transition.
*Why.* Gating the siren on a `sev >= 3` descriptor would leave a breach with no such descriptor yet silent and, on a derelict with no beacon, inaudible: a P2 violation. A breach with no siren fails reduce-flashing players and derelict listeners. *(orchestrator ruling: the two specialist drafts agreed on the aligned phase `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` and the fallback `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`; the audio-director's draft let the first aligned pulse follow the entry event by 1 to 128 ticks, the systems-designer's added the minimum-gap suppression. The minimum-gap rule is adopted because M1 says "one pulse per sweep", which an entry event plus an aligned pulse a few ticks later would break. The aligned form needs no `beacon_phase` read, so the systems-designer's gap GAP-E1 does not arise; "beacon live" is read from the Emitter's `emitters()` view, `(provisional — Emitter read of a live BEACON, CX-A15)`.)*
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AT-18, M1, M4, AUD-10, `LSG:976` (wording refresh owed, CX-A1); `beacon_aim` (EM-16).

**AA-21. Subside is not an all-clear.**
*Rule.* On BREACH to SUBSIDING the alarm cadence stops and **one short collapse** plays ("a short collapse, then silence", V10): no "danger over" cue, no relief sound. `phase == CALM` is **never** mapped to "all clear": no sound, music, ambience or mix change signals safety on entering CALM, and the Graph's failing-loop sounds (`wit`) carry the ongoing wrongness because STRESS can outlive SUBSIDING (`AT:1210`). Pre-tell creaks resume only by their own floor rule (AA-22). Audio reads `phase`, never `breach_phase`.
*Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* AUD-10, V10, `AT:1210`; AC-AT-67.

**AA-22. Pre-tell ambient creaks: stepped, floor-gated, in the listener's own section, never a meter.**
*Rule.* In `phase == CALM` only, ordinary hull-settling creaks (event ids distinct from tell beats, excluded from TellAudit and from the floor-window count) play at a density selected by the **quantised cue step** of Attention's `ambient_cue_step` (floor `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` 550, `CUE_STEPS` 4, `CUE_HYST_PM` 25, `CUE_MARGIN_PM` 100; cited, never restated). Step 0 (below the floor) plays none. Steps 1 to `CUE_STEPS` use a four-row table of mean spacing and minimum gap (`CREAK_MEAN_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 1800 / 1200 / 780 / 480 ticks, strictly decreasing, each step at least 1.3 times denser than the last; `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 600 / 480 / 360 / 240, each below its mean). **Recurrence is a stateless predicate of published state, never a countdown** (D.13): on each sim tick since the previous poll a creak is *eligible* when `phase == CALM`, the step is at least 1, `T - last_creak_T >= CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[step]` and the shared integer presentation hash of `(T, HASH_SEED, step)` modulo `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[step] - CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[step]` is zero; at most one creak starts per poll (no catch-up), and evaluating every elapsed tick keeps the density independent of the frame rate. The scan span is **primed to the current `T` on a restore and on a resume from pause** (no backlog scan) and is capped at `CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS` (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 60) ticks per poll (D.13), so a long hitch is a bounded loop, never a catch-up. The hash is the shared presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`, Attention, AT-28; the hash is shared with Lighting, LO-19), **never `rng_derive`** (a sim stream). **Position:** each creak is positional at a plate of **the listener's own section**, chosen by the hash, so a creak never points at the locus and is never louder, closer or pitched by step (a locus-anchored pre-tell creak would localise the future tell and make the step a distance meter by ear). A creak in flight at onset finishes. Pre-tell creaks stop at TELL entry and never play in BREACH or SUBSIDING. Four discrete densities, constant within a step: no continuous mapping of `level_pm` to any audible parameter, no pitched tone, no drone (AT-26). Creaks are a different timbre from tell beats (short, small, single-plate settle versus a deep structure-borne knock; lint L-10). `level_pm` appears in Audio only as the argument of `ambient_cue_step`. This rule is the pre-tell cue rule that replaces Attention's dangling "AUD-14" (CX-A5). *(orchestrator ruling: the systems-designer's stateless-hash form is adopted over the audio-director's per-creak sampled delay, because AUD-1 says "never from a timer in Audio" and a sampled delay is a countdown; the audio-director's means (1440 / 960 / 660 / 480) are superseded. The fallback, were AUD-1 read as forbidding even a stateless recurrence, is one creak per step entry plus one per CALM-minute window; OQ-AA-7 was resolved at the 2026-10-26 checkpoint in favour of the predicate. Creaks are deliberately not suppressed in the ticks before a possible onset: a suppression would itself predict the tell.)*
*Why.* AUD-8, AT-26, F-24: a four-step table is the most the guard rail allows, and it is floor-gated so the step-1 cue sits above the lamp's walk-beam reading.
*Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* AUD-1, AUD-8, AT-26, F-24 (`ambient_cue_step`).

**AA-23. No false calm, no phantom tell, no held breath, no music before beat 0.**
*Rule.* The Filter and Sanity can never filter, delay, mute, double or synthesise tell or breach audio (AUD-7; AT-17). No cue is suppressed during a real tell at any tier, stage or setting; no tell sound is ever played without a published tell. The "held breath" silence before breach is rejected (it would reduce evidence inside the floor window). A caption for tell beats is rejected (AA-41). Adaptive music must not telegraph the tell earlier than beat 0 (there is no music at MVP, AA-40).
*Pillar.* P2, P3, P4. *Decided by.* AUD-7, AUD-8, `AT:1223`, AT-17.

#### C. The witness and running layers (`wit`, `run`): consequence-channel true-state sound

**AA-24. The eight witness classes, played at the descriptor's anchor from the descriptor's true fields.**
*Rule.* For each live descriptor with `medium != NONE`, the Graph's `acoustic` class is played at the descriptor `anchor` (Layout anchor geometry composed with `section_offset`: the same source as the trail) with level and class parameter driven by the true `intensity_pm` (0 to 1000), and `dir` where the class has one. The class set is **exactly the LS-14 non-NONE ids: HISS 1, DRIP 2, HUM_DROP 3, CLANK 4, WIND 5, TICK 6, SIZZLE 7, CREAK 8 (a strained seal, SEAL_STRAIN).** **There is no WHISTLE class and no "loop whisper" class and none is invented** (CX-A2): "hiss, creak and whistle" in `LSG:44` and BS-4, and the routed phrase "hiss, hum, tick, whistle, creak, loop whisper", are read as prose for the enum, with WIND the only candidate for "whistle" and the running-loop sound (AA-26) for "loop whisper", pending the Graph owner's confirmation. The class parameter mapping is behaviour, not DSP (D.8): HISS level and pitch with flow; DRIP rate; HUM_DROP pitch sag with under-service; CLANK an onset one-shot at the descriptor's first step, with no recurrence; WIND level, stereo from `dir`; TICK rate (the flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK is the only cue at zoom k = 1, where the 2 px hairline does not resolve, `FT:1381`; **the flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK is driven by `sev` and `since_steps`, not `iota`**); SIZZLE level; CREAK rate and level. The request depends on the descriptor, **never on light**: a hiss in an unlit compartment, with zero lit emitters, plays at full true level (`FT:207`, E23: expected, not a bug). Audio may lead visuals (FT-35). The class stops when the descriptor stops, with no completion cue ("the reward is the absence", FT E7). The audio position equals the trail source position, both from Layout (FT AC-137). Continuous mapping from Graph **true values** is permitted and desirable (P1); the "no continuous mapping" ban is on `level_pm` only.
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* `LSG:163-168`, `LSG:976`, FT-35, `FT:843-870` (E1 to E15), run ruling R5.

**AA-25. Witnesses are never suppressed, never added; WORN is silent; an alarm class needs `alarm_eligible`.**
*Rule.* No hallucination, sanity stage, ducking, voice stealing, compressor, setting or geometry removes or alters a witness sound whose descriptor has `sev >= 1` and `intensity_pm >= 1` within its audible radius; no hallucination adds a hiss, creak or any `wit`-class sound. Two anti-suppression guarantees make that checkable: a **source-gain floor** for any `sev >= 1` descriptor with `intensity_pm >= 1` at zero distance (`G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB`) and a **post-attenuation audible floor** for a source inside its class radius (`WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB`, AA-10), related by one inequality that a data load must satisfy (D.8). A **flow-fraction** descriptor with `intensity_pm == 0` is silent: that is the **owner's own semantics** (`LSG:752`: a valve closed upstream leaves the LEAK descriptor and its `hp` but sets `intensity_pm` to 0, "the drip stops"), not a suppression, and the `LSG:1110` lint's wording is read with that carve-out. The carve-out does **not** extend to the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage or to FT-30's nine ids without an `intensity_pm` (SHORT, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT, SCRAM, FREEZE, ATMO_COLD/HOT/THIN/DECOMP): those have no `iota`, are driven by `sev` (D.8), and are never silent while `sev >= 1` and `medium != NONE`. The same holds for the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (`LSG:649`): its `intensity_pm` measures |dP|, not its thermal cause, so it is driven by `sev` and never silenced at `intensity_pm` 0. A **WORN** descriptor (`medium NONE`, `acoustic NONE`) is on the module-body channel and **no sound is ever played for it** (`LSG:1111`). The hum and the running "whisper" of a machine are true-state per `LSG:976` (AA-26) and carry the same no-remove, no-add rule. Alarm-class presentation in the Graph's sense (AA-27) plays only when `alarm_eligible = (sev >= ALARM_SEV_MIN) AND gate_open`, never as the first warning. Lint (`LSG:1110`): none suppresses a `medium != NONE` descriptor with `sev >= 1` from its acoustic channel; none plays an alarm class without `alarm_eligible`; Attention's breach event is exempt (AA-20). Alarm-grid snapping applies only to the alarm-class annunciator and the `attn` breach pulse, **never to a descriptor's witness class**, which starts on the poll that sees the descriptor.
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R5; `LSG:1110`, `LSG:1111`, `LSG:752`.

**AA-26. Running-loop sounds: one timbre per loop, read from `served_pm`; INTEGRITY is a room tone.**
*Rule.* One distinct running timbre each for O2, POWER, COOLANT and FUEL, driven by the true `served_pm` and node `state` of the published `node()` read (D.7), started and stopped by the `N/<id>` and `L/<loop>/<anchor>` dirty keys and polled at render rate for smooth values (`LSG:201`; the Graph hooks publish at divisor 6, so Audio slews over a short window). Level rises with `served_pm` between a per-source floor and a per-source ceiling; pitch sags with under-service. A node with `state` FAILED, DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT is silent; DEGRADED plays the running timbre at its true reduced `served_pm` while the descriptor's acoustic class (AA-24) plays the failing variant (HR-9: every loop has a distinct running sound and a distinct failing sound). **INTEGRITY is static** (`LSG:66`): its running sound is the room tone of a pressurised, intact hull (airborne, thinning with `compartment(cid).band_p` (NORMAL, LOW, DECOMP, VACUUM; LS-7; LS-19), woken by the `B/<cid>` key, and silent in VACUUM; **it must not be creaks**, because creaks are already spent on four other families (Attention's pre-tell settling and tell beats, the Graph's CREAK witness for SEAL_STRAIN, Horror's ingress/egress creak) and the identity table keeps all four apart (CX-A10 clarification). Concurrent audible running loops are limited by a **per-source level ceiling** and a **count cap** (`RUN_MAX_VOICES` nearest), not by a limiter, so their sum stays below the tell bed (CX-A11, AA-16). The hum of a running machine is true-state audio in the consequence channel under `LSG:976` but not in `LSG:44`'s enumerated list, and Boarding uses it as true-state corroboration, not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules (C-BS-4); **this GDD states the owner's reading (`LSG:976` wins over a consumer's wording) and Boarding has applied the refresh (`BS:1269` row 20; RF-6 closed); whether a machine's hum is a *witness of record* for the closed list in `LSG:168` remains the Graph's call (CX-A2)**. Audio uses `served_pm` from the published `node()` read; the `duty_pm` ask stays Emitter's (EM-14). Node lanterns hum while their machine runs; light itself is silent (`LO:429`). *(orchestrator ruling: the POWER hum's fundamental is at or above 160 Hz, the systems-designer's band plan, not the audio-director's "50-ish Hz" character, so that it does not sit in the tell bed's band and break the M2 margin; the transformer character is carried by harmonics.)*

| Loop | Running timbre (identity) | Failing class (descriptor acoustic) | Confusable pair and the distinguishing audio property |
|---|---|---|---|
| O2 | Soft air-handler whoosh with slow fan flutter; airy, broadband, mid-high | HISS, WIND (breach or leak), HUM_DROP (at the scrubber) | O2 vs COOLANT: O2 is airy and broadband; COOLANT is liquid and tonal |
| POWER | Transformer-like steady hum, tonal, fundamental at or above the reserved low-band edge | HUM_DROP, TICK, SIZZLE, CLANK | Amber vs FUEL: POWER is a continuous tonal hum; FUEL is a rhythmic mechanical feed |
| COOLANT | Pump with liquid circulation; burbling, a pitched pulse, a periodic cavitation tick | DRIP, HISS, CLANK | O2 vs COOLANT (above) |
| FUEL | Slow feed ratchet; low, irregular impulse train | HISS, DRIP, CLANK | FUEL vs Alarm: FUEL is dull, low, unpitched and irregular; alarm pulses are regular, on the sweep grid, and above the FUEL band |
| INTEGRITY | Room tone of a pressurised hull; wide, soft, no transients | CREAK (SEAL_STRAIN), TICK (flow-zero hull pre-stage) | Chemlight vs O2: the Chemlight glow is silent; its throw, land and wane belong to `suit` (AA-30) |

**Failing distinctness per loop (HR-9).** A loop's failing sound is the descriptor's LS-14 class played in the **loop variant** of its subject (O2 and INTEGRITY air, POWER electrical, COOLANT liquid, FUEL heavy or viscous). The loop is read from the descriptor's subject through `node(id)`/`edge(id)` and the descriptor's `medium`. The variant lives in the `<context>` slot of the `wit` asset name (`sfx_wit_<loop>_<class>_<variant>`), so no class id is invented and lint AA-6 (d) still checks only the eight class tokens. Together with the stalled or sagging running timbre (D.7), each of the five loops has a failing signature no other loop shares.

*The frequency-register separation is a recipe constraint for the sound-designer and the asset spec; the identity-table lint proves only tag disjointness. Blind two-clip identification at or above 90 per cent is the test for O2 vs COOLANT, FUEL vs Alarm and POWER hum vs the tell bed (AC-AA).*
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R6, HR-9, `LSG:66`, `LSG:199`, `LSG:201`; `ART:472-488`.

**AA-27. The Graph's alarm-class annunciator is a separate identity sharing the single alarm rhythm.**
*Rule.* When a descriptor is `alarm_eligible` (and only then) the Graph's alarm-class annunciator (token `ann`, bus Witness, an electromechanical klaxon timbre) pulses with the art bible's one alarm rhythm (`ART:845`): one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, so the ear, the warning mark (paired with shape and sound, HR-4) and the beacon agree. The annunciator pulses on the absolute grid `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`, except while `phase == BREACH` for a listener on the S3 FALLBACK branch, when it uses `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`; `last_alarm_T` and `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` are shared with the `attn` pulses (AA-20). `last_alarm_T` is shared across both identities and each pulse is capped at the same duty, so the union duty stays within `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM`; two pulses that start on the same tick are one rhythm with two stacked timbres, not two pulses. The annunciator's *first* pulse may wait for the next grid tick (at most `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` ticks); this is not a suppression, because the descriptor's own witness class and the Suit's body cues sound from the poll that sees it (OQ-AA-6). It never plays in a lie (L-A), and a lie never suppresses it. `(provisional — the grid sentence is a wording refresh owed to the Graph owner, CX-A1)`.
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* AT-18; `ART:845`; `LSG:976`.

#### D. The Horror layer (`hor`)

**AA-28. Four cue kinds, from published facts only.**
*Rule.* For `horror_t01`: (a) **floor-borne creep**, positional from `pos_px` while the entity is moving in LURK, STALK or RETREAT (HE V1, V2, V7; `HE:1193`); (b) **REACH resolve**: a positional, non-percussive lead-in from `locked_pt`, started `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` before `reach_end_T` (`REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO`, so the start is never before the REACH began, for every legal unfold of `UNFOLD_LO` to `UNFOLD_HI` ticks) and ending at `reach_end_T` with **no impact transient**. The impact is the strike cue (c), requested only on the notification that publishes `strike_T`. If `horror.view` leaves REACH without a `strike_T` (a fizzle to RETREAT, HE-15/HE-17, `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH`), the lead-in decays over `DECLICK_TICKS` with no impact: a pre-committed impact would be a true-family sound asserting a strike that never happened. The lead-in is scheduled against sim `T` (frozen by pause) and on a restore into it starts with a `seek` (AA-5, D.11); (c) **strike** at `strike_T`, positional at the seats and hull segments hit, requested on the same P5 notification that publishes `strike_T` (AA-13); (d) **hull creak** at an ingress or egress segment (structure-borne, `segment_at` anchor). **Marks and L3 dips are silent.** Mood: "patient, wet and structural ... weather with intent". The family is disjoint from `attn`, `wit` and `hal` and from the Graph loop sounds (AA-6; AC-HE-71). The `type_id` and `tell_id` enumeration is kept stable and enumerable for the Vertical Slice Knowledge Codex (HE-37). Phantom horrors (VS-A) use `hal` only and never a Graph witness class. Hidden real entities keep their sounds (`CON:243`, `SN:306`): a hallucination that hides a horror's body never silences its creep.
*Pillar.* P2, P1. *Decided by.* HE-37, `HE:374`, `HE:1193`, `HE:1285`; OQ 25 (`HE:1714`).

**AA-29. No proximity cue, no stinger; Horror never mutes, ducks or filters.**
*Rule.* There is **no non-diegetic proximity cue** for a horror: no sound whose loudness, pitch or rate encodes distance to or state of a horror (a distance-scaled stinger is a meter by ear). Every Horror sound is positional from `pos_px`, the segment or `locked_pt`, with ordinary physical falloff. No state stinger and no music cue. Horror never mutes, ducks or filters anything, and Audio never mutes or ducks a Horror cue at Horror's request. Horror pushes no noise (HE-36). Any conversion of Horror audio to a visual or tactile accessibility mapping is forbidden (`HE:1342-1360`, `HE:1714`); in particular no audio-to-visual mapping of any family may ever reveal a horror's position, state or direction (Horror OQ 25).
*Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided by.* `HE:1193`, `HE:1714`, HE-36.

#### E. Body, foley and event sounds

**AA-30. Suit body cues (consequence channel).**
*Rule.* Audio owns the sounds for the Suit's named cue states: breath cadence steps (normal, closed-reserve, thin-air, load), valve click on reserve engage and disengage, pack-creak load layer with a footfall-linked exhale, suit rattle (shiver while moving), pack cooling-fan whirr and tick (heat), seam hiss (leak), cell whine at `CELL_WARN_S`, equip clunk, top-up hiss, stagger gasp, glowstick throw, land and wane, and the gasp loop. Each derives from a true state the Suit owns and each starts **no later than its visual**. Load and thin-air breath are separable by cadence direction (a blind two-clip test at or above 90 per cent, AC-SE-42). They are tier 1; a hallucination may neither remove a real valve click nor add one (`SE:1222`). The Suit emits no noise to Attention (SE-31): these sounds are presentation of a true state, not a stimulus. The Chemlight (#86B896) glow is silent; its sounds are throw, land and wane only. Breath cadence derives from the atmosphere bands the Graph publishes (`compartment(cid).band_o2`: NORMAL, THIN, HYPOXIC, ANOXIC; LS-19) via the Suit; Audio redefines none of them.
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* `SE:474`, `SE:1220-1222`, SE-29, SE-31.

**AA-31. Footsteps: gait class only; the only noise readout.**
*Rule.* A footfall fires when the Controller's cycle position `floor(odometer / (STRIDE[gait] x 256))` (PCI D.11; the odometer is in 1/256 px) crosses a **foot-contact frame** (PC-11: the integer odometer counts pixels actually moved post-collision; `STRIDE_WALK` 10, `STRIDE_RUN` 15, `STRIDE_CARRY` 5 base px over an 8-frame cycle, cited by Controller rule and never restated as Audio values; the contact frames are an art/animation data list `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES`, `[PLACEHOLDER]` default frames 0 and 4 of the cycle, owned with the art-director). The cadence is therefore the animated foot's own: the Controller animates at 12 frames per second at every gait (`STRIDE_WALK` 10 at `V_WALK` 2, `STRIDE_RUN` 15 at `V_RUN` 3, `STRIDE_CARRY` 5 at `V_CARRY` 1 are each one frame per 5 ticks), so with contact frames 0 and 4 a footfall lands every 20 ticks walking, running and carrying alike, and a slowed avatar steps proportionally slower (D.14). **Gait changes the level and the timbre, never the rate**: the run is told by `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB` and the pack rattle. Walking into a wall adds 0 to the odometer, so it does not step; facing changes do not step; a player standing on a moving body does not step because the odometer counts moved pixels, not frame motion. Timbre is by gait class {WALK, RUN, CARRY}. RUN is `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB` louder (the pushed noise ratio `NOISE_RUN : NOISE_WALK`, 4 : 1, presented as a true state) and carries pack rattle. CARRY footfall is flat per tile and **never scales with carried mass** (`IDB:991`). A footstep is **never** a function of `level_pm`, locus distance, `phase`, Horror state or sanity. The player's own run is audible as the only noise meter (`PCI:135`, `PCI:821`). A slowed zone reduces speed and so footfall cadence proportionally; stagger, work, idle and down are time-stepped animation states (PC-11) in which no footfall fires, and the Suit's stagger gasp (AA-30) is that state's sound; facing changes do not step. Material-dependent footsteps are Vertical Slice. `NOISE_WALK`, `NOISE_RUN` and `NOISE_CARRY` are Controller values pushed to Attention (registry: `NOISE_PER_TICK`, walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1, source the Controller), cited by Controller rule (PC-10).
*Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* PC-10, PC-11, `PCI:821`, `IDB:991`, AT-26.

**AA-32. The interaction cue set.**
*Rule.* Cues are keyed on `channel_view` transitions and `result_seq`: press pre-play, `ia_start`, `ia_loop`, `ia_cancel`, `ia_abort`, `ia_done`, `ia_nothing`, `ia_no`, plus throw, landing, cut-complete, vent and door-motor. Edge-detected from `transition_seq` and `result_seq`; **only the terminal transition's cue plays**; "stopped" (`ia_cancel`), "interrupted" (`ia_abort`) and "nothing happened" (`ia_nothing`) are three different sounds; the TAP sound plays at the Applied result; there is no cue for a deferred rejection until the reveal (`PCI:211`); a restore plays no abort cue. A mute capture of cancel, abort, outcome and done yields four different sequences (AC-IA-100, visual redundancy). Interaction owns the keys' meaning; Audio names the sounds. The channelled-verb loop is lit-only (the lamp is on for it, `ia_loop` is not a light cue).
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* `IA:470`, `IA:977-979`, `PCI:211`.

**AA-33. Assembly, Body, pod and Boarding events.**
*Rule.* A weld or cut outcome plays only when `outcome_cue_gate` holds (D.12); the **bead settle is the single "it is done" for a weld**; the first weld has room in the mix (no escalation window in the bead settle, the first weep hiss and the bar disappearing, `MSA:1412`, OQ 29). The **seam-cut TOOL loop** is a positional, structure-borne sound audible on both sides of a closed seam (door penalties ignored, AA-10), because it is the far-side victim's tell (ASK AU1, MSA-18; the CASCADE cut's 10 to 30 s tell window, co-owned with Assembly, which owns its cue timing, `MSA:273`, `MSA:509`). `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION` and `BODY_TRANSFER` are wired at MVP with assets Vertical Slice; a hull thud is a sound, not an Attention stimulus (`BDY:849-851`). `POD_DEPARTED{t0,n}`, `POD_ARRIVED` and `BERTHED` drive the pod hops; clause (g) below gives the berth its own MVP bed; the pod leg bed (a VS asset) fades in and out over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS`, non-positional, leaving the tell bed unchanged (V13); `web.leg_timing()` is Web Map's confirmed name (CX-A19) and returns `{t0,n}` only while LEG (on a restore mid-leg without timing the bed starts at steady level). Boarding supplies an event list only (`BS:333`: "owns the names, mix and attack ramp for the stub hiss, the load breath layers (Suit), pickup and set-down, the pod hush, the structure-borne bed; Boarding supplies the event list only"; the structure-borne bed named there is Attention's tell bed (AA-16; AT-15, AUD-4), present from tell beat 0 to the release and decaying on abort, never a Boarding or CALM ambient, and in CALM only AT-26's floor-gated hull creaks play (AA-22); this GDD reads the stub hiss as the Graph LEAK descriptor's HISS at the STUB anchor, AA-24, with no separate stub asset, and an uncapped stub is a Graph LEAK, BS-8) and there is **no `listen` verb and no audibility boost** (BS-4, `BS:179`): listening is a stance, and what there is to hear is what AA-24 to AA-26 already play `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)`.
**(g) The berth bed (MVP).** While Web Map's published transit state is BERTHING (`transit_state`, WM-3 and WM-21; it is a field of `web.view`, `WM:297`, so Audio reads it there and adds no read: confirmed by Web Map, CX-A19), a sustained, non-positional `pod`-family **berth bed** plays (drive hum plus hull strain), for `berth` and for `unberth` alike. It fades in over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` from the edge into BERTHING and fades out over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` from the edge out of it (D.17), and it stops at `BERTHED` (or `UNBERTHED`), where the berth contact cue (or the single undock cue) plays. It reads **no progress datum**: no `t0`, no `n`, no elapsed or remaining ticks (the web read surface has none, `WM:300`), only the state edge; a restore into BERTHING starts the bed at steady level. Its hull-strain timbre tag is disjoint from every `attn` tag (lint L-10 extended) and it is not mistakable for the Tell: it is non-positional where creaks are positional, it carries no beat rate, and it is a continuous bed where the Tell is a rhythm. It is tier 4 and ducks under tier-2 impulses (D.18 victim set), so it never masks a beat. It is a witness of the crossing, never a stimulus (Audio pushes nothing, AA-3). The pod leg bed (VS) is a separate asset for LEG. This discharges AC-WM-117 (AC-AA-128).
*Pillar.* P2, P1. *Decided by.* `MSA:1092`, `MSA:1071`, `BDY:301`, `WM:103`, `WM:266`, `WM:295`, `BS:179`, `BS:333`.

**AA-34. No value, no reward, no combat, no death sound.**
*Rule.* A sound never encodes value or hidden condition (`IDB:991`): no loot-value chime, no condition tell, carry footfall flat. No hit, kill or reward stinger (not a combat game; tools repel). No sound attributes harm to a player. No sound for saving, no death screen, no death sound (`SL:23`). No sound at a sanity stage boundary, a debunk or a recovery.
*Pillar.* P1, P4, P5 (loss is silent). *Decided by.* `IDB:991`, `SL:23`, `SN:1259-1262`.

#### F. The hallucination family (`hal`), the human channel and music

**AA-35. The `hal` family, its MVP signature and its audio leak (the "violet of sound").**
*Rule.* `hal` ids are the Filter's `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` namespace, cited by reference (PF-20): MVP `FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE` (needle, readout, card, glyph) and `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE` (housing, machine); VS-A `FA_PHANTOM_MOVE` and `FA_PHANTOM_PRESENCE`. **At MVP there is one sound signature, the class-(b) reading-lie signature, realised under two Filter ids by surface type**; the two ids share the reserved signature and differ only in surface-appropriate content (a quiet sound under a needle versus under a lamp housing or machine), and the family is lint-disjoint from every other family (AA-6). *(Ruling R1 wrote "exactly one class"; PF-20 publishes two MVP ids; both are one class-(b) signature, and the wording tension is resolved at the 2026-10-26 checkpoint: CX-F2 and OQ-AA-10 are closed.)* Every `hal` sound is recognisable by a **reserved signature no other family may use**: (i) a detuned fringe partial in a reserved band; (ii) a slow amplitude breathing at the same period as the violet fringe (4 to 6 s per cycle, `ART:563`; cited, not restated); (iii) a tail that over-reaches its source. Mood "doubt, not horror" (`SN:1259-1262`): wrong, warm, fringed; no heartbeat, no tempo change, no sting; the class-(c) victim fringe is silent. The reserved band is an asset-spec and import-tag constraint enforced by the identity-table lint (AA-6 f, AA-45); this GDD states the **perceptual property and the test**, not a filter chain: blind two-clip identification of the lying instrument by sound alone at or above 90 per cent, **a near-field distinctness test** (the AC-SE-42 analogue; the tester stands already within the instrument's reading range, `HAL_RADIUS_PX` and AA-36, so it measures whether the signature is distinct, never whether a lie can be detected from afar) and, with hue removed and with audio removed in turn, a trained tester still identifies the lying instrument above chance (HR-5). The first lie carries all three leak modes (violet gleam, glide, sound); the Filter may drop only the SOUND mode, at stage 2 or 3, never on the first lie; GLEAM and the motion mode are mandatory on every lie (SN-15 L-L; PF-15), and a lie whose SOUND mode is dropped has **no audio request at all**. At MVP `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0, so every MVP lie has a sound.
*Why.* R-6 (`ART:962`): if audio can lie it needs a leak equivalent to violet. A pitch detune alone is confusable with a real groaning pump (P1); a glitch artefact reads as "audio glitch" (`ART:851`), the "scanline for the lie".
*Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R2; HR-5; SN-25; `SN:256`; PF-20.

**AA-36. `hal` transport and states `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)`.**
*Rule.* Audio never reads `sanity.perception` (SN-23, CR-12). It consumes only what the Filter supplies, per local viewer: the request `(viewer, ref or position, intent in {ON, LEVEL(pm), OFF}, class)` of PF-20 and, for a lying instrument, the `leak_modes` of the Filter's `perceived(ref, viewer)` pull (PF-8). **ON** is requested on the first ACTIVE frame, so the sound begins **no later than the glide** (`t_sound_on <= t_glide_visible`, SN:1256, PF-15: "the sound request is emitted on the first ACTIVE frame, not when the needle has visibly moved"); **LEVEL** follows `leak_amplitude_pm` (D.15); **OFF** arrives at CLEAR, and after an entry leaves `sanity.perception` the level follows the remaining discrepancy, then off, **presentation-identical for expired, debunked and invalidated lies with no verdict flash and no relief cue** (PF-12). The audible start of the leak is within `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` (Filter, 100 ms `[PLACEHOLDER]`, PF D.15) of the first ACTIVE frame. The measured device latency is part of spike AC-AA-6 and OQ-AA-4. LEVEL arrives quantised by `AUDIO_LEVEL_STEP_PM` (Filter, 20); Audio slews between received levels over `RUN_SLEW_TICKS` (the one slew window of the mix, D.7; no separate `hal` slew knob exists) and never extrapolates. Where the whole transport lies (a `PERCEPTION_OUT` request view versus the pull query) is the Filter's to fix; this GDD states only the fields it needs and renders neither a sound for a remote viewer (the Filter returns the honest record for any remote PlayerId, PF-10) nor a sound when the SOUND mode is not in force. A ref that resolves to a world surface is positional at that surface; a ref that resolves to a card or HUD reading is non-positional in the viewer's own space `(provisional — HUD and Instruments undesigned)`. Requests are `OWNER_PRIVATE` per seat. **Audible radius (`HAL_RADIUS_PX`, P4 verification economy).** The `hal` leak of a world-surface ref is audible only within `R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))` of the viewer's avatar, where `legibility_range_px(ref)` is the range at which the lying instrument's sprite is legible, so the leak can **never be heard from farther away than the instrument can be read**: spotting a lie by ear then costs the same approach (time and risk) as reading it, and checking is never free (P4: "checking costs time, risk or trust"). Beyond `R_hal` the `hal` voice is not requested from the pool (a cull, not a duck), fading in and out over `HAL_FADE_TICKS` at the boundary, with no sting. Only the `hal` voice is bounded: the instrument's true running or witness sound (AA-24, AA-26) is a different family, is untouched by this radius and keeps its own audible floor (AA-11, AA-25), so a lying gauge over a stalling pump still has the pump heard beyond `R_hal` (the falsifier, AA-37 e). A ref that resolves to a card or HUD reading is already non-positional and in the viewer's own space; it has no distance and `HAL_RADIUS_PX` does not apply to it. `legibility_range_px(ref)` is a read-only datum owned by the render and instrument layer `(provisional — In-World Instruments undesigned; until it lands, the data-load rule is R_hal = HAL_RADIUS_PX and HAL_RADIUS_PX is capped at the placeholder bound in Tuning Knobs §H)`. If the Filter refuses to emit audio requests, MVP class-(b) lies play **no** reading-lie sound and HR-5's sound mode is unmet. **Disposition (creative-director, CD-GDD-ALIGN):** the Filter must emit audio requests for every MVP class-(b) lie while `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0; a refusal reopens PF-20, not AA-35. Audio does not design around it: it keeps no reading-lie sound and the CONFLICT diagnostic (E-AA-51, AC-AA-60).
*Pillar.* P4. *Decided by.* run ruling R3; PF-20, PF-12, PF-15, PF-10; SN-23.

**AA-37. What a lie may never do to a true sound.**
*Rule.* A `hal` sound (a) never imitates a witness class, a running hum, a tell beat, an alarm or a Horror cue (disjoint ids, AA-6); (b) never removes, delays, ducks, masks or voice-steals a true cue (AA-11, AA-12); (c) never plays an alarm-class sound or the tell family (L-A); (d) never touches the human voice or the P1 cue; (e) is never placed as a lie about a real fault at a descriptor anchor in a way that replaces the true sound: **a real hiss still plays when the instrument falsely reads OK, which is the falsifier** (P4: "no lie is unfalsifiable"; at least one channel of truth remains). A `hal` bus has a maximum below the witness bus nominal by `HAL_BUS_REL_DB` so it cannot out-shout a true witness, and an asset-lint peak `HAL_PEAK_BELOW_BEAT_DB` under the loudest `attn` beat; its voice is exempt from tier ducking so a beat cannot mask the leak.
*Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R1, L-A (`SN:258`), SN-25, SN-27.

**AA-38. Sanity silence and no self-sign.**
*Rule.* Audio reads `sanity_view(player_id)` for exactly **one MVP-dormant purpose**: the P1 cue trigger (AA-39). **No mix, filter, EQ, ducking, loudness or ambience parameter is ever a function of the local player's own sanity stage or level.** No sound at a stage boundary, a debunk or a recovery; no heartbeat; no tempo change. The player must not learn their stage by ear (SN-14, `SN:244-247`). At every stage 0 to 3 the request list of every TRUE family is bit-identical for the same published sim state.
*Pillar.* P4. *Decided by.* SN-14, SN-27, `SN:1259-1262`.

**AA-39. The Voice bus and the P1 proximity cue.**
*Rule.* A `Voice` bus exists in the bus tree now (reserved, empty in solo MVP): no DSP from sanity, stage or Filter, never ducked by hallucination cues, never faked; the speaker indicator is HUD's. The **P1 proximity cue** (the class-(c) victim tell, VS-B; contract plus an identity-table row at MVP) triggers on the source crewmate's `sanity_view.tell_on` (not `harm_eligible`, so it leads eligibility by at least 113.4 s, `SN:296`); it is a physically plausible suit sound (regulator or breath irregularity, `P1_CUE_PERIOD_TICKS`) played at the crewmate's suit position **on every client except the source** (no self-sign, SN-14); **diegetic and positional, taking ordinary physical falloff like any suit sound** (the correction to the brief: what Horror forbids is a *non-diegetic* stinger whose loudness encodes distance to a horror, which this is not); never altered by any listener's sanity; outside the `hor` and `hal` families. Whether the source hears it is routed to the game-designer (`ART:909`; recommendation: no). In the solo MVP it is dormant.
*Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided by.* SN-11 (`SN:295`), SN-14, `ART:907-909`, HR-7, `IDX:41`.

**AA-40. Music and ambience.**
*Rule.* **No authored music at MVP**; the ship's sounds are the score. VS may evaluate a sparse non-melodic layer that is never keyed to `level_pm`, `phase`, `tier`, sanity stage, Horror state or the tell; adaptive scoring is Alpha. Ambience is a static room tone; the INTEGRITY room tone is `run` (AA-26), not `amb`; no ambience parameter is ever a function of `level_pm`, `phase` or sanity. A static drone is rejected at MVP (it costs headroom against the quantised pre-tell creaks). A running home-ship is never silent (the concept's "the ship must breathe", read as an instruction about audibility and the upkeep fantasy, not a timbre; CD-1 resolved by the creative-director at CD-GDD-ALIGN). A Vertical Slice Depth Ring may shift only the pre-tell creak density table, never the tell contract or the beat floor.
*Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* `CON:395`, `CON:479-480`, AUD-8.

#### G. Accessibility, settings, data, bands and lints

**AA-41. Captions policy (policy now, implementation Alpha).**
*Rule.* **Captionable, with precise source-naming only:** `wit`, `ann` and `run` sounds, Suit body cues, foley (including the player's own run, provisionally as own-action foley because it names the player's gait, not the web's regard, CX-A7), the three terminal interaction sounds, UI. **Never captioned:** tell onset, beats, bed and release; the breach event and alarm cadence; pre-tell creaks; Horror sounds; the `hal` family; the P1 cue. Consistency check: because `hal` ids are disjoint from every captionable family, an uncaptioned `hal` sound never sits beside a captioned twin, so the caption set is not a lie oracle. A deaf or hard-of-hearing player's tell channel stays **visual only**: the accepted cost of the standing user decision (no Attention indicator anywhere, `ART:954` item 15). R-6: because audio can lie, the leak (AA-35) is the answer; precise source-naming of the captioned sounds is the other branch.
*Pillar.* P3, P4. *Decided by.* R-6 (`ART:962`), `AT:1223`, `HE:1342-1360`, OQ-AT-1.

**AA-42. Tactile channel: none at MVP.** No rumble for the bed, the tell or Horror: any tell-synced rumble is an Attention indicator under the standing user decision. Routed as OQ-AA-2 to the user and the accessibility-specialist. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* `ART:954`, `AT:1331`.

**AA-43. Settings boundaries (rules now, UI Alpha).**
*Rule.* Per-group sliders exist for Music (`mus`), Ambience (`amb`), UI (`ui`) and Voice (`vox`). The **World group is every in-world family: `wit`, `ann`, `run`, `attn`, `hor`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `p1c`, `hal`, and shares one slider**. The Ambience slider covers `amb` only (a static room tone); the INTEGRITY room tone is `run`, so it sits on the World slider and no setting can strip a consequence-channel sound with the Ambience slider. So no setting can strip the `hal` SOUND leak or the tell independently of the witness sounds. No setting lowers the tell bed below its margin relative to other world sounds (the margin is enforced relatively, D.6). Mono downmix keeps the bed. Reduce-flashing and reduce-motion never reduce audio (M1; FT E20, E21). No setting displays or sonifies the Attention level, and none removes a leak mode (PF-15).
*Pillar.* P3, P4. *Decided by.* M1, M2, `ART:954` item 15; PF-15 ("never below two").

**AA-44. Data-driven assets and loudness.**
*Rule.* Every authored level, loudness (LU), radius, interval and class table lives in `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json` (and sibling data files), never in code (coding standard). The data path differs from Attention's reference `assets/data/attention/tell_audio.json` (`AT:1101`); the path refresh is owed to Attention (CX-A8). Asset spec (audio-director, after approval): OGG Vorbis, 48 kHz, mono for positional sources and stereo only for the bed and ambience; momentary-loudness metadata stored per asset for D.5; file-size budget and per-family loudness targets `[PLACEHOLDER]`.
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* run ruling R19, coding standards.

**AA-45. The band plan and masking constraint (asset-spec constraints, `[PLACEHOLDER]` values).**
*Rule.* Masking is band-local, so the identity table is paired with a band plan that the asset spec enforces by an import-time tag: (a) the tell bed occupies a low structure-borne band plus an upper-partial edge layer so it survives laptop speakers (`[PLACEHOLDER]` about 30 to 120 Hz and 200 to 400 Hz); (b) the tell beat and onset carry a transient readable on a small speaker, so the beats carry the evidence when the bed is lost (AUD-5: each channel sufficient alone); (c) the POWER running hum's fundamental sits at or above `[PLACEHOLDER]` 160 Hz, outside the bed band (AA-26); (d) the Graph's HISS, WIND and SIZZLE are broadband noise well above the bed; TICK, CLANK and CREAK are mid-band impulses; (e) the `hal` fringe partial occupies a narrow reserved mid band (`[PLACEHOLDER]` about 1200 to 1800 Hz) that **no other family may occupy** (AA-6 f); (f) Horror's creep and strike overlap the bed band by design and are accepted because they are positional and shorter, and the bed holds its margin against them as a non-tell sound. Two families may share a primary band only if their temporal envelopes differ (impulse versus sustained). The lint stores each asset's spectral centroid and 80-per-cent-energy band and enforces `family -> allowed band`; **MVP default: the tag-only lint plus the blind tests (AC-AA-28, AC-AA-58); the spectral-centroid and band lint is a Vertical Slice upgrade** (CD-GDD-ALIGN, OQ-AA-5), adopted when the spike proves it feasible and not before.
*Pillar.* P2, P4. *Decided by.* M2, M6, run ruling R2; the systems-designer's band-plan risk (small speakers, bed band overlap).

**AA-46. Per-listener scope and co-op addressing `(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)`.**
*Rule.* Every request carries a `listener_scope`: the tell bed is addressed to **ALL_LIVING** players (AT-15) and is the same at every client; positional layers are per listener; `hal` requests are `OWNER_PRIVATE` per seat; the P1 cue is ALL_EXCEPT_SOURCE; true sounds are identical on every client so "do you hear that too?" is a valid cross-check. A dead listener (VS-B) is addressed no bed. In the solo MVP there is one listener. Listener context (home-ship body, derelict, a pod in transit, a `NOT_LIVE` home-ship) changes only the alarm clock reference (AA-20), the door penalties (AA-10) and the pod leg bed (AA-33); the tell bed, the beats, the witness classes and the `hal` rules are identical in every context.
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* AT-15, SN-26.

**AA-47. Source lints (all build-breaking; each is a named acceptance criterion in part 2).**
*Rule.* L-1: no symbol `sanity.perception`, `tell_remaining`, `tell_progress_pm` or `breach_phase` in the audio source; `sanity_view` is read only by the P1-cue module. L-2: `level_pm` appears only as the argument of `ambient_cue_step`; no continuous audio parameter references it. L-3: only `hal` assets carry the `leak` tag; no other family's asset uses a `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` id. L-4: asset tag families are pairwise disjoint (M6) and the `hal` fringe band is occupied by `hal` only. L-5: no code path lets a `hal` request modify, delay, duck or remove a request of another family. L-6: `ann` assets are reachable only from a descriptor with `alarm_eligible`; `attn` breach-entry and pulse assets only from the `attention.view` BREACH path; both run on the shared alarm grid (`LSG:1110` plus CX-A1). L-7: no request is generated for a descriptor with `medium == NONE` (WORN). L-8: no compressor or sidechain targets a tier-1 bus (M3). L-9: `release_cue_relation` is true at data load. L-10: every pre-tell asset tag differs from every beat asset tag, and the berth bed's hull-strain timbre tag differs from every `attn` tag (beat, bed, tier stem, pre-tell, breach entry, pulse). L-11: the M4 and M2 proxies hold for the asset set (an offline computation). L-12: no symbol of `AudioServer`, `AudioStreamPlayer*` or a bus in Attention's source (AUD-9, owned by Attention). L-13: no request path silences a `medium != NONE`, `sev >= 1` descriptor whose mode has no Graph-defined `intensity_pm`, or a SEAL_STRAIN in its HEAT/TICK branch (`LSG:649`).
*Pillar.* P1, P2, P4. *Decided by.* M3, M6, `LSG:1110`, SN-23.

**Formula ids this section names** (written in Formulas, D.1 to D.18): D.1 cue dedupe and the per-poll classifier · D.2 alarm pulse phase, duty and minimum gap · D.3 release relation and bed release · D.4 restore bed fade · D.5 breach-entry loudness bound · D.6 bed audibility margin · D.7 running-timbre mapping · D.8 witness level and rate · D.9 room-graph transmission · D.10 voice allocation, reserve sizing and stealing order · D.11 REACH resolve scheduling · D.12 `outcome_cue_gate` usage · D.13 pre-tell creak predicate · D.14 footfall cadence and gait loudness · D.15 `hal` level and breathing · D.16 hull anchor derivation · D.17 pod leg bed envelope · D.18 ducking.

### States and Transitions

Audio owns **no sim state** and persists nothing. The machines below are *per-listener presentation memory*, rebuilt from published state after any restore; every transition is caused by a **change in a published fact** (never by a timer in Audio, AUD-1), and the "who may cause it" column names the owner of that fact. Audio itself causes no transition in any system.

#### S1. Cue kinds, and what each does at restore, pause and death

| Cue kind | Members | Trigger edge | On restore | On pause | On death stub | De-click / fade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sustained, steady | `run` loops, the INTEGRITY room tone, `wit` classes of live descriptors, `suit` breath loops, an in-progress seam-cut TOOL loop, the `p1c` loop (VS-B), the positional hull-stress layer | the state is present (`N/<id>` and `L/<loop>/<anchor>` keys for running loops; the descriptor set woken by `F/<id>` keys for `wit` classes; `B/<cid>` keys for the INTEGRITY room tone; `phase == TELL` with `tier >= 2`) | resume at steady level from published state | held with sim `T` | stop (nothing is published) | `DECLICK_TICKS` |
| Sustained, tell bed | the `attn` bed and its tier stems | `phase == TELL` | **fade in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`**; beats resume at the next published beat | held | stop | `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` |
| Sustained, lie | the `hal` SOUND leak | an active Filter request (ON or LEVEL) | resume with the lie at full leak level on the first frame | held | stop | `DECLICK_TICKS` only |
| Sustained, scheduled | the REACH resolve while `reach_end_T` is in the future | a published `reach_end_T` | start with `seek` if inside the lead-in; the lead-in ends at `reach_end_T`; no impact without `strike_T` | frozen (it counts sim `T`) | stop | `DECLICK_TICKS` |
| One-shot | onset, beat, release, breach entry, alarm pulse, subside, pre-tell creak, strike, ingress/egress creak, `ia_*` terminals, assembly outcome, `BODY_*`, `POD_*` | a strictly greater counter or a new event | **never replays**; the poll primes last-seen values | held | no sound | n/a |
| UI | `ui` | a UI event | n/a | continues | n/a | n/a |

#### S2. The `attn` cue machine, as consumed from `attention.view`

Attention owns the phases, timings and outcomes; this table lists only what Audio does on each *observed* delta. Audio reads `phase`, never `breach_phase` (AC-AT-67).

| From (observed `phase`) | To | Observed delta | Audio action | Voice | Who causes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CALM | CALM | quantised cue step change (`ambient_cue_step`) | the next creak predicate uses `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[step]` and `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[step]` (AA-22) | pool, tier 4 | `attention.step` (level crossing a band edge) |
| CALM | TELL | new `tell_epoch`, `phase == TELL` | **onset (carries beat 0)**; the bed starts at tier layer 1; no new pre-tell creak starts | reserved impulse voice plus the bed stream | `attention.step` (Attention's level and arming) |
| TELL | TELL | `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` strictly greater | **one beat** (at most one per poll) | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` (`beat_schedule`) |
| TELL | TELL | `tier` increases | crossfade to the next bed layer over `TIER_XFADE_TICKS`; add the positional hull-stress layer at tier 2 | bed stream / pool tier 3 | `attention.step` (`TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS`) |
| TELL | CALM | `tell_outcome == ABORTED` | beats stop; the bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS`; the release cue | reserved impulse voice | the player's restraint, through Attention (`ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`) |
| TELL | BREACH | `phase == BREACH` | the bed stream stops; the **breach-entry event is pulse 0**, with the bounded step (D.5); the alarm cadence begins | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` (`breach_due_T`) |
| BREACH | BREACH | the alarm clock reaches a pulse start (D.2) and the minimum gap holds | one alarm pulse, at most `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` of the sweep | reserved impulse voice | the tick grid (a pure function of `T`, `breach_enter_T`, the listener's body) |
| BREACH | SUBSIDING | `phase == SUBSIDING` | the alarm stops; one collapse; **no "danger over"** | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` |
| SUBSIDING | CALM | `phase == CALM` | **nothing** (never an all-clear) | none | `attention.step` |
| CALM | BREACH | a hitch skipped TELL | breach entry as above; no stale beat, no onset | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` |
| any | any (restore) | the post-restore notification | prime all last-seen values; no one-shot; the bed fades in if TELL; alarm pulses resume on the next grid tick if BREACH; nothing plays if CALM after ABORTED | the bed stream | Save/Load, Kernel |

#### S3. The alarm clock's branch (a small sub-machine inside BREACH)

| State | Entry | Exit | Behaviour | Who causes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ALIGNED | at a pulse boundary: the BEACON emitter is live **and** the listener is on the home-ship's body | at a pulse boundary: either condition false | pulses at `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` | the Emitter (beacon commanded at T+1, off at SUBSIDING entry), the listener's body |
| FALLBACK | at a pulse boundary: otherwise (derelict, `NOT_LIVE` home-ship, beacon absent or not yet commanded) | at a pulse boundary: ALIGNED conditions true | pulses at `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` | the listener's body, the Emitter |

A pulse in either state is emitted only if `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` have passed since `last_alarm_T` (shared with the Graph annunciator). The branch is re-evaluated only at a pulse boundary.

#### S4. The `hal` cue machine, as consumed from the Perception Filter `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan; states are Sanity's, mapped to sound by PF-12)`

| State | Entry (Filter request) | Audio action | Exit | Who causes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NONE | no request for the ref | silent | an ON request | Sanity decides *whether, when, for whom*; the Filter emits the request |
| ONSET | ON on the first ACTIVE frame (`lie_tau = 0`) | the SOUND leak starts at once, no later than the glide becomes visible | `lie_tau >=` the arrival time | the Filter (PF-12, PF-15) |
| HOLD | the glide has arrived | the leak is sustained at the D.15 level with the 4 to 6 s breathing | the entry leaves `sanity.perception` | the Filter |
| RETURN | the entry left `sanity.perception` (expired, debunked or invalidated: indistinguishable) | the level follows the remaining discrepancy, with the over-reaching tail; never below the ACTIVE minimum before CLEAR; **no sting, no verdict, no relief cue** | the discrepancy is below the Filter's threshold, then OFF | the Filter (PF-12, PF-14) |
| restore | the post-restore notification | resume in whatever state the Filter reports from the first frame (no clean frame) | as above | Save/Load, the Filter |

A lie with no SOUND mode in force has no request, so no machine instance exists for it. A stage change never ends a lie (SN-16) and so never ends this machine.

#### S5. The tell bed's lifecycle (per listener)

| State | Entry | Exit | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|---|
| ABSENT | `phase != TELL` and no release in progress | `phase == TELL` | no bed |
| RUNNING (tier 1/2/3) | onset; restore into TELL (with the `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` fade) | tier step (layer crossfade) or TELL exit | base bed; layers added at tiers 2 and 3; unaffected by zoom, camera, room, transit, reduce-flashing |
| RELEASING | TELL to CALM with ABORTED | `RELEASE_TICKS` elapsed | the level falls to silence by `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` over `RELEASE_TICKS`; a release cue sounds |
| ENDED | TELL to BREACH | immediate | the stream stops; the breach-entry event's tail carries the transition |

#### S6. Listener contexts (no change to cue content)

Listener on the home-ship's body, on a derelict, in a pod (LEG) or on a `NOT_LIVE` home-ship changes only (a) the alarm clock reference (S3), (b) the room-graph and door penalties (AA-10) and (c) the pod leg bed (AA-33). The tell bed, the beats, the witness classes and the `hal` rules are identical in every context (AA-46).

### Interactions with Other Systems

**Reading the tables.** Audio writes **nothing** to the simulation in any row below: "data out" is "none" everywhere, because its only output is sound. "Data in" lists the exact read view, dirty key, event or request the counterpart fixed. "Names fixed elsewhere" lists the hook, mutator, view or key names that Audio consumes verbatim and never renames. Each row **quotes the counterpart's own Interactions row** (trimmed) so bidirectional consistency can be checked; where the counterpart carries a stale token (it says "no file", "undesigned", "Soft", or cites a dangling `AUD-13`/`AUD-14`), the row says so and the correction is a *refresh owed* in Cross-References (rows RF-1 to RF-11), never an edit made here. "(provisional)" marks every neighbour that is in flight or absent.

#### I-B. Event sources (soft dependencies; each fixes the name Audio consumes)

Layout: `hull_anchor(section_id)`. Filter: the audio request fields of PF-20 as written, and a `Hard` filing of Audio. Attention: path and wording refreshes. Emitter: nothing new (`emitters()` already publishes `output_pm` per emitter).

## Formulas

**Conventions.** Cue *existence*, identity and timing are integers: published counters, sim ticks `T` and the shared integer presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`, Attention, AT-28; shared with Lighting, LO-19); gains, pitches and rates are presentation floats (the presentation layer never feeds the sim, CR-11) and tests compare them to 0.01 dB or 0.01 semitone. **Levels are in dBr**: authoring-reference decibels where 0 dBr is the Witness bus nominal peak. dBr is a mixing scale, not a loudness unit; the two loudness-bounded rules (D.5, D.6) are verified by offline capture, because Godot has no loudness meter. Attention's `beat_schedule` (F-12), `ambient_cue_step` (F-24) and `tell_window_contract`, Emitter's `beacon_aim` (EM-16), Assembly's `outcome_cue_gate`, the Graph's band tables (LS-7) and the Controller's odometer (PCI D.11) are **cited by name and never restated** beyond the one expression a worked example needs. Every constant marked `[PLACEHOLDER]` is Audio's, with its safe range in Tuning Knobs; locked names (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR`, `UNFOLD_LO`, `LIES_MAX`, `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS`, `TILE_PX`) are used at their registered values. Where the two specialist drafts differed the choice is written `(orchestrator ruling: ...)`.

**D.1 Cue dedupe and the per-poll classifier**

The `cue_dedupe` formula is defined as:

`lex_gt = (e > e_seen) OR (e == e_seen AND b > b_seen)` ; event (rows tested in order, first match wins): `ph == TELL AND e > e_seen` -> ONSET (this *is* beat 0) ; `ph_seen == TELL AND ph == TELL AND e == e_seen AND b > b_seen` -> BEAT ; `ph_seen == TELL AND ph == CALM AND out == ABORTED` -> RELEASE ; `ph_seen in {TELL, CALM} AND ph == BREACH` -> BREACH_ENTER ; `ph_seen == BREACH AND ph == SUBSIDING` -> SUBSIDE ; any other pair or a restore -> none ; **the rows are mutually exclusive on the published `(ph, e, b)`** (each tests a different `ph`, and ONSET `e > e_seen` excludes BEAT `e == e_seen`), so a poll classifies the *net* change from `seen` to `now` and at most one row can match; the intermediate transitions of a hitch are not observable and are not played (the lost-cue consequences are E-AA-67 and E-AA-68) ; after **every** poll, event or not, `seen := now`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e`, `e_seen` | int | 0 to 2^31 - 1 | `attention.view` `tell_epoch` / presentation memory | Epoch published now / last seen; +1 at each TELL entry |
| `b`, `b_seen` | int | 0 to about 21 | `attention.view` `tell_beat` / presentation memory | Beat index within the epoch (an 1800-tick tell has about 20.6 beats, `beat_schedule`) |
| `ph`, `ph_seen` | enum | CALM, TELL, BREACH, SUBSIDING | `attention.view` `phase` / memory | **Never `breach_phase`** (lint L-1; AC-AT-67) |
| `out` | enum | NONE, ABORTED, BREACHED | `attention.view` `tell_outcome` | Distinguishes an abort from a breach |
| `lex_gt` | bool | | derived | Lexicographic pair compare; a new tell resets `b` to 0 while `e` rises |
| result | event id or none | 0 or 1 per poll | derived | At most one `attn` event per poll (AUD-3) |

**Output Range:** 0 or 1 event per poll. The ceiling on beat cues is `TICK_RATE / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` = 2 per second; the nominal closing interval `BEAT_END_TICKS` gives 1.33 per second. A poll interval above 500 ms can coalesce beats: accepted as "one observable change", which is why AC-AT-47's audible half is captured at nominal frame rate. `seen := now` (never `seen + 1`) means a skipped beat is never replayed (AUD-3, AC-AT-115), and a restore that returns an *earlier* epoch cannot leave `seen` ahead of the next real onset (a false calm).
**Example:** `attention.step` at `T` = 12000 enters TELL and publishes `tell_epoch` 7, `tell_beat` 0 in the same run (AUD-2); the next poll sees `(7, 0)` with `ph_seen` CALM: ONSET, `seen := (7, 0, TELL)`. Beats at jitter 0 fall at 150, 292, 425 and 551 ticks after entry (`beat_schedule`, F-12). A 3-second hitch spanning ticks 12292 to 12472 publishes `(7, 2)` and `(7, 3)`; the next poll sees `(7, 3)` with `seen = (7, 1)`: **one** BEAT, beat 2 never sounds, `seen := (7, 3)`. A poll that reads `ph` BREACH at `T` = 12900 with a stale higher `b`: BREACH_ENTER only, no stale beat. A restore that returns `(7, 3, TELL)`: no event, `seen := (7, 3, TELL)`; the next published `(7, 4)` plays. A new tell `(8, 0)` after `(7, 9)`: `8 > 7`, ONSET (comparing `b` alone would drop it).

**D.2 Alarm pulse phase, duty and minimum gap**

The `alarm_pulse_phase` formula is defined as:

`S = BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` ; `W = floor(S * ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM / 1000)` ; `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS = S div 2` ; `mode_next = ALIGNED if (beacon_live AND listener_body == home_ship_body) evaluated at the tick of the last alarm-family pulse, else FALLBACK` ; `hit(T) = (T mod S == 0)` if ALIGNED, `((T - breach_enter_T) mod S == 0)` if FALLBACK ; `emit(T) = hit(T) AND (T - last_alarm_T >= ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS)` ; on emit `last_alarm_T := T` and `mode_next` is re-evaluated ; **on a restore into BREACH** `mode_next` is evaluated at the restore tick and `last_alarm_T` is primed to the latest `hit` of that mode at or before `T` ; the breach-entry event at `breach_enter_T` is **pulse 0** and sets `last_alarm_T := breach_enter_T`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S` (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`) | int ticks | registered | registry (Emitter) | One alarm pulse per sweep (M1); not restated |
| `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` | int permille | 100 to 400 (default 250) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Share of each sweep the alarm family may sound (M3 cap) |
| `W` | int ticks | 12 to 51 (32 at 250) | derived | Longest authored pulse window; an asset-length lint (AA-47) |
| `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | `S div 2` | derived | Smallest spacing of two alarm-family pulses (shared across `attn` and `ann`) |
| `beacon_live` | bool | | derived from Emitter `emitters()` | `exists e in emitters() with e.kind == BEACON and e.body_id == home-ship body and e.output_pm > 0` (EM-27; CX-A15 provisional) |
| `listener_body` | body id | | Body | The body the listening avatar is on |
| `breach_enter_T`, `T` | int ticks | >= 0 | `attention.view`; Kernel | Entry tick; current sim tick |
| `last_alarm_T` | int ticks | >= 0 | presentation memory | Last pulse of either identity (`attn`, `ann`); primed on restore (below) |

**Output Range:** Counting the entry event as pulse 0, a BREACH of length `B` has `floor((B - 1) / S) + 1` pulses in FALLBACK: 8 at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (900) and 29 at `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` (3600). In ALIGNED the count is that value or one fewer, so the range over both clocks is 7 to 29. Within one mode the spacing is exactly `S`; the first pulse after a mode choice is the first hit with gap >= `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, so the spacing after the entry event or after a mode switch lies in `[ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS, 3 * ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1]` ticks, 64 to 191 at the registered sweep (a first hit under the minimum gap is skipped and the next lands at most 63 + 128 ticks away).
