The union duty of `attn` and `ann` is at most `W / S` = `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM / 1000`, because two pulses that start on the same tick are one rhythm with two stacked timbres, and the shared gap forbids a second start within `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (> `W`). **On a restore into BREACH** `last_alarm_T := ` the latest `hit` tick <= `T` (the pulse that would have sounded, not replayed). The annunciator `ann` uses the absolute grid `T mod S == 0` outside BREACH and the same `hit(T)` while `phase == BREACH`. `beacon_aim(T)` is Emitter's pure function of absolute `T` and returns to its authored phase exactly at `T mod S == 0` (EM-16, EM D.10), so the aligned grid needs no `beacon_phase` read.
*(orchestrator ruling: the mode for the next pulse is chosen at the previous pulse, not continuously. A continuous choice lets a late switch stretch the gap to 255 ticks; this form makes the written 64 to 191 bound exactly true.)*
**Example:** `S` 128, `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` 250, so `W` = 32 ticks (0.53 s). *FALLBACK*, `breach_enter_T` = 20000: pulses at 20000 (the entry event), 20128, 20256, 20384. *ALIGNED*, `breach_enter_T` = 20000 (`20000 mod 128` = 32): entry at 20000, the first grid hit is 20096 (96 ticks after, >= 64) and later hits are 20224 and 20352. *ALIGNED*, `breach_enter_T` = 20060: the hit at 20096 is 36 ticks after entry and is skipped; the next pulse is 20224 (gap 164, inside 64 to 191). *Mode switch*: the listener leaves the home-ship body at `T` = 20500 after an aligned pulse at 20480 (`20480 = 160 * 128`): `mode_next` was evaluated at 20480 as ALIGNED, so the next pulse is 20608; at 20608 the mode is re-evaluated as FALLBACK (grid `20000 + 128 k`): the hit at 20640 is 32 ticks after and is skipped, the next is 20768 (gap 160). Restore at `T` = 20200 in ALIGNED: `last_alarm_T := 20096`, next pulse 20224.

**D.3 Release relation and bed release**

The `release_cue_relation` formula is defined as:

`release_cue_relation := (RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS) AND (STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS)` ; bed level during a release `L_bed(t_r) = L_bed0 - BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB * t_r / RELEASE_TICKS` for `0 <= t_r <= RELEASE_TICKS`, the stream stopping at `t_r = RELEASE_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RELEASE_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 119 (default 90) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Bed decay length on an abort; below 30 it is shorter than one beat gap and is not heard as a decay |
| `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | registered (a multiple of 6) | registry (Attention) | The hold that aborts a tell; `RELEASE_TICKS` must stay below it |
| `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` | int ticks | = `RELEASE_TICKS` | Attention (not a registry row) | The presenter's strand release; equality is the requirement |
| `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` | float dB | 36 to 60 (default 48) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Total drop over the release |
| `t_r` | int ticks | 0 to `RELEASE_TICKS` | derived | Sim ticks since the abort notification (frozen by pause) |

**Output Range:** `release_cue_relation` is a boolean that must be true at data load; a false value is a boot error (lint L-9). `L_bed` falls linearly in dB from `L_bed0` to `L_bed0 - BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB`, then the stream stops. `TELL_REARM_TICKS` (600) exceeds `RELEASE_TICKS`, so a release can never overlap the next onset.
**Example:** 90 < 120 and 90 == 90: true, with 30 ticks of margin. At `t_r` = 45 the bed is 24 dB down. `RELEASE_TICKS` 119 is true; 120 is false (`120 < 120`); `RELEASE_TICKS` 90 with `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` 91 is false.

**D.4 Restore bed fade**

The `restore_fade` formula is defined as:

`g(k) = 1` if `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS == 0`, otherwise `g(k) = sin( (pi/2) * min(1, k / RESTORE_FADE_TICKS) )` ; applied to the tell bed and its tier stems only

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `k` | int ticks | 0 to `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` | derived | Sim ticks since the post-restore notification (frozen by pause) |
| `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` | int ticks | 0 to 180 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Fade-in length; 0 disables the fade |
| `g` | float | 0.0 to 1.0 | derived | Linear amplitude multiplier on the bed |

**Output Range:** 0.0 at `k` = 0, 0.707 (-3 dB) at half the fade, 1.0 from `k` >= `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`. Beats are unaffected (each channel is sufficient alone, AUD-5) and resume at the next *published* pair (D.1). *(orchestrator ruling: the equal-power sine, the systems-designer's form, over the audio-director's linear ramp; a linear amplitude ramp on a single stream reads as a dip, the sine as a swell.)*
**Example:** restore into TELL with `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` 60: `k` = 15 gives `sin(pi/8)` = 0.383 (-8.3 dB); `k` = 30 gives 0.707 (-3.0 dB); `k` = 45 gives 0.924 (-0.7 dB); `k` = 60 gives 1.0. A pause at `k` = 20 holds `k` at 20.

**D.5 Breach-entry loudness bound**

The `breach_entry_trim` formula is defined as:

`LU_step = 10 * log10( (P_entry + P_bed + P_amb) / (P_beat + P_bed + P_amb) )` must be `<= BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` ; `P_entry_max = 10^(BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX / 10) * (P_beat + P_bed + P_amb) - (P_bed + P_amb)` ; `trim_dB = min(0, 10 * log10( P_entry_max / P_entry ))` applied as gain on the entry event

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_beat` | float, linear power | > 0 | asset metadata (K-weighted mean square, 400 ms momentary window) | Loudest beat asset played in this tell |
| `P_bed` | float | > 0 | asset metadata | The tier stems playing at the last beat (tier 2 or 3: `TELL_MIN_TICKS` equals `TIER2_TICKS`, so the earliest entry is already at tier 2) |
| `P_amb` | float | >= 0 | asset metadata | Concurrent non-tell ambience (the same in both windows) |
| `P_entry` | float | > 0 | asset metadata | The breach-entry asset (pulse 0) |
| `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` | float LU | 4 to 12 (default 8) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | M4 bound on the step from the last beat to the entry event |

**Output Range:** `LU_step` is 0 when `P_entry = P_beat` and rises with the entry asset; `trim_dB` is 0 or negative. When no beat played in the epoch (CALM to BREACH after a hitch skipped TELL) there is no beat to step from and no trim is applied; on a restore into BREACH the entry event does not play at all (D.1). The proxy is evaluated offline for every (beat asset, tier 2 or 3 bed, entry asset) triple and fails the build on violation (lint L-11); the live trim only guards authoring drift.
**Example:** normalise `P_bed` = 1.0, `P_amb` = 0.1, `P_beat` = 2.0, `P_entry` = 5.0: `LU_step` = 10 log10(6.1 / 3.1) = 2.94 LU, inside 8. The ceiling at 8 LU is `10^0.8` = 6.31, so `P_entry_max` = 6.31 * 3.1 - 1.1 = 18.5. A mis-authored `P_entry` = 25 gives `LU_step` 9.25 LU and `trim_dB` = 10 log10(18.5 / 25) = -1.3 dB.

**D.6 Bed audibility margin and tier steps**

The `bed_margin` formula is defined as:

`L_bed,b(t) = BED_BASE_DB + (t - 1) * TIER_STEP_DB` for tier `t` in 1..3 ; `L_nc,b = 10 * log10( sum_i 10^(L_i,b / 10) )` over the worst allowed concurrent non-tell set ; requirement for each bed band `b` in {B_LOW, B_EDGE}: `L_bed,b(1) - L_nc,b >= TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BED_BASE_DB` | float dBr | -24 to -12 (default -18) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Tier-1 bed level in each bed band at the listener (the bed is non-positional: no distance, door or zoom term, AA-16) |
| `TIER_STEP_DB` | float dB | 0 to 4 (default 2) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Bed loudness added per discrete tier step (AA-18) |
| `B_LOW`, `B_EDGE` | bands | about 30 to 120 Hz; about 200 to 400 Hz `[PLACEHOLDER]` | AA-45 band plan | The two bands holding the bed's energy; the edge layer keeps the bed audible on small speakers |
| `L_i,b` | float dBr | | asset metadata | Level of concurrent non-tell sound `i` in band `b` |
| worst set | | | AA-16 | `RUN_MAX_VOICES` running loops at `RUN_CEIL_DB`; one TOOL-class channel loop at `TOOL_LOOP_CEIL_DB`; the ambience at `AMB_TONE_DB`; the Suit fan at `SUIT_FAN_CEIL_DB`; the berth bed (while BERTHING) at `BERTH_BED_CEIL_DB`; the INTEGRITY room tone at `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB` (NORMAL band); up to `WIT_VOICE_CAP` sustained witness classes at `WIT_CEIL_DB - WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB` (a broadband hiss, wind or sizzle sits above 2 kHz and the asset spec holds its in-band energy `WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB` below its total) |
| `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` | float dB | 3 to 9 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | M2 margin |

**Output Range:** the margin is a floor, not a target; it is checked at tier 1 (the quietest state) and is larger at tiers 2 and 3. The data-load check computes `L_nc,b` from the knobs and refuses a set that cannot meet the margin; the *audible* result is a listening test with a retained capture on headphones and on laptop speakers (M2; not headless). Beats carry the evidence independently if the bed is lost (AUD-5). *(orchestrator ruling: the margin is power-summed over the concurrent set, which is stricter than the audio-director's single-loudest-bed form; the sustained witness classes are in the set only through their in-band energy, because a consequence-channel sound may not be attenuated to protect the tell, AA-11.)*
**Example:** `RUN_CEIL_DB` -36 with `RUN_MAX_VOICES` 4 contributes 4 * 10^-3.6 = 1.005e-3; the TOOL loop at -36 contributes 2.51e-4; the ambience, the Suit fan and the berth bed (`BERTH_BED_CEIL_DB` -44) at -44 contribute 3.98e-5 each; eight witness voices at -9 - 30 = -39 dBr contribute 8 * 10^-3.9 = 1.007e-3; the INTEGRITY room tone at `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB` -38 (NORMAL) contributes 10^-3.8 = 1.585e-4. The sum is 2.5415e-3, so `L_nc,b` = -25.95 dBr, and with `BED_BASE_DB` -18 the tier-1 margin is 7.95 dB, which is >= 6 (1.95 dB spare). At tier 3 the bed is -14 dBr and the margin 11.95 dB. A POWER hum at 100 Hz placed in `B_LOW` at -30 dBr would add 1.0e-3 (sum 3.5415e-3, -24.51 dBr) and drop the margin to 6.51 dB, with no spare once the other loops move: this is why the hum's fundamental is held at or above 160 Hz (AA-26, AA-45).

**D.7 Running-timbre mapping and the INTEGRITY room tone**

The `run_timbre` formula is defined as:

`audible = (state in {OK, DEGRADED}) AND (served_pm > 0)` ; `L_run = RUN_FLOOR_DB + (RUN_CEIL_DB - RUN_FLOOR_DB) * served_pm / 1000` ; `pitch_cents = RUN_PITCH_SPAN_CENTS * (served_pm - 1000) / 1000` ; per-tick change of `L_run` limited to `(RUN_CEIL_DB - RUN_FLOOR_DB) / RUN_SLEW_TICKS` ; at most `RUN_MAX_VOICES` running loops are started, the nearest by world px (ties: lowest node id) ; INTEGRITY room tone `L_tone = INTEGRITY_TONE_DB - TONE_THIN_DB[band_p]` with `TONE_THIN_DB = {NORMAL: 0, LOW: 6, DECOMP: 12, VACUUM: silent}`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `served_pm` | int permille | 0 to 1000 | Graph `node(id)` (LS-19) | Fraction of demand served; the same true value that dims the lamp (`LSG:213`) |
| `state` | enum | OK 0, DEGRADED 1, FAILED 2, DESTROYED 3, SALVAGED 4, ABSENT 5 | Graph `node(id)` | FAILED and above are silent; the failing sound is the descriptor's acoustic class (D.8), never a completion cue |
| `RUN_FLOOR_DB`, `RUN_CEIL_DB` | float dBr | -45 and -36 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Per-source level at `served_pm` 1 and 1000, at zero distance; `RUN_CEIL_DB` is bounded by D.6 |
| `RUN_PITCH_SPAN_CENTS` | int cents | 0 to 600 (default 300) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Pitch sag at `served_pm` 0 (never reached: a node with 0 is not running) |
| `RUN_SLEW_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 120 (default 30) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Slew over the Graph's divisor-6 publish (`graph.propagate.power` -> `.atmo` -> `.failure`) |
| `RUN_MAX_VOICES` | int | 2 to 6 (default 4) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Count cap that stands in for a limiter (AA-11 forbids attenuating tier 1) |
| `band_p` | enum | NORMAL, LOW, DECOMP, VACUUM | Graph `compartment(cid)` (LS-19), woken by `B/<cid>` | Pressure band of the listener's compartment |
| `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB` | float dBr | -44 to -32 (default -38) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Room tone of a pressurised hull; one pool-exempt stream per listener, never creaks |

**Output Range:** `L_run` lies in -45 to -36 dBr and `pitch_cents` in -300 to 0; the sum of `RUN_MAX_VOICES` loops at the ceiling is `RUN_CEIL_DB + 10 log10(RUN_MAX_VOICES)` = -30.0 dBr, which D.6 keeps below the bed. `L_tone` is -38, -44, -50 dBr in NORMAL, LOW, DECOMP and silent in VACUUM; the steps change only on the band event, so the tone is a discrete function of a true band, not a continuous meter. Coolant and O2 differ in timbre (AA-26 table), not in this mapping.
**Example:** the O2 scrubber with `served_pm` 750 plays at -45 + 9 * 0.75 = -38.25 dBr and -75 cents. When the Graph publishes `served_pm` 500 the target is -40.5 dBr, 2.25 dB lower; at 9 / 30 = 0.3 dB per tick the slew takes 7.5 ticks. The node going FAILED stops the loop at the `N/<id>` edge and the descriptor's HISS or HUM_DROP takes over. A compartment moving NORMAL to LOW on a `B/<cid>` key drops the room tone 6 dB; in VACUUM it is silent while the descriptor sounds (HISS, WIND, CREAK) still play.

**D.8 Witness level and rate, and the anti-suppression floors**

The `witness_level` formula is defined as:

`silent if iota == 0` **only for flow-fraction descriptors** (LEAK and hole-area BREACH flow, AIRFLOW: `LSG:158`; SEVERED: `LSG:772`; SEAL_STRAIN in its GAS/CREAK branch only: `LSG:649`), where 0 is the owner's "the drip stops" (`LSG:752`) ; **the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (|dT| > `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` with |dP| <= `WELD_DP_WARN`, `LSG:649`) carries an `intensity_pm` that measures |dP|, not its own cause, so it is driven by `sev` like the no-`iota` modes and is never silent while `sev >= 1` and `medium != NONE`** ; **for the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and FT-30's nine ids that have no `intensity_pm` (2 SHORT, 7 OVERLOAD, 3 OVERHEAT, 9 SCRAM, 4 FREEZE, 20 to 23 ATMO_COLD/HOT/THIN/DECOMP; `FT:197`), level is driven by severity, `L_w(sev) = G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB + (WIT_CEIL_DB - G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB) * (sev - 1) / 3`, and rate by `since_steps` and FT-30's per-row LIVE predicate; they are never silent while `sev >= 1` and `medium != NONE`** `(provisional — Graph adoption of FT-30 item c)` ; otherwise (a flow-fraction descriptor with `iota >= 1`) `L_w(iota) = G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB + (WIT_CEIL_DB - G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB) * (iota - 1) / 999` ; class driver by the table below ; **floors relation** (a data load that violates either line is refused): for every class `c`, `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB - ( FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE * max(0, R_AUDIBLE_PX[c] - NEAR_PX) / TILE_PX + TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB ) >= WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` and `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB >= AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` ; equivalently `R_AUDIBLE_PX[c] <= R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX = NEAR_PX + TILE_PX * (G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB - WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB - TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB) / FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `iota` (`intensity_pm`) | int permille | 0 to 1000; defined only for flow-fraction descriptors | Graph descriptor (LS-14) | The leak's, hole's, airflow's, severed-run's or seal-strain's (GAS/CREAK branch) true flow fraction (`LSG:158`, `LSG:649`, `LSG:772`); for those, 0 is the owner's silence (`LSG:752`: a valve closed upstream leaves the descriptor and sets `iota` to 0, "the drip stops"), not a suppression. A descriptor whose mode has no Graph-defined `intensity_pm` (mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage; SHORT, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT, SCRAM, FREEZE, ATMO_*; `FT:197`) has no `iota` and is driven by `sev` (below) |
| `sev` | int | 0 to 4 | Graph descriptor | The anti-suppression guarantee applies for `sev >= 1` and (`iota >= 1` or the mode has no `iota`) (`LSG:1110`); for the no-`iota` modes `sev` 1 to 4 drives `L_w(sev)`: -24.0, -19.0, -14.0, -9.0 dBr |
| `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` | float dBr | -30 to -18 (default -24) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Source gain at zero distance at `iota` 1: the anti-suppression floor at the source |
| `WIT_CEIL_DB` | float dBr | -12 to -6 (default -9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Source gain at `iota` 1000 (bounded by D.6 through `WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB`) |
| `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` | float dBr | -60 to -40 (default -54) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Post-attenuation floor for a `sev >= 1` witness inside its class radius (D.9) |
| `AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` | float dBr | -60 to -48 (default -56) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | A positional cue below this is not started |
| `R_AUDIBLE_PX[c]` | int px | table, each <= `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` data | Per-class guaranteed radius |
| `NEAR_PX`, `FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE`, `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB` | px, dB per tile, dB | 96; 3; 18 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | D.9 constants used by the relation |

Class drivers (continuous mapping from Graph **true values** is permitted and wanted, P1; the ban is on `level_pm` only):

| Class (id) | Driven parameter | Range | `R_AUDIBLE_PX` default |
|---|---|---|---|
| HISS 1 | level by `L_w`; pitch `+HISS_PITCH_SPAN_CENTS * iota / 1000` | 0 to +600 cents | 288 |
| DRIP 2 | rate `DRIP_RATE_MIN_HZ + (DRIP_RATE_MAX_HZ - DRIP_RATE_MIN_HZ) * iota / 1000` (0.5 to 4.0 Hz); impulse times from the presentation hash with the descriptor id as stream | 0.5 to 4.0 Hz | 144 |
| HUM_DROP 3 | pitch sag `-HUM_SAG_SPAN_CENTS * iota / 1000`, layered on the machine's running timbre (which sags with `served_pm`, D.7) | 0 to -600 cents | 288 |
| CLANK 4 | one-shot at the descriptor's first step (`since_steps == 0`), level by `L_w`; no recurrence | one event | 288 |
| WIND 5 | level by `L_w`; stereo from `dir` (0 to 7, 8 = none) | | 288 |
| TICK 6 | rate by `iota` only for a TICK whose descriptor is flow-fraction: `HULL_TICK_HZ_MIN + (HULL_TICK_HZ_MAX - HULL_TICK_HZ_MIN) * iota / 1000` (0.5 to 3.0 Hz); the flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK and the SEAL_STRAIN thermal TICK take rate from `sev` and `since_steps` (`HULL_TICK_HZ_MIN` at sev 1, `HULL_TICK_HZ_MAX` at sev 4); the flow-zero hull TICK is the only cue at zoom k = 1 (`FT:1381`) | 0.5 to 3.0 Hz | 144 |
| SIZZLE 7 | level by `L_w` | | 192 |
| CREAK 8 (SEAL_STRAIN) | rate `SEAL_CREAK_HZ_MIN + (SEAL_CREAK_HZ_MAX - SEAL_CREAK_HZ_MIN) * iota / 1000` (0.2 to 1.0 Hz), positional at the anchor | 0.2 to 1.0 Hz | 240 |

**Output Range:** `L_w` is -24 dBr at `iota` 1 and -9 dBr at `iota` 1000, never below `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` for `iota >= 1`; a flow-fraction descriptor at `iota` 0 is silent; a no-`iota` descriptor at `sev >= 1` plays at `L_w(sev)` (-24.0, -19.0, -14.0, -9.0 dBr for `sev` 1 to 4) and is never silent while `medium != NONE`. A WORN descriptor (`medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE) produces **no request** (`LSG:1111`). The request depends on the descriptor, never on light: a hiss in an unlit compartment plays at its true level (`FT:207`, E23). The Graph publishes at divisor 6, so `iota` changes at most every 6 ticks; the mapper slews level, pitch and rate over `SLEW_TICKS` but may not delay the *start* of a change beyond the poll that sees it ("audio may lead visuals"). The sustained rates of TICK (0.5 to 3.0 Hz) and SEAL_STRAIN CREAK (0.2 to 1.0 Hz) overlap the tell beat rate (0.4 to 1.3 Hz, `beat_schedule`); they are told apart by being **positional at the descriptor anchor** where beats are non-positional, and by lint L-10 and the identity table (a `wit` timbre tag never equals an `attn` tag).
*(orchestrator ruling: the audio-director's 768 px class radius with a -45 dBr floor cannot satisfy a floor relation (a 21 dB budget against 42 dB of distance cost); the radius is therefore derived from the floors and the per-class values are data below that bound. The checkpoint inequality is the load check, and the post-attenuation clamp (D.9) only guards rounding.)*
**Example:** HISS at `iota` 600: `L_w` = -24 + 15 * 599 / 999 = -15.006 dBr (-15.01 at two decimals), pitch +360 cents. `iota` 1: -24.0 dBr. A seam weep at `iota` 80: -24 + 15 * 79 / 999 = -22.814 dBr (-22.81 at two decimals; a test comparing at the 0.01 dB convention must use these two-decimal values). The relation with the defaults: `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` = 96 + 48 * (-24 + 54 - 18) / 3 = 96 + 48 * 12 / 3 = **288 px**; the HISS radius 288 gives -24 - (3 * 192 / 48 + 18) = -24 - 30 = -54 >= -54: holds with equality. A data row `R_AUDIBLE_PX[HISS]` = 768 gives -24 - (42 + 18) = -84 < -54 and the load is refused.

**D.9 Room-graph transmission, distance and pan**

The `transmission_attenuation` formula is defined as:

`A_d = FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE * max(0, d - NEAR_PX) / TILE_PX` ; `open_pm(door) = floor(1000 * area / area_max)` from the Graph `opening(edge_id)` read, **1000 if the state is unknown** ; `A_air(P) = DOOR_ATTEN_DB * sum over doors in P of (1000 - open_pm) / 1000 + HULL_ATTEN_DB * n_hull(P)` ; `A_str(P) = HULL_ATTEN_DB * STRUCT_FACTOR_PM / 1000 * n_hull(P)` ; `A_t = min( TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB, min over room-graph paths P of A_class(P) )` ; `L = L_src - A_d - A_t` ; for a `wit` source with `sev >= 1` and `d <= R_AUDIBLE_PX[class]`: `L := max(L, WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB)` ; `audible = (L >= AUDIBLE_CULL_DB)` ; `pan = clamp( dx / (PAN_SPAN_TILES * TILE_PX), -1, 1 )`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `d`, `dx` | int / float px | >= 0 / signed | derived | Distance and horizontal offset from the **listener avatar** (never the camera, its lead or zoom) to the source, both composed through `section_offset` and the body transform into one frame; **no zoom term exists** |
| `area`, `area_max` | int | `area_max` > 0 | Graph `opening(edge_id)` *(provisional, CX-A4)* | True opening state; a hole in the hull is an opening with the hole's area |
| `n_hull(P)` | int | 0 to 8 | Layout `room_at`, `segment_at` | Hull boundaries crossed by path `P` |
| `DOOR_ATTEN_DB` | float dB | 4 to 15 (default 9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | A fully closed door; the term falls continuously to 0 as the door opens |
| `HULL_ATTEN_DB` | float dB | 8 to 24 (default 15) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | One hull boundary, airborne |
| `STRUCT_FACTOR_PM` | int permille | 0 to 500 (default 250) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Structure conducts: structure-borne sources (the positional hull-stress layer, the seam-cut TOOL loop, Horror's ingress and egress creak) take this fraction of the hull term and **ignore doors** |
| `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB` | float dB | 12 to 30 (default 18) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Cap on geometry: transmission may soften, never delete |
| `PAN_SPAN_TILES` | float | 3 to 10 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Offset at which a source is hard-panned |

**Output Range:** `A_t` lies in 0 to `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB`; with an unknown door state `A_t` takes the door as open (0 dB), so the **default is audible** (R10). The tell bed is non-positional and exempt from every term. Distance is in world px and is identical at every step of the integer zoom ladder (k in [1, k_auto+2]) and at every camera lead (AA-9). A cull happens only when `audible` is false, never from voice count (D.10). No `Area2D` `audio_bus_override` zones are used (the 4.7 `area_mask` default 0).
**Example:** the HISS above (`iota` 600, -15.0 dBr), listener 6 tiles (288 px) away in the same room: `A_d` = 3 * 192 / 48 = 12 dB, `L` = -27.0 dBr. Behind one fully closed door: `A_t` = 9, `L` = -36.0. The door half open (`open_pm` 500): `A_t` = 4.5, `L` = -31.5. Door half open plus one hull boundary: 4.5 + 15 = 19.5, capped at 18, `L` = -45.0, still above -56. A `iota` 1 hiss (`L_src` -24) at the radius edge behind two closed doors and a hull boundary: `A_t` = 18 (capped), `L` = -24 - 12 - 18 = -54 = `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB`: the clamp changes nothing and the sound is audible. The seam-cut TOOL loop (structure-borne) across one hull boundary behind a shut door: `A_t` = 15 * 0.25 * 1 = 3.75 dB, the door ignored. A door whose `opening()` read is missing counts as open.

**D.10 Voice allocation, reserve sizing and stealing order**

The `voice_alloc` formula is defined as:

`RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES >= max(1, ceil(BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS))` ; **pool invariants** (data-load): `VOICE_POOL_SIZE >= RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP` (refused if false) and `VOICE_POOL_SIZE >= D123` (a DIAGNOSTIC warning if false) with `D123 = RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + HULL_STRESS_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP + SUIT_VOICE_CAP + FOOT_VOICE_CAP + HOR_VOICE_CAP + LIES_MAX(stage 3)` ; allocation for a request `r`: take a free general voice if one exists ; else the victim `v*` is `argmax` over active, non-PROTECTED voices `v` with `tier_v > tier_r` of the key `(tier_v, -L_listener(v), -start_T(v), cue_id(v))` (highest tier number, then quietest, then oldest, then `cue_id` lexicographic) ; if none exists the request is dropped, except a tier-1 request, which steals the quietest non-PROTECTED tier-1 voice quieter than itself and raises `voice_tier1_steal` ; `PROTECTED` = the reserved impulse voices, the tell bed and tier stems (pool-exempt streams), the positional hull-stress layer, the INTEGRITY room tone (pool-exempt), `hal` voices while their count <= `LIES_MAX`, and the seam-cut TOOL loop against requests of tier >= 4

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` | int | 32 to 64 (default 32) `[UNV]` | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Pooled players (run ruling R19 fixed 32; recommendation 48, OQ-AA-9) |
| `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` | int | 1 to 3 (default 2) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Never lent to another family; M5's "one" is the floor |
| `BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 90 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Longest audible tail of a beat asset (asset-length lint) |
| `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | registered | registry (Attention) | Minimum spacing of beats; not restated |
| `RUN_MAX_VOICES`, `WIT_VOICE_CAP`, `SUIT_VOICE_CAP`, `FOOT_VOICE_CAP`, `HOR_VOICE_CAP`, `HULL_STRESS_VOICES` | int | defaults 4, 8, 6, 2, 4, 1 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Per-family voice caps; `WIT_VOICE_CAP` is the **tier-1 witness cap** (it includes the `ann` annunciator) |
| `LIES_MAX` | int | 1 / 2 / 4 by stage 1 / 2 / 3 | registry (Sanity) | Per-seat live-or-pending class-(b) lies; a `hal` voice is never stolen while the count is within it |
| `tier`, `L_listener`, `start_T`, `cue_id` | int, dBr, tick, id | tiers 1 to 5 | request | The sort key; deterministic, with `cue_id` ordering via sorted keys (no Dictionary-order dependence) |

**Output Range:** `D123` with the defaults is 2 + 1 + 4 + 8 + 6 + 2 + 4 + 4 = **31**, so a pool of 32 holds tiers 1 to 3 with one voice to spare for tier 4: this is why `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` 48 is recommended and 32 is only the ruling's placeholder. The systems-designer's plausible worst-case scene (derelict B, stage 3, a told breach with a REACH, a TOOL channel active) demanded 37 voices with the three bed stems pooled and seven running voices; making the bed pool-exempt (AA-12) and capping running loops at `RUN_MAX_VOICES` brings the same scene to 31. The reserve is `ceil(60 / 30)` = 2 at the defaults and 1 if the beat tail is bounded at 30 ticks. A tier-1 steal is a bug in a CI soak (the audibility cull of D.9 must fire before the count cull).
**Example:** pool 32, 31 tiers 1 to 3 voices busy and one free: a tier-4 `ia_done` takes the free voice; a second tier-4 request finds no strictly lower victim and is dropped. A tier-1 `CLANK` with a full pool steals the quietest, oldest tier-4 voice. A pre-tell creak (tier 4) finding the pool full of tier 1 to 3 voices is dropped. With `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` 48 the same scene has 17 free voices. A pool of 12 fails the refusal check (2 + 4 + 8 = 14).

**D.11 REACH resolve lead-in and the strike ordering guarantee**

The `reach_resolve_start` formula is defined as:

`start_T = reach_end_T - REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` ; `seek = max(0, T_notify - start_T)` ; constraint `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO` ; the lead-in **ends at `reach_end_T` with no impact** and, if `horror.view` leaves REACH at tick `t_leave` without a `strike_T`, it stops at `t_leave` with a fade of `DECLICK_TICKS` ; the strike cue is requested in the handler of the P5 notification that publishes `strike_T` and starts at `strike_T`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `reach_end_T` | int ticks | `T_reach + UNFOLD_LO` to `T_reach + UNFOLD_HI` | `horror.view` (published on the run REACH begins) | The tick at which the REACH predicate is re-checked |
| `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 95 (default 90) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Length of the non-percussive lead-in asset; at most `UNFOLD_LO` so `start_T` is never before the REACH began |
| `UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI` | int ticks | registered | registry (Horror) | Shortest and longest REACH telegraph; not restated |
| `T_notify` | int ticks | | Kernel | Tick of the notification that reads `reach_end_T` (equals the REACH entry run in normal play) |
| `seek` | int ticks | 0 to `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` | derived | Late-start offset, nonzero only on a restore into the lead-in |
| `strike_T` | int ticks | >= `reach_end_T` | `horror.view` (STRIKE entry run) | Published only if the predicate held; the strike is the sole impact |
| `DECLICK_TICKS` | int ticks | 1 to 6 (default 2) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | De-click length |

**Output Range:** `start_T` lies in `[T_reach + 5, T_reach + 90]`, so `seek` is 0 in normal play for every legal unfold. The lead-in is scheduled against sim `T` and a pause freezes it. **A fizzle (HE-15/HE-17; `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH`; a restore that finds the predicate false) publishes no `strike_T` and so produces zero strike-cue requests**: the telegraph built and nothing struck, which is the truth. Between `reach_end_T` and `strike_T` there is at most one horror run; the strike cue is never pre-scheduled. The strike's "within 1 tick (17 ms)" (`HE:1285`) is an ordering guarantee (the request is created in the notification handler and `play()` is called in the same frame); measured output latency is a spike (OQ-AA-4).
**Example:** `horror.step` enters REACH at `T` = 7000 with an unfold draw of 140: `reach_end_T` = 7140, `start_T` = 7050, `seek` 0. A restore at `T` = 7100 starts the lead-in with `seek` 50 and a `DECLICK_TICKS` de-click (the restore fade is the bed's only, D.4). If the predicate holds, `strike_T` = 7140 (the first run with `T >= reach_end_T`) and the strike cue is requested on that notification. If `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` fires at 7100, the lead-in stops at 7100 and is silent by 7102 with zero strike requests. With the draw at `UNFOLD_LO` 95: `reach_end_T` = 7095 and `start_T` = 7005, five ticks in.

**D.12 Outcome cue gate usage and edge detection for interaction and assembly**

The `outcome_cue_usage` formula is defined as:

`cue_plays = (last_result.T != last_seen_T) AND (T_now - last_result.T <= OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS)` (Assembly's `outcome_cue_gate`, cited) ; after every poll `last_seen_T := last_result.T` even when the gate is false ; after every restore `last_seen_T := last_result.T` ; for `ia_*` keys: edge on `transition_seq` and `result_seq`, only the **terminal** transition's cue plays

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `last_result.T` | int ticks | >= 0 | Assembly / Interaction | Tick of the latest result |
| `last_seen_T` | int ticks | >= 0 | presentation memory | Latest result tick already consumed |
| `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` | int ticks | registered (range 12 to 180) | registry (Assembly) | Staleness window; not restated |
| `transition_seq`, `result_seq` | int | >= 0 | Interaction `channel_view` | Counters; a strictly greater value is an edge |

**Output Range:** a boolean per poll; at most one outcome cue per result. Priming on restore is defence in depth: Assembly clears `last_result` on restore, and a producer that does not clear cannot replay a ten-tick-old result after a load.
**Example:** a result at `T` = 1000; the poll at 1010 sees the gate true and plays the cue; the poll at 1011 sees `last_seen_T` = 1000 and plays nothing. Assembly's `post_restore` clears `last_result`, so no outcome cue can follow a load; Audio's priming `last_seen_T := last_result.T` is defence in depth for any producer that does not clear (Interaction results). Cancel, abort and "nothing happened" are three terminals with three different sounds (AA-32).

**D.13 Pre-tell creak predicate (stateless, floor-gated, bounded scan)**

The `creak_predicate` formula is defined as:

for each tick `t` in `( max(scan_T, T - CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS), T ]`: `eligible(t) = (phase == CALM) AND (s >= 1) AND (t - last_creak_T >= CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]) AND ( H(t, HASH_SEED, s) mod (CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s] - CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]) == 0 )` ; the first eligible tick starts **one** creak, sets `last_creak_T := t` and ends the scan for this poll ; the creak's plate is `H(t, HASH_SEED, s, 1) mod n_plates(listener_section)` ; after every poll `scan_T := T` ; **on a restore and on a resume from pause `scan_T := T`** (no backlog scan) ; load check for every step: `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s] > CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]`, the means strictly decreasing, each at least 1.3 times denser than the last

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s` | int | 0 to `CUE_STEPS` | `ambient_cue_step(level_pm, s_prev)` (Attention F-24; floor `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`, hysteresis `CUE_HYST_PM`) | The quantised step; **the only function of `level_pm` anywhere in Audio** (lint L-2); the value and `phase` at the poll are used for every scanned tick |
| `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` | int ticks | 360 to 2400, defaults 1800 / 1200 / 780 / 480 for s = 1..4 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Mean spacing at step `s` |
| `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]` | int ticks | `< CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]`, defaults 600 / 480 / 360 / 240 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Minimum spacing at step `s` (no burst) |
| `CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 120 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Cap on the ticks evaluated per poll: a long hitch is a bounded loop |
| `H` | int | 0 to 2^31 - 1 | shared presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`, AT-28; Lighting LO-19), **never `rng_derive`** | Pure integer hash; a presentation stream, not a sim stream |
| `last_creak_T`, `scan_T` | int ticks | >= 0 | presentation memory | Last creak start; last tick already scanned |
| `n_plates(listener_section)` | int | >= 1 | Layout `plate_positions` | HULL_SEGMENT plates of the **listener's own section** (a locus-anchored pre-tell creak would localise the future tell, AA-22) |

**Output Range:** 0 or 1 creak per poll (no catch-up). Four discrete densities, constant within a step: mean creaks per minute `3600 / CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` = 2.0, 3.0, 4.6 and 7.5 for s = 1..4 (successive ratios 1.5, 1.54 and 1.63, all >= 1.3); nothing at s = 0 (below `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`). The per-tick fire probability after the gap is `1 / (CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s] - CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s])`, so the mean spacing is the table value. Creaks are never suppressed in the ticks before a possible onset (a suppression would itself predict the tell). A creak in flight at onset finishes (its tail is bounded by `PRETELL_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`). The predicate holds the fallback of AA-22 in reserve.
**Example:** step 3 (780 / 360, modulus 420), `last_creak_T` = 5000: no creak before `T` = 5360; from then each scanned tick fires iff `H(t, HASH_SEED, 3) mod 420 == 0`. A poll at `T` = 5400 after a 12-tick hitch scans ticks 5389 to 5400. A 3-second hitch (180 ticks) scans only `(T - 60, T]`. A restore at `T` = 9000 sets `scan_T` = 9000: nothing is scanned retroactively and no creak plays on load.

**D.14 Footfall cadence and gait loudness**

The `footfall_cadence` formula is defined as:

`cycle_pos = floor( odometer / (STRIDE[gait] * 256) )` (PCI D.11; the odometer is in 1/256 px) ; `frame = posmod(anim_counter, 8)` (PCI D.11) ; a footfall fires on the poll in which `frame` becomes a member of `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` ; `interval_ticks = (TICK_RATE / ANIM_FPS) * (8 / |FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES|)` ; `gain_dB = FOOT_REF_DB + FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB * [gait == RUN]` ; `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB = round( 10 * log10(NOISE_RUN / NOISE_WALK) )`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `odometer` | int, 1/256 px | >= 0 | Body (PC-11, PC-14); post-collision moved pixels, diagonal weight `ODO_W` 181 | Walking into a wall adds 0; a standing avatar on a moving body adds 0; facing changes add 0 |
| `STRIDE[gait]` | int px | WALK 10, RUN 15, CARRY 5 | Controller (PC-11), cited | Base px per animation frame |
| `ANIM_FPS` | int | 12 | Controller (PC-11: "12 fps at every gait"), cited | Animation frames per second |
| `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` | set of frame indices | default {0, 4} of the 8-frame cycle | animation data, owned with the art-director `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Frames at which a foot lands |
| `FOOT_REF_DB` | float dBr | -12 to -3 (default -9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Walk level |
| `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB` | float dB | 3 to 9 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Mirrors the pushed noise ratio `NOISE_RUN : NOISE_WALK` of 4 : 1 (6.02 dB), presented as a true state |
| `NOISE_WALK`, `NOISE_RUN`, `NOISE_CARRY` | int | 1, 4, 1 | Controller (PC-10), cited; registry `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1) | Pushed units |

**Output Range:** with the defaults `interval_ticks` = 5 * 4 = **20 ticks (3 per second) at every gait**: `STRIDE_WALK` 10 at `V_WALK` 2, `STRIDE_RUN` 15 at `V_RUN` 3 and `STRIDE_CARRY` 5 at `V_CARRY` 1 each advance one animation frame per 5 ticks. **Gait changes the level and the timbre, never the rate:** the run is -3 dBr, the walk and the carry -9 dBr (carry footfall is flat and never scales with carried mass, `IDB:991`). A slowed avatar steps proportionally slower, and a time-stepped state (stagger, work, idle, down: PC-11 steps them at 12 fps from committed tick counts, not from the odometer) fires **no** footfall, the Suit's stagger gasp being the sound of a stagger; a diagonal keeps the cadence because the odometer weights each axis. The footstep is never a function of `level_pm`, locus distance, `phase`, Horror state or sanity.
**Example:** run 120 ticks (2 s): the odometer advances 360 px, which is 24 frames, three 8-frame cycles, so 6 footfalls at -3 dBr. Walk 120 ticks: 240 px, 24 frames, 6 footfalls at -9 dBr. Carry 120 ticks: 120 px, 24 frames, 6 footfalls at -9 dBr. Over 40 ticks each gait produces exactly 2. An avatar pressed into a wall for 120 ticks: the odometer is unchanged, 0 footfalls.

**D.15 Hallucination level, breathing and start**

The `hal_level` formula is defined as:

`L_peak = HAL_FLOOR_DB + (HAL_CEIL_DB - HAL_FLOOR_DB) * leak_amplitude_pm / 1000` ; `m(t) = 1 - (HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM / 1000) * (1 - cos(2 * pi * t / HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS)) / 2` ; `L_hal(t) = L_peak + 20 * log10( m(t) )` ; the breathing phase never phase-locks to `tell_beat` or the alarm sweep: its normalised cross-correlation against each stays below `BREATH_LOCK_CEIL` (Filter, AC-PF-27; cited, not restated) ; start `start_T <= glide_start_T` ; relations (data-load): `HAL_FLOOR_DB + 20 * log10(1 - HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM / 1000) >= HAL_ACTIVE_MIN_DB` and `HAL_CEIL_DB <= WIT_CEIL_DB - HAL_BUS_REL_DB`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `leak_amplitude_pm` | int permille | 0 to 1000 | Filter `perceived(ref, viewer)` *(provisional, PF-8)* | Remaining discrepancy; the level follows it through RETURN, never below the ACTIVE minimum before CLEAR; it arrives in steps of `AUDIO_LEVEL_STEP_PM` |
| `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` | int ms | 0 to 200 (Filter default 100) | Filter (PF D.15), cited | The latest the audible start may follow the first ACTIVE frame |
| `AUDIO_LEVEL_STEP_PM` | int permille | 10 to 50 (Filter default 20) | Filter (PF D.15), cited | The smallest change in `leak_amplitude_pm` the Filter re-sends as a LEVEL request; Audio slews between received levels and never extrapolates |
| `HAL_FLOOR_DB`, `HAL_CEIL_DB` | float dBr | -42 and -18 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Peak at amplitude 0 and 1000 |
| `HAL_RADIUS_PX` | int px | 96 to 288 (default 192) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Audible radius of a world-surface `hal` leak: `R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))`; beyond it the `hal` voice is culled over `HAL_FADE_TICKS`; no effect on a card or HUD reading or on any true family (AA-36) |
| `HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM` | int permille | 200 to 600 (default 400) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Depth of the slow amplitude breathing |
| `HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS` | int ticks | 240 to 360 (default 300) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | The 4 to 6 s breathing period of the violet fringe, cited `ART:563` |
| `HAL_ACTIVE_MIN_DB` | float dBr | -54 to -42 (default -48) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Lowest level of an ACTIVE leak: the leak is audible at its trough |
| `HAL_BUS_REL_DB` | float dB | 3 to 12 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Hallucination bus maximum below the witness nominal, so a lie cannot out-shout a true witness |
| `glide_start_T`, `start_T` | int ticks | | Filter request (PF-12, PF-15) | The SOUND request goes ON on the first ACTIVE frame, no later than the glide (`SN:1256`) |

**Output Range:** `L_hal` lies in `HAL_FLOOR_DB + 20 log10(1 - depth)` to `HAL_CEIL_DB`; with the defaults -46.4 to -18 dBr. For a world-surface ref the leak is audible only where the avatar-to-surface distance `d <= R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))` (default 192 px, 4 tiles; AA-36); beyond `R_hal` no `hal` voice exists, whatever `L_hal` is, and the boundary crossing fades over `HAL_FADE_TICKS`. A lie whose SOUND mode is dropped has **no request**, so no voice exists (at MVP `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0 and every lie has a sound). `HAL_FADE_TICKS` governs the OFF at CLEAR with no sting.
**Example:** `leak_amplitude_pm` 400: `L_peak` = -42 + 24 * 0.4 = -32.4 dBr; at the breathing trough (`t` = `HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS / 2`) `m` = 0.6, `L_hal` = -32.4 - 4.44 = -36.8 dBr. Relations: -42 - 4.44 = -46.4 >= -48 holds; -18 <= -9 - 6 = -15 holds. If the Filter's request has `start_T` 40998 and the glide starts at 41000, the leak sounds 2 ticks before the needle moves.

**D.16 Hull anchor derivation** *(provisional — Layout has no `hull_anchor(section_id)`; CX-A4)*

The `hull_anchor` formula is defined as:

`hull_anchor(section_id) = section_offset(section_id) + ( floor( sum_x / n ), floor( sum_y / n ) )` where the sums run over the `plate_positions` of the lowest-id HULL_SEGMENT in the section and `n` is the plate count (integer division, section-local base px composed with the offset)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `section_offset` | int px pair | | Layout (`LY:153`, `LY:309`) | Composition offset of the section |
| `plate_positions` | list of int px pairs | n >= 1 | Layout (`LY:266`) | Section-local base px of each plate of a HULL_SEGMENT |
| `locus_section_id` | id | | `attention.view` | The only place the tell is positional (AA-17) |

**Output Range:** an integer px pair, deterministic; Layout's own read supersedes it when added.
**Example:** the lowest-id segment has plates at section-local (96, 144) and (144, 144): centroid (floor(240 / 2), 144) = (120, 144); with `section_offset` (480, -96) the anchor is (600, 48).

**D.17 Pod leg bed envelope**

The `leg_bed_envelope` formula is defined as:

`F = max(1, min(LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS, n div 2))` ; `g(T) = clamp( min( (T - t0) / F, (t0 + n - T) / F, 1 ), 0, 1 )`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t0`, `n` | int ticks | `n` > 0 | `POD_DEPARTED{t0, n}`; `web.leg_timing()` (Web Map's confirmed name, CX-A19; `{t0, n}` only while LEG) | Departure tick and leg length |
| `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` | int ticks | 0 to 180 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Fade length (Web Map cites it as Audio's); 0 or `n` = 1 makes the envelope a step via the `max(1, ...)` guard |
| `g` | float | 0.0 to 1.0 | derived | Pod leg bed gain; the **tell bed is independent and unchanged in transit** (V13) |

**Output Range:** 0 at `t0` and `t0 + n`; a plateau of 1 for `n >= 2F`; on a short leg `F = n div 2` makes a triangle that still peaks at 1. If `web.leg_timing()` is unavailable on a restore mid-leg the bed starts at steady level 1.0.
**Example:** `t0` 1000, `n` 600, `F` 60: `T` = 1030 gives 0.5, `T` = 1300 gives 1.0, `T` = 1590 gives 10 / 60 = 0.167. `n` = 90: `F` = 45, a triangle peaking at 1.0 at `T` = `t0` + 45. `n` = 1: `F` = 1 (guard).
**Berth bed envelope (AA-33 (g)).** The berth bed has no `t0` and no `n` (the web read surface carries no progress datum, `WM:300`), so its envelope is edge-driven and uses the same `F = max(1, LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS)`: `g_berth(T) = clamp( (T - T_in) / F, 0, 1 )` from the edge `T_in` at which `transit_state` becomes BERTHING, and `g_berth(T) = clamp( 1 - (T - T_out) / F, 0, 1 )` from the edge `T_out` at which it leaves BERTHING (the fade-out overlaps the start of the contact cue, which is a separate one-shot). A restore into BERTHING starts at `g_berth` 1.0. Example: `T_in` 2000, `F` 60: `T` 2030 gives 0.5; `T_out` 5000: `T` 5045 gives 0.25. The envelope is a function of a state edge, never of progress, so it cannot be read as an ETA.

**D.18 Ducking**

The `bed_ducking` formula is defined as:

`duck_db(v) = -DUCK_DB` if `v` is a sustained bed of tier >= 4 and not exempt and a tier-2 impulse cue is inside its duck window, else 0 ; envelope: attack `DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS`, hold `DUCK_HOLD_TICKS` from the impulse start, release `DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` ; overlapping impulses re-trigger the hold and **never sum** (the depth is the maximum, `DUCK_DB`)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| victim set | | | AA-11 | Ambience, the TOOL-class channel loops **except the seam-cut TOOL loop**, the pod leg bed, the berth bed (AA-33 (g)) |
| exempt set | | | AA-11, AA-12 | Tiers 1 to 3, every PROTECTED voice (tell family, `hal`), the seam-cut TOOL loop (the far-side victim's tell, AA-33), Horror's cues, UI and Voice |
| tier-2 impulse | | | AA-2 | A beat, a strike, a breach pulse |
| `DUCK_DB` | float dB | 3 to 9 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Duck depth |
| `DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS`, `DUCK_HOLD_TICKS`, `DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` | int ticks | 3 to 12; 6 to 60; 12 to 60 (defaults 6, 24, 30) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Envelope |

**Output Range:** 0 or -`DUCK_DB`. Beats never duck under music (empty at MVP). No compressor, limiter or sidechain may attenuate a tier-1 cue (M3, lint L-8). Late in a tell the beat spacing (`BEAT_END_TICKS` 45) is shorter than attack + hold + release (60), so the duck holds continuously at `DUCK_DB` and never deepens.
**Example:** a beat at tell tick 150 ducks the ambience from 0 to -6 dB over 6 ticks, holds 24 ticks and releases over 30; the next beat at 292 finds the ambience restored. A seam-cut TOOL loop playing under the same beat is not ducked, and the beat stays audible over the other TOOL-class loops at the D.6 margin.



## Edge Cases

*Format: "If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]". Ids `E-AA-n` are numbered in reading order. Themes: the tell and cue delivery; the alarm clock; witness and running sounds; Horror; footsteps; save, restore, pause and death; hallucination interplay; geometry, listener contexts and zoom; pool, mix and data; co-op hooks.*

### The tell and cue delivery

- **E-AA-1. If a poll observes `phase` BREACH with a `tell_beat` above `b_seen`**: BREACH_ENTER plays and no stale beat does (D.1 precedence). A knock after the breach event would read as a second breach.
- **E-AA-2. If a hitch coalesces beats 2 and 3**: one BEAT plays, for beat 3, and `seen := (e, 3)`; beat 2 never sounds and is never replayed. The floor `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` is a *published-beat* guarantee (AUD-5); a catch-up burst is forbidden (AUD-3).
- **E-AA-3. If the frame time exceeds about 500 ms across an armed tell**: the audible beat count may fall below `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR`. The audible half of AC-AT-47 is therefore captured at nominal frame rate; the headless half asserts the published count.
- **E-AA-4. If a new `tell_epoch` arrives while `b_seen` is higher than the new `tell_beat` (a new tell resets `b` to 0)**: the lexicographic compare fires ONSET, which carries beat 0, and no second beat-0 cue plays. Comparing `b` alone would drop the onset.
- **E-AA-5. If a hitch skipped TELL and the poll sees CALM to BREACH**: BREACH_ENTER plays with no onset, no stale beat and no D.5 trim (there is no beat to step from, and the bed never started). Whether such a breach was fair is Attention's rule, not Audio's.
- **E-AA-6. If a pre-tell creak is in flight when ONSET arrives**: it finishes (its tail is at most `PRETELL_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`) and no new creak starts. A cut at onset would be an audible anomaly ahead of beat 0.
- **E-AA-7. If `ambient_cue_step` is 0 (below `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`)**: no pre-tell creak plays. If `level_pm` sits on a step edge, Attention's `CUE_HYST_PM` stops the step flickering, so the density changes only on a real step change. Audio never maps `level_pm` to anything else (lint L-2).
- **E-AA-8. If `level_pm` stays high in CALM after an abort (a rearm is pending)**: creaks continue at the step's fixed density and nothing else changes; `TELL_REARM_TICKS` governs when a new TELL may start. The step is the only readout, and it is quantised.
- **E-AA-9. If the player aborts a tell**: the beats stop, the bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS` (90) and a release cue sounds, with no sting. `TELL_REARM_TICKS` (600) exceeds `RELEASE_TICKS`, so a release and a new onset can never overlap.
- **E-AA-10. If an abort and a breach resolve in the same poll**: Attention decides the outcome and publishes one net `ph`; Audio classifies the net change from `seen` to `now` (D.1 rows are mutually exclusive), so it plays BREACH_ENTER if the published `ph` is BREACH and RELEASE only if it is CALM with `out == ABORTED`. A breach is never masked by a release because a published BREACH is never also a published CALM.
- **E-AA-67. If a hitch spans an abort, `TELL_REARM_TICKS` and a new onset (added by the Acceptance Criteria review, QA finding F-2)**: the poll sees the new epoch in TELL against `seen` in TELL of the older epoch and plays ONSET only; the RELEASE of the older tell is not played, because its transition is not observable from the net change. The bed is re-requested by the new onset. Accepted: a 600-tick rearm plus a hitch that long is a failure of the frame loop, not of a rule, and the new tell is still told.
- **E-AA-68. If `ph` goes from BREACH to CALM with SUBSIDING skipped by a long hitch (added by the Acceptance Criteria review, QA finding F-2)**: no SUBSIDE collapse is played (the row needs `ph == SUBSIDING`), the alarm cadence stops, and `seen := now`. Nothing is replayed and no all-clear is ever sounded (AA-21); the silence is the same silence the subside would have left.

### The alarm clock

- **E-AA-11. If `breach_enter_T` is itself a grid hit (`T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` while ALIGNED)**: one pulse plays. The entry event is pulse 0 and `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` suppresses the grid hit at gap 0 (D.2).
- **E-AA-12. If the first aligned grid hit lands 36 ticks after entry**: it is skipped (under `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS`) and the next pulse is the following hit, 164 ticks after entry, inside the 64 to 191 bound (CX-A14: accepted, because the entry event is itself pulse 0).
- **E-AA-13. If the listener changes body mid-BREACH (a pod hop, a detach)**: the clock for the *next* pulse is chosen at the last pulse, so the spacing after the switch stays within 64 to 191 ticks (D.2 example: 20608 then 20768). A mid-sweep switch can never produce two stings inside a second.
- **E-AA-14. If the home-ship is `NOT_LIVE`, the beacon is commanded a tick late or is absent, or reduce-flashing is on**: the alarm uses the FALLBACK or ALIGNED clock by the `beacon_live` rule and its content is unchanged. Reduce-flashing never reduces audio (M1; FT E20, E21).
- **E-AA-15. If BREACH_ENTER, an `alarm_eligible` annunciator pulse and a Horror strike land in the same poll**: all three are requested in the same frame in the order `ann` (tier 1), strike (tier 2), BREACH_ENTER (tier 2), on free and reserved voices. The `ann` pulse and the `attn` pulse share the grid and `last_alarm_T`, so two pulses starting on one tick are one rhythm with two stacked timbres and the union duty stays within `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM`.
- **E-AA-16. If a descriptor becomes `alarm_eligible` outside BREACH**: its witness class and the Suit's body cues sound on the poll that sees it, and the `ann` pulse waits for the next `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` tick (at most one sweep). This is not a suppression: the annunciator is presentation layered on a consequence sound that was never delayed (CX-A18).
- **E-AA-17. If a descriptor stops being `alarm_eligible` mid-pulse**: the pulse in progress completes (at most `W` ticks) and no further pulse starts. A cut mid-pulse would be an audible anomaly.
- **E-AA-18. If SUBSIDING returns to CALM**: nothing plays, never an all-clear. STRESS can outlive SUBSIDING (`AT:1210`), and the failing-loop `wit` sounds carry the ongoing wrongness (AUD-10).

### Witness and running sounds

- **E-AA-19. If a LEAK descriptor's `intensity_pm` is 0 because its valve is closed upstream**: its class is silent while the descriptor persists. This is the owner's semantics (`LSG:752`), not a suppression; the `LSG:1110` lint is read with that carve-out. The carve-out covers the flow-fraction descriptors of D.8 only (LEAK, hole-area BREACH flow, AIRFLOW, SEVERED, and SEAL_STRAIN in its GAS/CREAK branch): a no-`iota` descriptor (mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage, SHORT, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT, SCRAM, FREEZE, ATMO_*) and the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (whose `intensity_pm` measures |dP|, not its thermal cause, `LSG:649`) are never silenced this way (D.8, L-13).
- **E-AA-20. If a `sev >= 1` hiss at `intensity_pm` 1 is 6 tiles away behind two closed doors and a hull boundary**: `A_t` is capped at `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB`, `L` = -54 dBr = `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB`, and it is audible (D.9 example). Geometry softens a witness; it never removes one inside its class radius.
- **E-AA-21. If the compartment has zero lit emitters (a dark room)**: the hiss still plays at its full true level and no mist is drawn. This is expected, not a bug (`FT:207`, E23): the request depends on the descriptor, never on light.
- **E-AA-22. If a descriptor is WORN (`medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE)**: no request exists and no sound plays (`LSG:1111`; lint L-7).
- **E-AA-23. If a door is animating (`area / area_max` = 0.4)**: the door term is `9 * 0.6` = 5.4 dB and follows the true area continuously. If `opening(edge_id)` is not yet published, the door is treated as **open** (R10): a guessed muffle that hid a real hiss would be a suppression by default.
- **E-AA-24. If the listener's own compartment is in the VACUUM pressure band (`compartment(cid).band_p`)**: the INTEGRITY room tone is silent, and airborne sources reach the listener by the structure-borne model (`A_str`, contact with the suit and hull) instead of `A_air`; no witness is removed (P1). The timbre of a suit-conducted sound is the sound-designer's. *(orchestrator ruling: a deterministic transmission class rather than a silent mute; OQ-AA-11.)*
- **E-AA-25. If a running node goes FAILED, DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT**: its running timbre stops at the `N/<id>` edge with no completion cue; the descriptor's acoustic class (the failing variant) takes over. "The reward is the absence" (`FT` E7).
- **E-AA-26. If more than `RUN_MAX_VOICES` running loops are within range**: only the nearest `RUN_MAX_VOICES` are started (ties: lowest node id); the rest are beyond the physical radius or beyond the cap. The cap stands in for a limiter because a limiter on tier 1 is forbidden (AA-11), and the INTEGRITY room tone is a separate pool-exempt stream.
- **E-AA-27. If the player plays with the screen dark (by sound alone)**: every consequence sound is present (witness at anchors, running loops, tell bed and beats). This strategy is protected, not degenerate (`LSG:800`).

### Horror

- **E-AA-28. If a REACH fizzles (the predicate fails at the last tick, the phase has returned to CALM, `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH`, or a restore finds the predicate false)**: no `strike_T` is published, so **zero strike-cue requests** are made; the non-percussive lead-in ends at `reach_end_T` (or stops over `DECLICK_TICKS` if cancelled earlier). A pre-committed impact would be a true-family sound asserting a strike that never happened.
- **E-AA-29. If the REACH is the shortest legal unfold (`UNFOLD_LO` 95)**: the lead-in (`REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90) starts 5 ticks in with `seek` 0; the constraint `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO` guarantees `start_T` is never before the REACH began.
- **E-AA-30. If the strike lands (`strike_T` published)**: the strike cue is requested in the same notification handler and starts at `strike_T`, positional at the seats and hull segments hit; the lead-in already ended at `reach_end_T`. Strike one is witnessed by the sound; it is not pre-announced by an impact.
- **E-AA-31. If a hallucination hides a real horror's body**: its floor-borne creep keeps sounding (`CON:243`, `SN:306`); Audio never silences a real entity because the viewer is lied to. Phantom horrors (VS-A) use `hal` only.
- **E-AA-32. If Horror's marks, L3 dips or disturbances change**: nothing plays; they are silent facts (AA-28). There is no proximity cue and no state stinger.

### Footsteps

- **E-AA-33. If the avatar walks into a wall**: the Controller's odometer adds 0 (post-collision pixels only, PCI D.11), so there are 0 footfalls; facing changes also add nothing.
- **E-AA-34. If the avatar stands still on a moving body (a pod in transit, a section rotating during a weld)**: 0 footfalls, because the odometer counts ship-local moved pixels, not frame motion.
- **E-AA-35. If the player walks, runs or carries for 40 ticks**: each gait produces exactly 2 footfalls (a footfall every 20 ticks at every gait); only the level (-9, -3, -9 dBr) and timbre differ. A test that expects a faster run cadence is wrong; the run is told by level and the pack rattle.
- **E-AA-36. If the avatar carries a heavy item**: the footfall is flat and never scales with carried mass (`IDB:991`). A sound that encoded a hidden value would be a meter by ear.
- **E-AA-37. If the avatar is in a slowed zone**: the odometer advances slower, so footfalls come proportionally slower at the same level. **If the avatar is staggered, working, idle or down** (the time-stepped states of PC-11): no footfall fires, because no odometer cycle is crossing a contact frame; the Suit's stagger gasp (AA-30) is the single sound of a stagger. One sound per true state.

### Save, restore, pause and death

- **E-AA-38. If a restore lands mid-TELL**: no onset or beat replays; the bed fades in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`; beats resume at the next *published* pair; `seen := now`.
- **E-AA-39. If a restore lands mid-BREACH**: the entry event does not replay; `last_alarm_T` is primed to the latest grid hit at or before `T`; pulses resume on the next grid hit with the gap rule.
- **E-AA-40. If a restore lands in CALM with `tell_outcome` ABORTED**: nothing plays (AUD-6). If it returns an *earlier* epoch than `e_seen`, `seen := now` with no cue, so the next real onset (a higher epoch) still plays and is never mistaken for a false calm.
- **E-AA-41. If a restore lands mid-lie**: the SOUND leak resumes with the lie at full leak level on the first frame; the Filter keeps no clean frame (PF-33), so a restore must not briefly drop a leak mode.
- **E-AA-42. If a restore lands inside a REACH lead-in or in STRIKE**: the lead-in starts with `seek = T_notify - start_T` and only a `DECLICK_TICKS` de-click (AA-5); in STRIKE the strike cue does not replay (the strike was an entry-run fact, E-HE-38).
- **E-AA-43. If a restore finds a result 10 ticks old (`last_result.T` = 4990 at `T` = 5000; a producer that does not clear)**: Assembly's `post_restore` clears `last_result`, so no outcome cue can follow a load; Audio's priming `last_seen_T := last_result.T` is defence in depth for any producer that does not clear (Interaction results), and no weld-done or terminal cue plays after the load (D.12).
- **E-AA-44. If the game is paused**: sim `T` is held, so every sim-derived sound pauses (the scheduled lead-in is frozen, the bed holds, `k` and `t_r` hold) and UI sounds continue; the sim is never paused by audio (CR-11). On resume the creak `scan_T := T`.
- **E-AA-45. If the player dies and the death stub restores**: no sound and no screen (`SL:23`). The stub is a restore like any other; `restore_reason` is not read and no content depends on it. Saving plays no sound.

### Hallucination interplay

- **E-AA-46. If the sanity stage is 0, 1, 2 or 3**: the request list of every TRUE family is bit-identical for the same published sim state (a property test); only the `hal` list differs, by Filter requests. No mix, EQ, ducking or ambience parameter reads the local player's own sanity (AA-38).
- **E-AA-47. If the Filter drops the SOUND leak mode of a lie at stage 2 or 3**: that lie has no audio request at all, and still keeps two leak modes (PF-15, never below two). At MVP `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0 and every lie has a sound.
- **E-AA-48. If more than `LIES_MAX` lies reach Audio**: the extras are dropped with a DIAGNOSTIC (a Filter bug), and no PROTECTED voice is stolen to make room.
- **E-AA-49. If the machine under a lying instrument has a real fault**: the machine's real hiss or hum still plays, because a lie can only add a fringed sound and never replace a true one. This is the falsifier (P4).
- **E-AA-50. If a lie is debunked, expires or is invalidated**: the three look identical to the ear: the level follows the remaining discrepancy, then OFF over `HAL_FADE_TICKS`, with no sting, no verdict and no relief cue (PF-12).
- **E-AA-51. If the Filter declines to emit audio requests**: MVP class-(b) lies play no reading-lie sound and HR-5's sound mode is unmet; that is a P1/P4 finding against the Perception Filter, not something Audio designs around (creative-director at CD-GDD-ALIGN: the Filter must emit audio requests for every MVP class-(b) lie while `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0; a refusal reopens PF-20, not AA-35). Audio keeps no reading-lie sound plus the CONFLICT diagnostic (CX-A3).
- **E-AA-69. If the viewer stands beyond `R_hal` of a lying world-surface instrument whose machine has a real fault or a normal running sound**: the `hal` leak is silent (culled, no voice), and the machine's true witness or running sound is heard at its ordinary level and audible floor; stepping inside `R_hal` fades the leak in over `HAL_FADE_TICKS` with no sting and no change to any true request. A card or HUD reading is non-positional and is not radius-gated. (Added at CD-GDD-ALIGN; the id is out of reading order.)
- **E-AA-52. If a viewer asks for the sound of another player's lie**: none is rendered; the Filter returns the honest record for any remote `PlayerId` (PF-10), and `hal` requests are `OWNER_PRIVATE`.

### Geometry, listener contexts and zoom

- **E-AA-53. If the zoom step changes (any k in [1, k_auto+2]) or the camera leads**: every request, gain and pan is unchanged, because attenuation and pan use world px from the avatar and no zoom term exists. A property test sweeps k.
- **E-AA-54. If the listener is on a derelict**: the alarm uses the FALLBACK clock and audio carries the breach alone (derelicts have no beacon, AUD-10); the bed, beats and witness classes are identical to the home-ship.
- **E-AA-55. If the listener is in a pod in transit (LEG)**: the tell bed is non-positional and unchanged; the pod leg bed follows D.17 and ducks under beats; positional sources are composed through the pod body's transform. **If a BERTHING runs while a TELL is active** (the hop and the berth are allowed during a tell, WM-26): the berth bed and the tell bed play together, the berth bed is a tier-4 victim and ducks under each beat, the tell bed is unchanged and L-10 keeps their timbres disjoint.
- **E-AA-56. If a seam is cut and the seam is closed**: the seam-cut TOOL loop is structure-borne and audible on both sides (door term ignored); it is never ducked and not stealable by tier >= 4 requests, because it is the far-side victim's tell (ASK AU1).
- **E-AA-57. If a source sits on a body that moves relative to the listener's body**: both anchors are composed in world px each frame, so a hull-mounted hiss stays on its hull while the ship drifts.

### Pool, mix and data

- **E-AA-58. If the voice pool is exhausted**: tier 4 and 5 requests drop first; a tier-1 request first steals by the D.10 key (highest tier number of 2 to 5, then quietest, then oldest); only if no such victim exists does it steal the quietest non-PROTECTED tier-1 voice quieter than itself and raise `voice_tier1_steal` (a bug in a CI soak); the tell family, the reserved impulse voices, the room tone, the bed and in-budget `hal` voices are never stolen.
- **E-AA-59. If a data file sets `RELEASE_TICKS` to 120 or more, or `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` differs from `RELEASE_TICKS`**: `release_cue_relation` is false and the boot refuses the file.
- **E-AA-60. If a data file gives a witness class a radius above `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` (768 px against a derived 288), a `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` at or below `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]`, a `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` above `UNFOLD_LO`, or a pool below `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP`**: the load is refused with the named relation. A silent clamp would hide a P1 break.
- **E-AA-61. If the worst-case concurrent set cannot meet `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` (D.6)**: the data load is refused; the listening test then verifies the shipped set. A POWER hum authored at 100 Hz fails the lint, which is why its fundamental is held at or above 160 Hz.
- **E-AA-62. If any bus is muted or the audio driver is the headless dummy**: `state_hash` is unchanged (AUD-9) and every cue request is still produced, since requests are a pure function of published state.
- **E-AA-63. If an asset is tagged with two families or two assets share a sound id across families**: the identity-table lint fails the build (AA-6 a, b, h); `ann` stays disjoint from `attn` even though both run on the alarm grid.

### Co-op hooks (VS-B, provisional)

- **E-AA-64. If a listener is dead *(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)***: no bed request is addressed to that seat (`ALL_LIVING`); the tell remains audible to every living player.
- **E-AA-65. If two crewmates both have `tell_on` (VS-B)**: two P1 cues play from their suits with different hash phases; neither plays on its own source's client (no self-sign, SN-14) and neither is altered by any listener's sanity.
- **E-AA-66. If a crewmate stands in a room two doors away**: their footsteps and suit sounds attenuate by D.9 like any airborne sound; true sounds are identical on every client, so "do you hear that too?" is a valid cross-check.


## Dependencies

Status vocabulary: **designed** (a GDD on disk with header Status "Designed"); **in-flight** (a draft on disk, header Status "In Design"); **undesigned-provisional** (no GDD on disk; this GDD states a request and re-checks it when the neighbour lands). Glob of `design/gdd/` at the time of writing: 23 files. "Names Audio?" answers the bidirectional-consistency rule: does the counterpart's own GDD name Audio & Tells in its Dependencies or Interactions, and with what wording.

| System | Direction | Hard or soft | Interface | Names Audio? | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Life-Support Graph | Audio depends on it | **Hard** (index) | descriptors (`acoustic`, `intensity_pm`, `sev`, `alarm_eligible`, `anchor`), `node()`, `loop_component()`, `failures()`, `compartment(cid).band_p`, the keys `N/<id>`, `L/<loop>/<anchor>`, `C/<cid>`, `B/<cid>`, `F/<id>` | yes, `LSG:270`, `LSG:865` ("provisional"; hum/wind wording stale, RF-5) | designed |
| Attention System | Audio depends on it | **Hard** (index) | `attention.view` fields of AT-16; `ambient_cue_step`; `beat_schedule` | yes, `AT:375`, `AT:977` ("provisional"; "no file", RF-1) | designed |
| Horror Entities | Audio depends on it | **Hard** (index `IDX:202`) | `horror.view` | yes, `HE:374`, `HE:926` ("undesigned"; `AUD-13`, RF-2) | designed |
| Sanity & Hallucination Rules | Audio depends on it | **Hard** in the index; "Soft" in `SN:490`, `SN:968` (CX-A6) | `sanity_view` (`tell_on` only, dormant at MVP); SN-25 authorisation | yes, `SN:490-496`, `SN:968` ("Soft downstream", RF-3) | designed |
| Command & Simulation Layer | Audio depends on it | **Hard** | `PRESENTATION` subscription, P5 notify, `read_view`, post-restore notification | yes (the subscriber table names audio nodes) | designed |
| **Perception Filter** | Audio depends on it | **Hard for the lie family**; soft otherwise (the Filter agrees, `PF:559`, `PF:1155`) | audio request `(viewer, ref or position, intent, class)`; `perceived()` leak modes; `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` | yes, `PF:559`, `PF:1155` | **designed** |
| Suit & Equipment | Audio depends on it | Soft (event source) | cue-state read-view fields | yes, `SE:474` | designed |
| Player Controller & Input | Audio depends on it | Soft | gait class, position, `channel_view` | yes, `PCI:211` row 11 | designed |
| Interaction System | Audio depends on it | Soft | `ia_*` keys, `transition_seq`, `result_seq` | yes, `IA:470` | designed |
| Modular Ship Assembly | Audio depends on it | Soft | `last_result`, `outcome_cue_gate`, seam-cut loop state | yes, `MSA:509` | designed |
| Ship Body & Interior Physics | Audio depends on it | Soft | `BODY_*` events, body transform | yes, `BDY:301` | designed |
| Web Map & Transit | Audio depends on it | Soft | `POD_*`, `BERTHED`, `UNBERTHED`, `DERELICT_*`, `transit_state` (BERTHING), `web.leg_timing()` (name confirmed, CX-A19) | yes, `WM:421`, `WM:892` | designed |
| Ship Section & Layout | Audio depends on it | Soft | `anchor_geometry`, `room_at`, `section_offset`, `segment_at`, `plate_positions`; `hull_anchor(section_id)` **ask** | not by name (Layout names the Graph ask only, `LY:426`) | designed; `hull_anchor` **undesigned-provisional** |
| Graph `opening(edge_id)` | Audio depends on it | Soft | door transmission; interim: unknown door is open | Layout's ask (`LY:426`) | **undesigned-provisional** (ask) |
| Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | Audio depends on it | Soft | `E/<id>` keys; `emitters()`; `beacon_aim(T)` | yes, `EM:338` | designed |
| Failure & Trail System | Audio depends on it | Soft (constraint) | FT-35, AC-137, AC-144 | yes, `FT:207` | designed |
| Save/Load & Versioned Format | Audio depends on it | Soft | restore notification; `SL:23` no-sound rule | by rule (`SL:23`) | designed |
| Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera | Audio depends on it | Soft (independence constraint) | none; the camera has no audio role | no | designed |
| Item & Module Database | Audio depends on it | Soft (constraint) | `IDB:991` | yes (a rule binding "every audio author") | designed |
| Boarding & Salvage | Audio depends on it | Soft | an event list | yes, `BS:333` | **designed** |
| HUD & Suit Layer | both | Soft | UI bus; the alarm warning mark's paired sound | yes (hud-and-suit-layer.md) | designed (hud-and-suit-layer.md) |
| In-World Instruments & Inspect View | both | Soft | the Filter's `perceived()` SOUND leak; UI-bus `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_OPEN` / `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_CLOSE` on `card_opened` / `card_closed`; a sound tell per IW-12 stress source (I-C row) | yes, `IW:397`, `IW:501` | designed (in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md) |
| Chart Table / menus | both | Soft | UI bus; pause behaviour | yes, `CT:393`, `CT:524`, `CT:570` | **in-flight** |
| Debug Overlay (True Values) | it depends on Audio | Soft (DIAGNOSTIC) | identity table, voice counters | yes (debug-overlay-true-values.md) | designed (debug-overlay-true-values.md) |
| Chat, Voice & Pings (VS-B) | both | Soft | `Voice` bus | no file | undesigned-provisional |
| Co-op Session (VS-B) | both | Soft | `listener_scope` | no file | undesigned-provisional |
| **Accessibility Settings** (Alpha) | **it depends on Audio** | **Hard** (reverse edge, `IDX:113`, `IDX:209`) | the boundaries of AA-41 to AA-43 | no file | undesigned-provisional |
| Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha) | it depends on Audio | Soft | the World-group slider rule | no file | undesigned-provisional |

**Downstream dependents among the systems designed in this run:** none. The only reverse hard edge in the index is Accessibility Settings. **Cycle check:** no cycle exists. Audio is a pure sink of the dependency graph (it registers no hook, block, mutator or command, so nothing in the simulation can depend on it), and the single soft cycle in the neighbourhood (Sanity "Soft downstream" versus an index hard edge) is a wording mismatch, not a data cycle: no neighbour reads a value Audio produces.

**Index corrections routed (this GDD never edits the index):** (1) the Progress Tracker row `IDX:96` and the Design Order row `IDX:240` move to "Designed" when this GDD is approved; (2) Sanity's "Soft downstream" is to be reconciled with the index's hard edge (RF-3, RF-4); (3) the dependency list on `IDX:96` should gain Perception Filter as a hard dependency for the lie family and Emitter State (for `emitters()`/`beacon_aim`) as soft; (4) Accessibility Settings' list should cite AA-41 to AA-43.

## Tuning Knobs

*Every value below is an Audio-invented `[PLACEHOLDER]` with a proposed registry name, tuned by listening captures and the data-load relations of the Formulas section, not derived from first principles. Levels are dBr (0 dBr = the Witness bus nominal peak). **★** marks a **cross-system fact** that is a registry candidate (another GDD cites it, or its value is bound to another GDD's constant); a knob without ★ is a local Audio tunable in `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json` (AA-44). The "Interacts with" column names the source formula, so each value links to its rationale. Locked constants (`BEAT_*`, `TELL_*`, `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `UNFOLD_LO`, `LIES_MAX`, `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` and others) are listed once at the end and are **not** knobs of this system.*

### A. Tell, release, restore and bed

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RELEASE_TICKS` ★ | 90 | ticks | 30 to 119 (strictly < `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`) | collides with the 120-tick hold; `release_cue_relation` false and the boot refuses | the abort is not heard as a decay | D.3; `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` must equal it; `TELL_REARM_TICKS` |
| `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` | 48 | dB | 36 to 60 | the tail lingers after the web "lost interest" | the bed cuts off audibly | D.3 |
| `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` ★ | 60 | ticks | 0 to 180 (0 = no fade) | the bed is absent for seconds after a load into TELL | the bed pops in on load | D.4; beats unaffected |
| `BED_BASE_DB` | -18 | dBr | -24 to -12 | the bed dominates the mix and fatigues | the bed is masked by tool noise and the margin fails | D.6; `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB`; every `*_CEIL_DB` |
| `TIER_STEP_DB` | 2 | dB | 0 to 4 | reads as a continuous rise (a meter by ear, AT-26) | tiers are not distinguishable | D.6; AA-18 (discrete steps only) |
| `TIER_XFADE_TICKS` | 30 | ticks | 12 to 60 | a tier change is inaudible | a layer pops in | AA-18; `TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS` (cited) |
