## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Rule ids in this section are `AAV-n` (sync rows) and `M-n` (motion rules). Citations: `ART x.y` = the art bible section; `AA-n` = this GDD; neighbour ids as in the Cross-References citation key. Constants are cited by name only. The art-director was consulted for this section (REQUIRED for an Audio system); the art-bible refreshes it implies are listed at the end and are **routed, not applied**.*

### Scope line and sync direction rule

**Audio draws nothing.** It is a PRESENTATION subscriber that writes audio nodes only (AA-3). So this section is not a visual design. It is (1) a **sync table** that pairs every audio cue with the visual twin its neighbour already owns and fixes the ordering and shape constraints between them; (2) the motion rules that bind the pairs; (3) the art bible principles, palette pairings and channel classification; (4) the few visual-side deliverables audio implies. The art-director authors **no new sprite, VFX, shader or light** for this system. Content, mix, bus and triggers remain Audio's (`ART:962`, `ART:210`).

**Sync direction rule (one sentence, binding for every row).** For every event where both senses exist, `audio_start_tick <= visual_start_tick`, because both are requested on the same P5 notification that publishes the fact (AA-13) and the visual never draws first (HR-9, AUD-2, FT-35 "audio may lead"). The reverse (visual before audio) is a defect. A visual that is absent (dark room, nothing lit, no beacon) never weakens an audio cue and an audio cue is never withheld because its visual is absent.

### Events: the sync table (AAV-1 to AAV-39)

Column key. **Twin** = the visual the neighbour owns (rule id). **Channel**: T = truth (SIM-derived, no hallucination may add, remove or alter), C = corruptible (Filter, per viewer). **Slice**: MVP, VS, A (Alpha). A row with "none" in the twin column is deliberate and accessibility-load-bearing where marked **[LB]**.
#### A. Tell layer (`attn`) and the pre-tell cue

| ID | Audio cue (AA) | Visual twin and owner | Sync / constraint | Ch. | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AAV-1 | Pre-tell creak, quantised step 1..`CUE_STEPS` (AA-22) | Attention V2 / AV-6 static stubs at web roots in the lit rim; 3-frame stepped reveal (Attention) | Both derive from the same `ambient_cue_step` (same `CUE_HYST_PM`), so ear and eye never disagree on the step. No lead requirement (neither is a tell). Creak is positional in the **listener's own section**, never at the locus; stubs are never drawn toward a locus. With nothing lit the creak is the only pre-tell channel **[LB]**. Neither may vary continuously with `level_pm`. | T | MVP |
| AAV-2 | Onset carrying beat 0 (AA-14) | V3: first resolve with band-slide (AV-5); presenter draws nothing for an epoch before beat 0 (Attention) | Audio request and visual draw are issued in the same notification; the onset attack transient must land inside the **first stepped frame** of the resolve (<= 5 ticks at 12 fps), never on the last. Audio is never delayed to "match" the 4-frame resolve. | T | MVP |
| AAV-3 | Beat: one unpitched knock per `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` (AA-15) | V4: guaranteed resolve + one dust impulse, band-slide (AV-5, AV-7) | Beat transient on resolve frame 1. At most 2 per second on both senses (`BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS`), so both sit under the 3 Hz ceiling (HR-13 world-side). One beat per poll on both sides; no catch-up on either. A hitch that coalesces beats shows one resolve and plays one knock. | T | MVP |
| AAV-4 | Bed tier layers, crossfade over `TIER_XFADE_TICKS` (AA-18) | V5: drift / twitch / sway motion classes (AV-8) | Both step only at the published `tier` boundary. Audio is **never** the source of any strand motion and no strand motion is driven by bed level. | T | MVP |
| AAV-5 | Positional hull-stress layer at the locus hull anchor, tier >= 2 (AA-17) | **None.** Hull-stress hairlines were cut from the Tell permanently (Attention ruling 3); tell dust direction is never toward the locus (Attention shared-vocabulary note) | Deliberate asymmetry: the visual tell is global and beat-locked, so audio is the **only** localiser of the tell, and only to a section. **[LB]** for a listener who cannot see the rim. ART 2.7 still lists "hull-stress hairlines" under *Creaking*: refresh owed (see Art-bible refreshes and flags, R-1). | T | MVP |
| AAV-6 | Bed (non-positional, every living player) (AA-16) | Strands exist only inside the lit region of a player-controllable emitter (AV-14) | The bed plays when the visual cannot (nothing lit, listener at an unoccupied derelict, listener in a pod). `ART:954` item 15 accepts this: audio is the only non-visual Attention channel. **[LB]** | T | MVP |
| AAV-7 | Abort release: bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS`, release cue (AA-19) | V7: reverse stepped retraction over `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS`; no band-slide, no success glyph (AV-9, AV-11) | **Start ticks equal, lengths equal** (`release_cue_relation`: `RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` and `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS`). At the placeholder `RELEASE_TICKS` 90 ticks and 12 fps the retraction is 18 stepped frames. Neither sense carries a reward cue; the release must not read as a beat on either. | T | MVP |
| AAV-8 | Breach entry = alarm pulse 0, loudness step bounded by `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (AA-20) | V8: snap-taut in `TAUT_SNAP_FRAMES` stepped frames then absolute stillness (AV-10); beacon lens lit at T+1 (Emitter) | Audio at T, taut within the same notification, beacon at T+1: audio leads the beacon by exactly one tick and never trails it. The held rim (AV-10) is a non-red visual phase cue that matches the audio's role on a derelict. | T | MVP |
| AAV-9 | Alarm cadence: one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, duty <= `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM`, aligned to `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` or fallback `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` (AA-20, S3) | Beacon sweep (Alarm Red, Emitter); HUD warning mark pulsing in phase with the beacon at ~50 % duty (ART 7.3, HR-13) | **Period-lock and leading-edge-lock, not coincidence.** The pulse starts on the sweep boundary (the beam's authored phase), not when the red passes the listener's screen. Audio duty (<= 25 % at the placeholder) is shorter than the mark's ~50 %; that is intended, the **leading edge** is the contract. Same rhythm everywhere (`ART:845`): one 2.13 s clock for beacon, mark, `attn` pulse and `ann`. On a derelict (no beacon) the fallback clock carries the breach alone; the held rim and trails are the visual. | T | MVP |
| AAV-10 | Subside: one short collapse, then silence, no "danger over" (AA-21) | V10: beacon off, strands slack then stepped retraction; nothing drawn that says "all clear" (AV-11) | Neither sense signals safety. `phase == CALM` after SUBSIDING plays and draws nothing; trails and failing-loop sounds (`wit`) carry the ongoing state (`AT:1210`). | T | MVP |
| AAV-11 | Restore into any phase: bed fades in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`; no one-shot replays (AA-5) | V12: picture appears already in its phase-correct state, one pop-in allowed (Attention) | Accepted asymmetry: the picture pops, the bed fades. Neither plays onset, snap, release or breach-enter. A paused frame is silent for sim-derived sound and still. | T | MVP |
| AAV-12 | Bed during pod transit unchanged (AA-33, AA-46) | V13: strands continue only in lit regions of eligible kinds (Attention) | Visual may be absent (pod lights are FIXTURE only if authored eligible); the bed is not. **[LB]** | T | MVP |

#### B. Witness (`wit`), running (`run`) and annunciator (`ann`)

| ID | Audio cue | Visual twin and owner | Sync / constraint | Ch. | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AAV-13 | Witness classes HISS, DRIP, HUM_DROP, CLANK, WIND, TICK, SIZZLE, CREAK at the descriptor anchor, from true `intensity_pm` (AA-24) | Failure & Trail E1-E15: mist, frost, scorch, drip, hairline trails (FT) | Same anchor, same Layout source (FT AC-137). Audio may lead; FT never waits (FT-35). The sound class changes at the same descriptor step as the trail medium, never earlier than the descriptor exists. | T | MVP |
| AAV-14 | HISS in an unlit compartment, zero lit emitters (AA-24) | **None, by Principle 1.** Rooms are unlit, trails exist only under light (FT E23) | A hiss with no visible mist is expected, not a bug. **No visual substitute is permitted**: no ripple, ping, radar, glow, outline or "sound marker" in the dark. The audio is the evidence; the player must spend light to see it. **[LB]** | T | MVP |
| AAV-15 | Flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK (AA-24) | 2 px hairline (FT AC-144) that does not resolve at zoom k = 1 | At k = 1 the cue is the only channel; at higher k audio and hairline coexist. Identical at every Filter stage (consequence channel). **[LB]** | T | MVP |
| AAV-16 | DRIP stop / resume; class ends with the descriptor, no completion cue (AA-24; FT E5-E7) | Drip stops; trail relaxes | "The reward is the absence." No relief chime, no "fixed" sound. | T | MVP |
| AAV-17 | Running timbres O2, POWER, COOLANT, FUEL from `served_pm`; INTEGRITY room tone (AA-26) | Rotor, sight-glass, flow chevrons, lamp brightness from `served_pm` (Graph V/A, ART 2.3 "humming") | Both read the same true value, so the **direction of change must agree** (a sagging hum next to a full-speed rotor is a contradiction). No frame-lock between audio rate and chevron speed is required or wanted. The lamp dims honestly and the hum sags; the lamp itself is silent. | T | MVP |
| AAV-18 | HR-9 loop pairs: five loops, distinct running and failing sounds (AA-26 table) | Loop hue + shape grammar (ART 3, 4.7) | Hue is never the only channel (ART 4.0 principle 2); for the four confusable pairs of ART 4.7 the **sound cue** is the named AA-26 distinguishing property (O2 airy-broadband vs COOLANT liquid-tonal; POWER continuous tonal vs FUEL rhythmic feed; FUEL dull irregular vs alarm regular on-grid; Chemlight silent glow vs O2). | T | MVP |
| AAV-19 | Graph alarm annunciator `ann`, only on `alarm_eligible`, never first warning, on the shared grid (AA-27) | HUD warning mark (7.2 glyph, Alarm Red, HR-4: "paired with shape and sound"); Alarm Red sweep | The mark is a **mark, never a word** (HR-4); its sound pair is `ann`. Under reduce-motion (HR-8) the mark holds solid and **the sound carries the alarm**, so `ann` is load-bearing for that player. Under reduce-flashing the audio is unchanged (M1, FT E20/E21). | T | MVP |
| AAV-20 | Cryo wake first fault (the seeded coolant leak, ART 2.2): the descriptor's witness class at the leak anchor | The frost trail from leak to far wall (the tutorial, ART 2.2 carried element) | Mood is "hushed, condensed": no music, no sting; the leak sound must be audible from the start position at its true level so **the ear leads the eye to the source**. The guttering lamp, the dying pod light and every light event in 2.2 are silent. | T | MVP |

#### F. Captions, settings and diagnostics (policy MVP, implementation Alpha)

| ID | Feature | Visual spec | Constraint |
|---|---|---|---|
| AAV-38 | Sound captions (AA-41) | HUD `CanvasLayer` plate, **Ground `#1C2128` opaque**, text Paper White `#DDE4EA` (about 12.6:1), instrument or reading face per 7.1; no family colour, no icon colour; never Alarm Red (HR-4), never violet or flesh (4.6); captions are an anchor (HR-6): not shaken, tinted, scaled or filtered by any Filter state | Captionable families only (`wit`, `ann`, `run`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `ui`). **Never** the tell, breach, pre-tell, Horror, `hal`, `p1c`. Caption appears no earlier than its sound, only for sounds above the listener's audibility floor (never a radar), **no direction arrow or locator** (ART 7.6 item 12 spirit). Names the source precisely ("Hiss", "Coolant pump", "Seam cut") because `hal` ids are disjoint from every captionable family, an uncaptioned `hal` sound never sits beside a captioned twin: the caption set is not a lie oracle. |

Settings UI (Alpha) visual rules: sliders are **linear bars with a needle-mark** (7.3 hold-to-confirm grammar, never radial), chamfer-only corners (3.4), Ground plates (HR-2), no violet, no flesh, no Alarm Red; one World slider; no control displays, sonifies or meters Attention; no waveform, spectrum or "sound radar" in release builds. Debug Overlay voice counters (DIAGNOSTIC, `voice_tier1_steal`) are absent from release and use UI palette only.

### Motion constraints (M-1 to M-8)

- **M-1 Nothing is driven by audio.** No visual parameter (position, scale, brightness, hue, alpha, shake) is a function of any audio level, bus meter, cue start or voice state. Lint: no presenter reads Audio (Attention V14, AV-4; AA-3 inverse). No screen flash, vignette, camera nudge or HUD pulse on a tell beat, strike or breach pulse.
- **M-2 Audio-first is ordering, not delay.** Audio is never postponed to match a stepped visual and no visual is postponed to match an audio tail. Latency figures (device buffer, Windows stack) are a spike (OQ-AA-4); the rule is the shared notification.
- **M-3 One alarm rhythm.** `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 (2.13 s, inside the 2-3 s window of ART 2.8) is the single period for beacon, warning mark, `attn` pulse and `ann`. Nothing luminous or audible alarm-like runs faster; the mark never exceeds 3 Hz (HR-13); no full-frame flash, ever.
- **M-4 Reduce-motion (HR-8) and reduce-flashing.** The sweep becomes a static mark plus HUD glyph and **sound**; trail propagation is never suppressed; neither setting lowers any audio level or removes any cue (M1, FT E20/E21). The held rim (AV-10) is already still.
- **M-5 Stepped visuals, unstepped audio.** Visual twins are stepped at 12 fps (every 5th tick); audio is continuous. Audio events align to the **first stepped frame** of their twin, and audio never imitates the stepping (no 12 Hz tremolo on the bed).
- **M-6 Footfall contact frames.** The crewmate cycles are 8 frames for walk, run and carry-walk (ART 5.2; sheet budget in 5.7), at 12 fps, 5 ticks per frame, consistent with AA-31 (a contact every 4 frames = 20 ticks at every gait; gait is told by level and timbre, never rate). The animator must draw the **contact pose** (lead foot planted) on frames 0 and 4 of every cycle. **Default `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` = {0, 4} for all three gaits**, exported from the Aseprite sidecar as a per-frame flag (Asset implications), not typed in code. Result of the check against ART 5.2 / 5.7: **clean, no conflict** (8-frame cycles confirmed at all three gaits). Mirrored facings reuse frames, so the flag holds for mirrored files; no left/right sound distinction exists.
- **M-7 Honest motion stays silent.** Settle, tremor and the honest digit tick (ART 7.3) have no sound. Glide has no sound of its own: the lie's sound is the `hal` signature (AAV-33), not a "glide whoosh".
- **M-8 Pixel-space invariants.** Nothing audible depends on zoom k in [1, k_auto + 2] or on camera lead; no visual twin changes its audio because a rung changed (restate at every ladder step; pattern 3).

### Palette rules (pairing rules, not hexes)

- **Loop pairing.** `run` and `wit` sounds pair only with their loop's hue and shape in the world: O2 Cyan, Power Amber, Coolant Ice, Fuel Ember, Hull Grey (integrity). A witness sound never pairs with a hue that is not its loop's. Confusable pairs are separated in sound by AA-26's property for each pair (ART 4.7) in addition to the shape backups.
- **Alarm Red pairing.** Only `ann` and the `attn` breach pulses pair with Alarm Red (the sweep and the failing-instrument warning mark). No other sound is associated with red. Alarm Red is never caption text (HR-4).
- **Sane-palette channel (violet and flesh).** `hal` is the **only** audio family that pairs with violet or flesh visuals. No TRUE family's visual twin may carry either hue, and no caption, settings control or audio UI element may carry either (4.6 divergence; the only violet that reaches screen space is a lie's gleam on a reading's glass).
- **Chemlight.** `#86B896` glow is silent; sounds belong to throw / land / wane and never to the light.
- **Tungsten / human light.** Light is silent. Lamp hum is the machine's (EM-14, `LO:429`), never a lamp sound; a crewmate's beam has no sound, so the human channel carries no audio of its own beyond foley and voice.
- **Luminance ladder analogue.** The ear's analogue of the luminance ladder (ART 4.5) is the band plan (AA-45): the tell bed low, the POWER hum above it, `hal` in its reserved narrow band that no one else may occupy. Treat it as the audio backstop in the same sense: not the primary channel, the thing that survives when the primary one collapses.
- **Depth never changes hue; depth never changes the tell contract.** Ring depth may shift only pre-tell creak density (VS), never a sound's hue pairing.
- **Verification.** The existing visual sampling tests (for example lit steel hue >= 36 degrees, ART 4.4) are unchanged by audio; audio adds only the two blind tests: hue-removed and audio-removed identification of the lying instrument (HR-5), and the achromatopsia test for the below-threshold crewmate (HR-7).

### Truth channel and corruptible channels

| Visual or audio | Class | Note |
|---|---|---|
| All `attn`, `wit`, `run`, `ann`, `hor`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `p1c`, voice, `amb`, `ui` audio | **T** | Identical at every sanity stage and Filter state (AA-38) |
| Attention strands, stubs, band-slide, taut hold, release, beacon | **T** | Matches Attention's own classification table ("the bed, beats, release, alarm: T") |
| F&T trails, Graph-driven lamp brightness, rotor / chevrons | **T** | Consequence channel |
| Horror rig, marks, rime, hairlines, L3 dips | **T** | Only per-viewer omission of the body (VS) and phantom / class-(c) rigs are C |
| Violet gleam, glide, continuous tick on a lying reading | **C** | Filter, per viewer |
| `hal` reading-lie sound (`FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE`, `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE`) | **C (PERCEIVED)** | Exactly one family; always with an audible leak |
| Class-(c) violet fringe | Filter-drawn, marks a true state | Honest marker; paired with the **T** P1 cue |
| Captions | **T** (name only true sounds) | Never name a `hal` sound |

**Designed mismatch rows (not defects).** (1) Hidden real horror: body C (omitted), sound T. (2) Lit locus: the visual tell is global, the positional hull-stress layer localises to a section. (3) Dark room: sound T, no visual by Principle 1. (4) Fringe vs P1 cue: one passes through the Filter, one does not. (5) Picture pops on restore, bed fades.

### Asset implications (specifications, no pixels, no new sprites)

1. **Foot-contact flag in the character sidecar (the only visual-side deliverable).** In `char_crew_body_alb.png`'s Aseprite export (8.5 sidecars), tag frames 0 and 4 of `walk_<facing>`, `run_<facing>` and `carry_<facing>` with a per-frame user-data flag `foot`; `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` is **exported, not typed** (same rule as 8.5's per-frame offset table). Owner: art-director (animation) with the Controller.
2. **No new sprites, sheets, shaders, lights or VFX** are created by this system. Explicitly absent: no sound-visualiser, ripple, ping, subtitle-for-tell, rumble icon, waveform or meter.
3. **Alpha UI parts (implied, not scheduled):** caption plate (reuse the `ui_frame_*` Ground plate family; e.g. `ui_frame_caption.png`), settings slider (linear bar plus needle-mark; e.g. `ui_frame_slider.png`), all in the 4.6 palette and the one UI `Theme`.
4. **Audio file naming (conformance note).** AA-6's `sfx_<token>_<context>_<name>_<variant>` is a second deliberate deviation from the project's generic `[category]_[name]_[variant]_[size].[ext]` (8.5 documents the first, for visual sheets, where the trailing slot is the map suffix). For audio the trailing slot is a two-digit variant index. It is snake_case and lowercase, so it fits `naming.files`. `sfx` / `mus` / `amb` / `ui` / `vox` are not in 8.5's `cat` table (visual categories only). Recommend recording an audio addendum beside 8.5; do not block.
5. **Evidence rig (QA, not game asset).** Tick-stamped capture of audio-request tick versus first visual draw tick for the sync table, stored in `production/qa/evidence/`.

### Accessibility and reduced-effect behaviour

- **Reduce-motion (HR-8) and reduce-flashing** change nothing audible: no level, cue or rhythm is reduced (M1, FT E20 and E21). Under reduce-motion the warning mark holds solid and the sound carries the alarm, so `ann` and the `attn` pulse are load-bearing for that player (AAV-19).
- **Low vision.** The tell is audible through the bed and the beats alone (AA-15, AA-16); the dark-room hiss, the flow-zero TICK and the seam-cut loop are audio-only channels by design (AAV-5, AAV-14, AAV-15, AAV-31).
- **Deaf and hard of hearing.** The tell channel is the visual rim alone, the accepted cost of the standing decision that no Attention indicator exists in any form (`ART:954` item 15; OQ-AA-15). Captions name only true, captionable sounds and never the tell, breach, pre-tell creaks, Horror or `hal` (AAV-38).
- **Mono and small speakers.** The bed keeps an upper-partial edge layer so it survives a laptop speaker and a mono downmix (AA-16, AA-45); mono never removes the bed (AC-AA-92).
- **No tactile channel** at MVP (AA-42; OQ-AA-2).
- **Colour.** No audio-bearing visual relies on hue alone (ART 4.0 principle 2); the four confusable loop pairs of ART 4.7 are separated by the AA-26 sound properties in addition to shape.

### Engine feasibility notes

- Audio is engine nodes only; nothing here is drawn, so no shader, light or particle cost is added. The Godot audio reference has no scripting or core file; the relevant file is `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/audio.md`, and the listener policy under the 640x360 world SubViewport at integer scale is a spike (AC-AA-81, OQ-AA-1) with the manual pan-and-gain mix as the named fallback (-> ADR candidate).
- `AudioEffectSpectrumAnalyzer.tap_back_pos` is removed in the pinned version: the tell bus is never probed by spectrum for gameplay, and the band lint reads import-time tags (AA-45, OQ-AA-5).
- `Area2D` `audio_bus_override` zones are not used (the 4.7 `area_mask` default 0, D.9).

- R-1 (RF-12). **ART 2.7** *Atmospheric descriptors* lists "Creaking (visually: hull-stress hairlines...)". Hairlines were cut permanently by Attention; creaking is now carried by the audio positional layer (AAV-5). Reword.

- R-4 (RF-15). **ART 4.7** Chemlight vs O2 row: point the "sound cue" backup at AA-30 (throw, land, wane; glow silent) and the four named pairs at AA-26.
- R-5 (RF-16). **ART 7.3 / 7.6 item 15**: record that under reduce-motion `ann` becomes the carrier of the alarm and under `ART:954` the tell is audio-only for a low-vision player and visual-only for a deaf one (already stated; add the cross-reference to AA-41).

**Flags (each routed to an Open Question or discharged; F1 and F8 need no action):**
- F1. **ART:962 hand-off** is discharged in the hand-off table above; the only item still open is "heard by its own source" (game-designer).
- F2. **8.5 naming deviation** (R-2).
- F3. **ART 2.10 death dim-out vs AA-5 / AA-34.** Question for Lighting and Save/Load: is the dim-out sim-driven (then `run` sags and ends as a true consequence, nothing added) or a presentation overlay (then sound hard-stops at the cut)? Either is compatible with "death plays no sound"; the GDD should state which. Not a conflict.
- F4. **Idle pack breathing (ART 5.2) vs audible breath cadence.** The animation is fixed-rate (cannot carry state, ART 2.9). Suit may want a laboured-breath pose variant (ART 5.3 honest body states); not required by audio. Open question to Suit and art.
- F5. **`hal` breathing phase.** Recommend deriving from the Filter's lie-time; confirm with the Filter GDD (PF-20 / PFV-15). Optional phase-lock.
- F6. **Alarm duty versus mark duty.** `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` placeholder (250) vs the mark's ~50 %: the contract is the leading edge (AAV-9); confirm the accessibility-specialist agrees (HR-13).
- F7. **Caption locator.** No direction arrow, no off-screen indicator for captions (ART 7.6 item 12 spirit; `HE:1342-1360`); accessibility-specialist may ask for one: it would conflict with the standing decisions for horror and tell sounds, so raise as a trade-off, not an addition.
- F8. **5.7 cycle-length check: CLEAN.** Walk, run and carry-walk are 8-frame cycles; the AA-31 default {0, 4} is consistent. No conflict to surface.
- F9. **Phase-locked sound for the fringe (class c, VS-B)** is not needed; the three modes are independent legs.

### Acceptance mapping

The art-director's ten acceptance proposals are folded into the Acceptance Criteria, not duplicated: AC-AAV-1 is AC-AA-110; AC-AAV-2 is AC-AA-118; AC-AAV-3 is AC-AA-119 (and AC-AA-35); AC-AAV-4 is AC-AA-120 (and AC-AA-27); AC-AAV-5 is AC-AA-121; AC-AAV-6 is AC-AA-28 and AC-AA-58; AC-AAV-7 is AC-AA-122; AC-AAV-8 is AC-AA-123; AC-AAV-9 is AC-AA-34; AC-AAV-10 is AC-AA-124 (and AC-AA-107).

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. This system draws nothing and authors no sprite, sheet, shader, light or VFX; its asset surface is audio only (OGG Vorbis, identity-table names `sfx_<token>_<context>_<name>_<variant>`, AA-6 and AA-44) plus one animation-sidecar flag (`foot` on frames 0 and 4 of the walk, run and carry cycles). **Preconditions:** the art bible must be approved and its audio naming addendum (RF-13) and the 2.7 *Creaking* refresh (RF-12) must have landed. Then run `/asset-spec system:adaptive-audio-and-tells` to produce the per-asset descriptions and generation prompts from this section and the identity table.

## Game Feel

*Scope note.* Audio has no verb of its own: the player never "operates" it, so the feel being specified is **the weight of the tell, the honesty of the ship's voice, and the quiet that follows restraint**. The player's body, view and controls never react to a sound: nothing about the player's character panics, the camera does not shake, the controller does not rumble (AA-42), the HUD does not pulse. If a tester says "my character got scared", the feel is wrong; the *ship* changes, the player does not (`PCI:135`, `PIPE:151`). Latencies are given at `TICK_RATE` 60 (one tick is 16.7 ms); the sim-side numbers are cited from the owners, the audio-side numbers are `[PLACEHOLDER]` values from the Tuning Knobs. **Who owns which feel:** the *tell's* dread curve and the *breach's* silence are Attention's design (Attention Game Feel owns the visual rim and the 10 to 30 s arc) and Audio owns how it sounds; the *loop and failure* feel is the Graph's and Failure & Trail's, and Audio owns the ear on it; the *own-footstep* feel is the Controller's (PC-11) and Audio owns the sound; the *channel bar and result* feel is Interaction's; the *lie's leak* feel is Sanity's and the Filter's, and Audio owns the signature. This GDD owns the sound of each, the cue's timing against its visual twin, and the rule that nothing is announced that did not happen.

### Feel Reference

*Borrowed.* Subnautica's distant leviathan call: heard everywhere, located nowhere, which is the structure-borne bed (AA-16: non-positional, every living player). Alien: Isolation's vent knock before the drop: the building announces the threat before the screen does, which is the beat that arrives ahead of the first filament (AA-13). Pandorum's groaning hull and Aliens' dripping-pipe silence: the ship has a voice that is a physical fact, not a score. Project Zomboid's own-noise radius: the player is the cause of the danger and the game lets them hear it, which is the footstep as the only noise readout (AA-31). *Anti-reference.* Dead Space's and Resident Evil 7's stingers, heartbeat and breath-rate sanity cues, and Darkest Dungeon's narrator: no sound tells the player a number about themselves, no sound scares for its own sake, and no sound congratulates. A music cue that rises before the first beat, a "danger over" chime and a proximity stinger are each a defect (AA-23, AA-21, AA-29).

### Input Responsiveness

Audio has no input of its own; the rows are the **latency from a published fact (or a player action) to its sound**. "Request" means the cue request created in the handler of the P5 notification that publishes the fact; "play" means the engine call in the same frame. The device-buffer and Windows-stack delay on top is a measured spike (AC-AA-6), not a rule.

| Event | Max latency, fact to request | Budget | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tell onset carrying beat 0 | 0 ticks (same notification as beat 0) | before the first filament, never after | AA-13, AA-14, AUD-2 |
| Tell beat | 0 ticks (same poll as the published pair) | at most one per poll, no catch-up | AA-4; the poll interval above 500 ms can coalesce beats, accepted |
| Breach entry (pulse 0) | 0 ticks | at `breach_enter_T`, one tick ahead of the beacon lens | AA-20, AAV-8 |
| Strike cue | at most 1 tick (17 ms) from `strike_T` | the request is created in the notification handler | `HE:1285`; AA-13 |
| Witness class at a new descriptor | the poll that sees it (the Graph publishes every 6 ticks) | never delayed to the alarm grid | AA-25, AA-27 |
| Own footfall | the tick the animation presents a contact frame (every 20 ticks at every gait) | no step on idle, work, stagger, down or a blocked move | AA-31, M-6 |
| Interaction terminal cue (done, cancel, abort, nothing) | same notification as the result | only the terminal transition plays | AA-32; the press pre-play is the one cue allowed before a result |
| Hallucination signature ON | the first ACTIVE frame of the lie | audible within `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` (100 ms) of the first ACTIVE frame; before the glide is visible (`t_glide_visible` 0.125 to 0.83 s) | PF-12, AA-36 |
| Device output latency (play to first sample) | measured, p95 recorded | `[PLACEHOLDER]` target 50 ms | AC-AA-6; OQ-AA-4 |

### Animation Feel Targets

Audio has no animations; the equivalent is the **envelope** of each cue class, in ticks at `TICK_RATE` 60, set against the stepped 12 fps visual twin (one frame is 5 ticks). Audio is unstepped (M-5) and never imitates the stepping.

| Cue | Attack / startup | Sustain / active | Release / tail | Feel goal | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tell beat | transient inside the first 5 ticks | one unpitched knock | tail at most `BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` (60) | a structural knock, countable, never a drum | spaced `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (30) or more apart |
| Tell onset (beat 0) | as a beat | as a beat | as a beat | the first knock is the news | lands in the first stepped frame of the resolve (AAV-2) |
| Bed tier step | `TIER_XFADE_TICKS` (30) crossfade | `TIER_STEP_DB` (2) louder per step | n/a | heavier, not faster; steps, never a ramp | tier edges `TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS` |
| Abort release | none | none | `RELEASE_TICKS` (90), bed down `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` (48) | the web losing interest; relief without reward | equals `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` |
| Breach entry and alarm pulse | step at most `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (8 LU) | pulse at most 32 ticks (`ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` 250 of a 128-tick sweep) | short | firm and rhythmic, not a jump scare | one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` (128) |
| Subside | one short collapse | none | then silence | the pressure lets go, nothing is declared safe | AA-21 |
| REACH lead-in | builds over `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` (90) | no impact | ends at `reach_end_T` | a telegraph that asserts nothing the picture does not | fizzle decays quietly |
| Strike | at `strike_T` | single hit | short | the witness of strike one; no kill stinger | AA-28 c |
| Footfall | at the contact frame | one step | short | weight by level and timbre, not by rate | -9, -3, -9 dBr walk, run, carry |
| Ducking | `DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS` (6) | `DUCK_HOLD_TICKS` (24) | `DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` (30) | the beat cuts through without pumping | tier 4 and below only |
| Restore fade-in | `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` (60) | steady level | n/a | the bed returns, nothing replays | sustained cues only |
| Hallucination signature | starts on the first ACTIVE frame at its D.15 level, de-click `DECLICK_TICKS` only; audible within `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` | breathing at `HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS` (300) | `HAL_FADE_TICKS` (24) fade out at CLEAR, no sting | a sound no machine makes, never a sting | AA-35 |
| Berth bed | `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` (60) fade in at the edge into BERTHING | steady drive hum and hull strain, no rhythm | `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` (60) fade out at BERTHED, then the contact cue | the crossing is witnessed, not shown; never an ETA | AA-33 (g), D.17 |

### Impact Moments

The punctuation of this system is **silence and restraint as much as sound**.

| Impact type | Duration | Effect description | Configurable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tell onset | one beat, then the bed from tier 1 | the first knock from nowhere, before anything on screen changes | knobs of Attention (beat schedule) and Audio (`BED_BASE_DB`) |
| Abort release | `RELEASE_TICKS` (90) | the bed sags and lets go; the quiet after it is the reward | yes, with the `release_cue_relation` bound |
| Breach entry | one event, then the cadence | a firm, bounded step into rhythm; the entry is itself pulse 0 | `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` |
| The silence after a breach | the SUBSIDING-to-CALM interval | one short collapse, then nothing declares safety | not configurable by design (AA-21) |
| Death and load | none | **no sound**: no sting, no loss tone, no re-wake chime | not configurable by design (AA-34) |
| Hit-stop, screen shake, camera impact, time-scale | none | none; no audio-driven camera or time effect exists (M-1) | no |
| Controller rumble | none | none; a tell-synced rumble is an Attention indicator in effect (AA-42) | OQ-AA-2 (user) |
| Strike | one hit | the witness of strike one; consequence is shown by the Suit and the hull, not by the sound | no |

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight.** Heavy and slow at the tell, flat and dry at the witness. The bed and the beats are low and structural; the Graph's sounds are small, specific and physical (a hiss, a drip, a clank, a hum that sags). Nothing is bright, nothing is musical, and nothing is louder because it matters more than the thing it points at.
- **Player control.** Total over the cause, none over the sound. The player controls the world's sound by what they do (light, power, noise, restraint) and never by a setting that lowers the tell (no setting lowers the bed below its margin, AA-43). Going still is the only control, and the release is its answer.
- **Snap quality.** Crisp and binary at the events (a beat is a knock, a pulse is a pulse), smooth and analog at the true values (a loop's pitch and level follow `served_pm`, a leak's level follows `iota`), and stepped, never ramped, at the tier and creak-density steps so that no sound is a continuous meter.
- **Acceleration model.** Simulation, not arcade. Cues do not swell for effect; beats tighten because the schedule tightens, a loop sags because the node sags, and the slews (`RUN_SLEW_TICKS`, `SLEW_TICKS`) only de-zipper the Graph's 10 Hz publish.
- **Failure texture.** Fair and legible. A player who is caught was told: the knocks were audible with the screen dark, the pre-tell creaks were stepping up, the failing loop was sounding at its place, and their own run was louder than a walk. The read on why they failed is always a sound they could have acted on; the game never hides a consequence by sound and never invents one (P1).

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A tester playing audio-only (video hidden) says the hull is knocking and counts the beats before anything on screen changes (AC-AA-4).
- [ ] No tester describes the abort as a reward or a victory; most describe relief or the thing losing interest (AC-AA-12).
- [ ] The breach entry is not reported as a jump scare and its loudness step is within `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (AC-AA-14).
- [ ] A tester with the lamp off points to a seam weep's side before lighting the lamp, and the mist is where the ear said (AC-AA-36).
- [ ] Testers name their own footsteps, never a meter, as what told them they were louder (AC-AA-52).
- [ ] A panel told nothing about the signature identifies the lying instrument from sound alone at 90 per cent or better (AC-AA-58).
- [ ] No tester says they could tell how close a monster was, how much attention they had, or how sane they were, by sound (Player Fantasy, "We will know this was right if").
- [ ] No tester reports the death or the load as accompanied by a sound, and no tester reports a "danger over" cue (AC-AA-124).

## UI Requirements

**No UI of its own at MVP, by decision.** Adaptive Audio & Tells ships no HUD element, no screen, no menu, no world-space icon, no meter and no instrument: it draws nothing (AA-3, the Visual/Audio Requirements scope line). The art bible forbids an Attention indicator in any form (`ART:741`, 7.6 item 15 at `ART:954`) and Pillar 1 makes the pipe the player reads, so no surface may display or sonify the Attention level, the sanity stage or a Horror's proximity. There is accordingly **no UX Flag** at MVP and no per-screen UI evidence is owed; AC-AA-118 (no audio-driven visual) and AC-AA-75 (settings boundaries, review) stand in for the UI gate. Two surfaces are nonetheless *required by this GDD's policy* and belong to neighbours who do not yet exist (Alpha): the sound captions (AA-41) and the audio settings (AA-43). The table lists each surface, its owner and the constraint Audio places on it; those owners must repeat the constraints or surface a CONFLICT.

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition | Owner and constraint |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The attention level, the tell's progress, beat count, tier, time left | **Nowhere.** The world carries it (the bed, the beats, the rim) | n/a | n/a | No caption, no meter, no waveform, no "sound radar" in any release build; `tell_remaining` is never read by a PRESENTATION subscriber (AC-AA-19a) |
| Sound captions *(Alpha)* | HUD `CanvasLayer` plate, Ground `#1C2128` opaque, Paper White `#DDE4EA` text, no family colour, no icon colour, no direction arrow | on the caption's sound start, no earlier | a captionable family only, above the listener's audibility floor | **HUD & Suit Layer** designed (hud-and-suit-layer.md) **/ Accessibility Settings** `(provisional — undesigned)`. Captionable: `wit`, `ann`, `run`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia` terminals, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `ui`. Never the tell, breach, alarm cadence, pre-tell creaks, Horror, `hal` or `p1c`. Names the source precisely ("Hiss", "Coolant pump", "Seam cut") so the caption set is not a lie oracle; never Alarm Red, violet or flesh (HR-4, ART 4.6) |
| Audio settings *(Alpha)*: master, World, Ambience, UI, Voice, mono, caption toggle | the settings screen: linear bars with a needle-mark (ART 7.3), chamfer-only corners (ART 3.4), Ground plates, one UI `Theme` | on change | always | **Settings & Input Rebinding / Accessibility Settings** `(provisional — undesigned)`. One World slider covers the 13 tokens of AA-43; the Ambience slider covers `amb` only; no setting lowers the bed below its margin; mono keeps the bed; reduce-flashing and reduce-motion never reduce audio |
| Own-run caption | as the caption plate | on the run sound | provisional | Controller `PCI:821` asks for it; AA-41 treats own-action foley as provisionally captionable (CX-A7, RF-7) |
| Diagnostics: voice counters, `voice_tier1_steal`, the request log, bus meters | the **Debug Overlay** only | per frame, development builds | development only | **Debug Overlay (True Values)** designed (debug-overlay-true-values.md); absent from release builds; UI palette only; the one place audio values may be shown |
| Save and load, death | none from Audio | n/a | n/a | No sound and no indicator (AA-34); Save/Load owns the gate |
| Voice and ping surfaces *(VS-B)* | the Voice bus; a ping is a UI cue | on use | co-op only | **Chat/Voice/Pings** and **Co-op Session** `(provisional — undesigned)`; the voice channel is never processed by sanity, stage or the Filter (AA-39) |

*Stored for display?* Nothing. Audio persists nothing and replicates nothing (AA-3). *Neighbouring UI that must not absorb Audio:* HUD & Suit Layer (the chest-lamp state and suit vitals are the player's own, never the web's), In-World Instruments (a needle moves only when the ship's loop moves; the tell never moves one), Chart Table (depth rings are place, not attention). Each of those GDDs must repeat the no-indicator rule or surface a CONFLICT. When the Accessibility Settings GDD is authored, run `/ux-design accessibility` against AA-41 to AA-43 and AAV-38; no UX flag is raised now because the surfaces are not this system's to design.

`STRIDE_WALK`, `STRIDE_RUN`, `STRIDE_CARRY` are Controller values cited by PC-11), `P1_CUE_PERIOD_TICKS`, `CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS`, `R_AUDIBLE_PX` (the class table, with its derived bound `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX`), `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB`, `AUDIBLE_CULL_DB`, `WIT_VOICE_CAP`, `BED_BASE_DB`; the full tuning list is Tuning Knobs, where the **★ rows** are the cross-system candidates (`RELEASE_TICKS`, `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`, `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB`, `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM`, `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX`, `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS`, `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS`, `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES`, `VOICE_POOL_SIZE`, `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES`). *Formulas:* `release_cue_relation` (`RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS AND STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS`, registered as a **formula**, not two constants), `alarm_pulse_phase` (D.2), `cue_dedupe` (D.1), `reach_resolve_start` (a non-percussive lead-in; the impact is the strike cue) with its constraint `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO` (D.11), `transmission_attenuation` (D.9), `voice_alloc` (D.10), `hal_level` with its trough relation (D.15), `bed_margin` (D.6), `breach_entry_trim` (D.5), `restore_fade` (D.4), `witness_level` with its floors relation (D.8), `footfall_cadence` (D.14), `creak_predicate` (D.13), `leg_bed_envelope` (D.17), `bed_ducking` (D.18), and the identity table as a registry-tracked list `audio_families`. *Events:* none: Audio names cue ids, not events. *Existing rows that gain `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md` in `referenced_by`:* `sanity_view`, `horror_t01`, `horror.view`, `SAN_TELL_ON`, `UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI`, the `BODY_TRANSFER` family, `outcome_cue_gate`, `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS`, `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, `OUTPUT_DIRTY_QUANTUM_PM`, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`, `CUE_STEPS`, `TELL_REARM_TICKS`, `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR`, `LIES_MAX`. *Not registry rows today (cited by rule, not by name):* `NOISE_WALK`, `NOISE_RUN`, `NOISE_CARRY` (Controller), `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` (Attention).

## Acceptance Criteria

*Ids are `AC-AA-n`. Rule ids are `AA-n` (Detailed Design), formulas `D.n`, edge cases `E-AA-n`, lints `L-n` (AA-47), Visual/Audio rows `AAV-n`; a neighbour's id is written with its prefix (`AC-AT-47`, `AC-HE-71`, `LSG:1110`). Section A to I are the QA-lead-reviewed first set; section J holds the criteria the review added for rules, formulas and edge cases that the first set left open, and section K holds the Visual/Audio sync and policy criteria. Ids are not renumbered, so a reader holding the review can follow every change.*

**Tags.** `(auto)` a headless test with the dummy audio driver that asserts **cue requests**, which are pure functions of published state (AA-3); it lives in `tests/unit/audio/`, or in `tests/integration/audio/` when tagged `integration`. `(lint)` a build-time, data-load or source lint, run in the same CI job as the unit tests. `(review)` a document or discharge review that produces a checklist, not a build artefact. `(manual)` a human listening test with a **retained capture**: the file in `production/qa/evidence/` carries the criterion id and the date in its name, plus a result sheet (tester count, trials, outcome); a capture that was made and discarded is an assertion, not evidence. `(spike)` a measurement whose recorded result decides an ADR; it closes with the result on disk, not with a pass or a fail, except where a threshold is stated. Modifiers: `provisional PF-20` (the criterion depends on the Perception Filter's request transport, which is on disk and headed `Designed` but whose Audio rows are re-checked at the X-6 confirming scan), `provisional <neighbour>` (a read or an event that the owner has not yet confirmed), `VS-B dormant` (contract only at MVP, excluded from the MVP gate), `Alpha` (the surface does not exist at MVP). Every audibility claim is split into a headless **trigger** test and a manual **audible** test; the first proves the request exists, the second proves it can be heard.

**Value convention.** A criterion names a registry constant and gives its value "at time of writing" in parentheses at first use. The test fixture reads the registry or `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json` as the source of truth, so a retune cannot leave a stale number in a test; restating the same value here is allowed, a different value is a CONFLICT. Level comparisons use a tolerance of 0.01 dB and the two-decimal values of the Formulas section; pitch comparisons use 0.1 cent; tick comparisons are exact.

**Manual-test panel protocol (applies to every `(manual)` criterion).** The solo developer knows every sound and cannot be the only panellist. A closing panel is at least 5 testers who have not seen the asset list, none of them the audio author; every blind identification pools at least 50 trials across the panel and a percentage threshold applies to the pooled trials; a count threshold ("at least 4 of 5 testers") applies per tester. A fallback panel of 3 is allowed at a pre-alpha checkpoint only and is marked "indicative, not closing" in the result sheet. Loudness is measured as K-weighted momentary loudness (400 ms) on the retained capture, band levels as band-limited RMS of the same capture. Whether a panel of this size is feasible for a solo first-time developer is an open question (OQ-AA-13).

**Evidence classes.** Logic and integration criteria are BLOCKING at the workflow's `full` tier. Every `(manual)` listening criterion is a Feel-class check whose retained capture lives in `production/qa/evidence/` and is not waived at `qa.level: minimal`; whether an audio capture counts as the Visual/Feel evidence that the testing standard words in terms of screenshots is an open question (OQ-AA-14). Config and data checks are advisory smoke checks except the load-time refusals listed here as lints, which block the boot.

### A. Cue delivery, the tell and the abort (P0)

- **AC-AA-1 (auto)** GIVEN the 40-row fixture `tests/unit/audio/fixtures/d1_classifier_rows.json` (columns `seen_e, seen_b, seen_ph, now_e, now_b, now_ph, out, restore, expected_event`; the rows cover new epoch, same epoch higher beat, same epoch same beat, TELL to CALM with ABORTED, TELL to BREACH, CALM to BREACH, BREACH to SUBSIDING, SUBSIDING to CALM, a restore, a hitch that skips beats, and a breach with a stale higher beat), WHEN each row is polled, THEN the produced event equals `expected_event` (none or exactly one of ONSET, BEAT, RELEASE, BREACH_ENTER, SUBSIDE), no row produces two events (the D.1 rows are mutually exclusive on the published state), and `seen == now` after every poll. [AA-4, D.1, AUD-3]
- **AC-AA-2 (auto)** GIVEN `tell_epoch` 7 entering TELL at `T` 12000 and a 3-second hitch that publishes `(7, 2)` and `(7, 3)`, WHEN the next poll runs, THEN exactly one BEAT request (for beat 3) is made, beat 2 is never requested later, and `seen == (7, 3)`; GIVEN `seen == (7, 9)` and a poll of `(8, 0)`, THEN ONSET is requested. [AA-4, D.1, AC-AT-115]
- **AC-AA-3a (auto)** GIVEN the closed integer grid that Attention's tell-window fixture publishes (`tell_len` from `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (600) to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` (1800) in steps of 6 ticks, crossed with 32 epoch seeds at `BEAT_JITTER_TICKS` (8, nominal) and at jitter 0; the fixture is copied from Attention's `tell_window_contract` test and re-read at the X-6 scan), WHEN a tell is published and not aborted and the poll runs at 1-tick intervals, THEN the BEAT-plus-ONSET request count in the first `TELL_MIN_TICKS` is at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` (4) and beat 0 is the onset (no second beat-0 request). [AA-14, AA-15, AUD-5]
- **AC-AA-3b (auto)** GIVEN the same grid and polls at 1-tick intervals, WHEN every published beat is counted, THEN the number of BEAT requests equals the number of published beats after beat 0 and none is suppressed before `breach_due_T`. [AA-15, AUD-3]
- **AC-AA-4 (manual)** GIVEN a 600-tick armed tell captured audio-only at nominal frame rate with the screen hidden, WHEN each tester is asked the open question "what is happening to the ship?" and then asked to count the knocks, THEN every tester counts at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` (4) knocks and at least 4 of 5 testers describe the event as structural or hull-borne rather than as a machine or a monster; the audio-only capture and result sheet are retained (the audible half of AC-AT-47). [AA-15, AC-AT-47]
- **AC-AA-5 (auto, integration)** GIVEN an onset and a presenter stub, WHEN the `attention.step` run that publishes beat 0 notifies, THEN the onset request is created in that notification handler and the stub draws no filament for the epoch before the request exists (an ordering guarantee, not a latency). [AA-13, AA-14, AUD-2, AC-AT-24]
- **AC-AA-6 (spike)** GIVEN the shipped audio stack on a Windows reference machine, WHEN the delay from `play()` to the first audible sample, from beat 0 to the first filament and from `strike_T` to the strike cue are measured over 200 events, THEN the p95 values are recorded in `production/qa/evidence/` and OQ-AA-4 is answered; no rule changes without the result. [AA-13, HE:1285]
- **AC-AA-7 (auto)** GIVEN a tell at tier 1, 2 and 3 (`TIER2_TICKS` 600, `TIER3_TICKS` 1200), WHEN each tier is entered, THEN the bed layers change only at the tier step (crossfade `TIER_XFADE_TICKS`, `TIER_STEP_DB` 2 dB per step), the positional hull-stress layer is requested at tier 2 and 3 at the `hull_anchor` of `locus_section_id` and not at tier 1, and no request parameter varies continuously with `tell_elapsed`. [AA-17, AA-18]
- **AC-AA-8 (manual)** GIVEN the worst allowed concurrent state (`RUN_MAX_VOICES` (4) running loops at `RUN_CEIL_DB`, a TOOL-class loop, the ambience, the Suit fan, the berth bed, the INTEGRITY room tone (NORMAL band) and `WIT_VOICE_CAP` sustained witness classes), WHEN the tier-1 bed plays on headphones and on laptop speakers and the capture is analysed as band-limited RMS over the two bed bands (about 30 to 120 Hz and 200 to 400 Hz) with the bed alone and with the concurrent set alone, THEN the bed exceeds the set by at least `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` (6) in both bands, and the beats alone remain countable with the bed removed (the same panel and count as AC-AA-4); the capture, the two band levels and the count are retained. [AA-16, D.6, M2]
- **AC-AA-9 (lint)** GIVEN the knob set, WHEN the data load computes `L_nc,b` for the worst concurrent set, THEN `BED_BASE_DB - L_nc,b >= TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` for both bed bands (the D.6 example gives 7.95 dB at tier 1 and 11.95 dB at tier 3, with the berth bed and the INTEGRITY room tone in the set); a `BED_BASE_DB` of -24 gives 1.95 dB and the load is refused; a POWER hum authored at 100 Hz in `B_LOW` at -30 dBr gives 6.51 dB (passes the margin) and fails the band lint because its fundamental is below `POWER_HUM_MIN_HZ` (160). - **AC-AA-15 (auto)** GIVEN pre-tell creaks at steps 0 to 4, WHEN 360,000 CALM ticks (100 simulated minutes) are run per step with one poll per tick and a fixed `HASH_SEED`, THEN step 0 emits none; step s emits no two creaks closer than `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]`; at most one creak starts per poll; the mean spacing is within 15 per cent of `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` (about 200 creaks at step 1, so the standard error is about 5 per cent); the creak plate is in the listener's own section; the creak parameters (gain, pitch, rate) are identical at every step; and no creak starts in BREACH or SUBSIDING. ### E. Hallucination family and the human channel (P4)

- **AC-AA-54 (auto)** GIVEN 200 random published-state fixtures and sanity stages 0 to 3, WHEN request lists are produced, THEN the list of every TRUE family equals the stage-0 list bit for bit; only the `hal` list differs, by Filter requests. [AA-38, E-AA-46, SN-27]
- **AC-AA-55 (auto, provisional PF-20)** GIVEN a Filter request with and without SOUND in its leak modes, and `start_T` 40998 against a glide at 41000, WHEN processed, THEN a request without SOUND makes no `hal` request, `start_T <= glide_start_T`, an OFF at CLEAR fades over `HAL_FADE_TICKS` (24) and expired, debunked and invalidated lies produce the same request sequence with no sting. [AA-35, AA-36, D.15, PF-12, E-AA-47, E-AA-50]
- **AC-AA-56 (lint)** GIVEN the identity table, WHEN linted, THEN every `hal` id is in `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES`, no non-`hal` asset carries the `leak` tag, the reserved fringe band is occupied by `hal` only, no `hal` asset is bound to a non-`hal` bus, and no `hal` request can modify, delay, duck, steal or remove another family's request. [AA-6 c, f, g; AA-37; L-3, L-4, L-5]
- **AC-AA-57 (auto, provisional PF-20)** GIVEN `HAL_FLOOR_DB` -42, `HAL_CEIL_DB` -18, `HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM` 400 and `leak_amplitude_pm` 400, WHEN D.15 runs, THEN `L_peak` is -32.4 dBr and the trough -36.8 dBr; `m(t)` is 1.0 at t 0, 0.6 at half a period and 1.0 at a full period; the level follows `leak_amplitude_pm` through RETURN to the ACTIVE minimum; both data relations hold (`HAL_BUS_REL_DB` and the trough relation); and a depth of 700 (-42 + 20 log10(0.3) = -52.5, below `HAL_ACTIVE_MIN_DB` -48) is refused. [D.15]
- **AC-AA-58 (manual)** GIVEN a panel told nothing about the signature, WHEN shown a lying instrument and a true one, THEN at least 90 per cent identify the lying one from the full signature (a **near-field distinctness test**: each tester starts inside the instrument's reading range and inside `R_hal`, so it is not a remote-detection test; remote detection is covered by AC-AA-129); and with hue removed, and separately with audio removed, a two-alternative forced choice over at least 20 trials per condition is at least 15 of 20 (binomial p about 0.02 against 50 per cent); the capture and trial logs are retained. [AA-35, HR-5, AC-SE-42 analogue]
- **AC-AA-59 (manual)** GIVEN a lying gauge reading healthy over a stalling pump, WHEN the tester walks to the pump, THEN the pump's true running or witness sound is audible throughout the lie; the capture is retained (the automated falsifier is AC-AA-109). [AA-37 e, P4 falsifier, E-AA-49]
- **AC-AA-60 (auto, provisional PF-20)** GIVEN a mock Filter that emits no audio requests, WHEN class-(b) lies run, THEN no reading-lie sound is produced and a CONFLICT diagnostic is logged (HR-5's sound mode unmet), and no other family changes. [AA-36, E-AA-51, CX-A3; creative-director disposition: a refusal reopens PF-20, not AA-35]
- **AC-AA-61 (auto, provisional PF-20)** GIVEN more than `LIES_MAX` lies (1, 2 and 4 at sanity stages 1, 2 and 3; the extra lie is the (`LIES_MAX` + 1)th), WHEN requests arrive, THEN the extras are dropped with a DIAGNOSTIC and no PROTECTED voice is stolen. [AA-12, E-AA-48]
- **AC-AA-62 (auto, VS-B dormant, provisional PF-20)** GIVEN a peer seat with `tell_on` (a fixture; the cue triggers on `tell_on`, not on `harm_eligible`, so it leads eligibility), WHEN requests are produced for the local seat, THEN one P1 cue request exists at the peer's suit position with `listener_scope` ALL_EXCEPT_SOURCE and ordinary falloff, it is absent for the source seat, absent in solo play, and identical for listener stages 0 to 3. [AA-39, SN-14, E-AA-65]
- **AC-AA-63 (lint)** GIVEN the bus tree, WHEN linted, THEN a `Voice` bus exists with no DSP, no sanity, stage or Filter input and no duck by a `hal` cue. [AA-39]

### F. Mix, voices and ducking

- **AC-AA-64 (auto)** GIVEN pool 32 with 31 tiers 1 to 3 voices busy, WHEN a tier-4 request, a second tier-4 request and a tier-1 `CLANK` arrive, THEN the first takes the free voice, the second is dropped (no strictly lower victim) and the `CLANK` steals the quietest, oldest tier-4 voice; GIVEN pool 48 the same scene has 17 free voices. [AA-12, D.10]
- **AC-AA-65 (auto)** GIVEN a full pool, WHEN a tier-1 request finds only tier-1 victims, THEN it steals the quietest non-PROTECTED tier-1 voice quieter than itself and `voice_tier1_steal` increments; the same counter is non-zero in a CI soak only on a bug. [AA-12, D.10]
- **AC-AA-66 (lint)** GIVEN the data file, WHEN loaded, THEN `VOICE_POOL_SIZE >= RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP` (a pool of 12 is refused), `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES >= max(1, ceil(BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS))` (2 at a 60-tick tail and a 30-tick gap), and a warning is raised if the pool is below `D123` (31 at the defaults). [D.10, checkpoint Q3]
- **AC-AA-67 (auto)** GIVEN the tell bed, the tier stems, the reserved impulse voices, the positional hull-stress layer, the room tone and in-budget `hal` voices, WHEN the pool is exhausted, THEN none is stolen or ducked, and the seam-cut TOOL loop is neither ducked nor stealable by a tier >= 4 request. [AA-11, AA-12, D.18]
- **AC-AA-68 (auto)** GIVEN ambience, a TOOL loop, the seam-cut TOOL loop and the pod leg bed under a beat, WHEN D.18 runs, THEN the first three, excluding the seam-cut loop, duck by `DUCK_DB` with attack 6, hold 24 and release 30 ticks, overlapping impulses never sum, and the seam-cut loop, tier-1 to 3 cues, Horror cues and `hal` voices are not ducked; GIVEN a late tell with beat spacing 45 (under the 60-tick duck length), THEN the duck is continuous and its depth never deepens, and the D.18 envelope value at tell tick 150 matches the worked example. [AA-11, D.18]
- **AC-AA-69 (lint)** GIVEN the bus graph, WHEN linted, THEN no compressor, limiter or sidechain targets a tier-1 bus. [AA-11, L-8, M3]
- **AC-AA-70 (auto)** GIVEN any bus muted or the headless dummy driver, WHEN the simulation runs 3600 ticks, THEN `state_hash` is unchanged and every cue request is still produced. [AA-3, AUD-9, AC-AT-09]

### G. Restore, pause, death and settings

- **AC-AA-71 (auto)** GIVEN restores into TELL, BREACH, CALM after ABORTED, a live lie and a result 10 ticks old (a fixture whose producer does not clear; Assembly's `post_restore` clears it), WHEN the first post-restore poll runs, THEN no ONESHOT request is emitted, the bed fades in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` (60; `g` = 0.383, 0.707, 0.924, 1.0 at k = 15, 30, 45, 60), a `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` of 0 gives `g` 1 immediately, the lie's leak resumes at full leak level on the first frame, and `last_seen_T` is primed. [AA-5, D.4, D.12, E-AA-38 to E-AA-43]
- **AC-AA-72 (auto)** GIVEN a restore that returns an earlier epoch than `e_seen`, WHEN the next real onset (a higher epoch) is published, THEN the onset is requested (never a false calm). [AA-4, D.1, E-AA-40]
- **AC-AA-73 (auto)** GIVEN a pause during a REACH lead-in, a release and a restore fade, WHEN `T` is held, THEN each holds its position (`k`, `t_r` and `seek` are functions of sim `T`), UI requests continue, and the sim is unaffected. [AA-5, E-AA-44]
- **AC-AA-74 (auto)** GIVEN a death stub restore and a save, WHEN processed, THEN no request is made for either and no content branches on `restore_reason`. [AA-34, `SL:23`, E-AA-45]
- **AC-AA-75 (review)** GIVEN the settings boundary rules, WHEN the design is reviewed against the data lint of AC-AA-104, THEN the World slider covers `wit`, `ann`, `run`, `attn`, `hor`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `p1c` and `hal` as one group, the Ambience slider covers `amb` only, no setting lowers the bed below its margin, mono keeps the bed, reduce-flashing and reduce-motion never reduce audio, and no setting displays or sonifies the Attention level. [AA-43]
- **AC-AA-76 (review)** GIVEN the captions policy, WHEN reviewed against the data lint of AC-AA-104, THEN `wit`, `ann`, `run`, Suit body, foley, the three terminal interaction sounds and UI are captionable with precise source-naming, and the tell, breach, alarm cadence, pre-tell creaks, Horror and `hal` are never captioned, so no caption is a lie oracle. [AA-41, R-6]

### H. Identity table and data
